### DQ-18 — `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.01-fan-graph-owned-audience/20.01.04-fan-list-import-hygiene.md`

**The sendability of the `artist-asserted` provenance tier is specified two contradictory ways across sibling files, and the parent CX explicitly flags the question as unresolved with no owner.**

**Resolution**: Resolved. Three sources — one of them later-dated and higher in the tree than either contradicting file — already fix the answer: an artist-asserted record whose provenance question was ANSWERED is sendable under a warm-up throttle; the unmarketable tier is reserved for the UNANSWERED-provenance case. The finding elevates a single parenthetical gloss in one edge-case cell into a competing rule, and treats a superseded parent-CX marker as live. (1) The later, parent-level synthesis supersedes the sub-domain CX marker. fanbase-direct-to-fan-cx.md is [DEEP], last updated 2026-07-18 (both contradicting files are 2026-07-17) and states in CX-02 (Fan Graph <-> Broadcast, the exact edge the pending marker sits on): "Provenance strength and consent staleness (>=24 months) additionally drive send throttling and tiering in 20.03.04", and its trigger chain reads "Campaign -> segment resolve -> per-recipient consent + provenance + staleness resolve at dispatch -> throttle tier -> send. Fails closed: ledger unavailable => no send." Provenance resolves into a throttle tier, not a block; the only fail-closed condition named is ledger unavailability. That answers 20.01-...-cx.md CX-01 §3 ("sendable-with-warning or hard-blocked until re-permissioned") in favour of sendable-with-throttle, so the §3 [PENDING] is a stale marker, not missing policy. (2) The broadcast sub-domain, which owns the send path, is unanimous and unambiguous that imported unwitnessed lists send. 20.03.04 D-01..D-05 plus its edge case: "Artist blasts a freshly-imported unwitnessed list \| Warm-up throttle from import provenance; complaint rate watched hard; halt on breach." 20.03-broadcast-fan-messaging-cx.md CX-02: "A fresh sender, or one that just imported 4,000 unwitnessed addresses (20.01.04), is throttled to a warm-up ramp", with the artist told "your 4,000-person announcement will take 6 hours to go out, because this list is new". 20.03.03 D-06 ratifies that object-derived urgent sends "bypass the warm-up ramp" — a ramp that can be bypassed is a send path, not a block. 20.03.03 Cross-Cut Notes: "Reads import provenance from 20.01.04 — a fresh import forces a warm-up ramp." (3) 20.01.04 already distinguishes the two tiers cleanly and never conflates them. D-03 is scoped to the unanswered case: "Unanswered provenance imports data anyway, into an unmarketable tier". Edge Case row 1 is likewise scoped: "Artist cannot answer the provenance question \| Import completes and records land in an unmarketable tier". States row Partial: "Import succeeded, provenance unanswered \| Records exist, marked unmarketable". Happy Path 5 (provenance ANSWERED) puts those rows in the warm-up tier. Same for the gig-signup-sheet edge case: "Provenance = artist assertion + a date range they give ... Same flow; warm-up tier." So the unmarketable tier and the warm-up tier are two distinct, consistently-scoped states, and 20.01.04 D-03 is not in conflict with its own Happy Path. (4) The lone dissenting text — 20.01.02 line 88, "Import completes; records land in the artist-asserted (unmarketable-until-re-permissioned) tier" — is a parenthetical gloss in an edge-case cell, is contradicted twice inside its own file, and is incoherent with that file’s own ratified decision. Line 93 of the same file places an artist-asserted cohort in the "artist-asserted, deliverability-downgraded tier" (i.e. sendable). Line 129 (Cross-Cut Notes) states "provenance strength and consent staleness drive send throttling and tiering". The Decisions table contains no decision making artist-asserted unmarketable; D-08 says only that "provenance strength is server-assigned ... owner-supplied events are coerced to artist-asserted". Decisively, line 88’s scenario (an owner claiming platform-witnessed strength to smuggle records into a strong tier) cannot route to a distinct penal tier at all under D-08 and the Behavior section, which say "any provenance an owner supplies is ignored, because provenance is not an owner-authorable fact at all" — the field is not read, so the platform has no detected-fraudulent-claim state to route on. The outcome that parenthetical describes would require machinery the same file abolishes. (5) Choosing the hard-block reading would also make the ONLY unblock path a mechanism whose lawfulness is itself an open question — 20.01.04 Q-01, "Is a re-permission campaign to imported records lawful? ... Needs counsel, not design" (User, /ideate-validate), echoed by 20.01-...-cx.md CX-01 §4 — and would kill what 20.01.04 DT-01 calls "the adoption gate for the entire domain". No source proposes that trade. Consequently there is no owner decision here. What remains is editorial reconciliation, which does not need a ratification: (a) 20.01.02 line 88 should read that the claimed strength is rejected and the records land in the artist-asserted, deliverability-downgraded (warm-up) tier, exactly as line 93 and line 129 already say; (b) 20.01-...-cx.md CX-01 §3 should drop its [PENDING] and record the settled rule — import permission does not confer send permission, answered-provenance artist-asserted records are sendable under a warm-up throttle governed by 20.03.03/20.03.04, and unanswered-provenance records are unmarketable until the provenance question is answered or the fan re-permissions. Genuinely open and correctly parked elsewhere are only the thresholds (20.01.04 Q-02, 20.03.03 Q-01 -> /create-prd-architecture) and re-permission lawfulness (20.01.04 Q-01 -> /ideate-validate).

## Entries

### DQ-01

> **File**: `01-identity-profiles-organizations/01.01-person-identity-roles/01.01.02-artist-names-aliases-projects.md` · **Domain**: `01` · **2 open / 2 locked**

**When someone creates an artist alias and does not yet hold the implied role facet, how does the platform tell them the facet is being added — and which facet is it?**

**Why it blocks**: Two ratified [DEEP] files specify contradictory behaviour for the same step of the same flow. 01.01.01 D-12 and its Facet lifecycle (line 84) say implied facets are auto-added with a visible undoable notice and "never prompted", naming alias creation as the worked instance; 01.01.02 D-10, Happy Path step 4 (line 54) and the Fan Role Lens (line 26) say the facet is added "inline, one confirm" and "never silently". An implementer writing the alias-creation flow cannot choose a step ordering, cannot decide whether a decline branch and a corresponding state row exist, and cannot write the facet-write contract without picking one file over the other. Separately, the sub-domain CX file still carries CX-04 §2 as "[PENDING — Step 5]" (01.01-person-identity-roles-cx.md line 85), so the marker cannot be closed either. 01.01.01's own Cross-Cut Note (line 169) asked Step 6 to carry the resolution back into 01.01.02; it demonstrably never happened, and no later decision, index D-entry or reconciliation note in either child resolves it.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| C — Reversibility of the implied facet (whether the add can be undone, and what may block the undo) | 01.01.01-person-record-role-facets.md D-06 (line 195): "**Live obligations block facet removal; history never does** \| DT-06. Blocking on history means the users with the longest careers can never remove a facet... The obligation list is closed and per-facet, and is re-evaluated atomically at confirm time". The per-facet table (line 94) gives `performer` exactly one blocker: "Any confirmed booking is future-dated". Facet lifecycle line 83: "**Removing does not delete.** Nothing done under a facet is touched (D-03...)". |
| D — Whether the implied facet authorises anything, or a permission gate is built at this seam | 01.01.01-person-record-role-facets.md D-07 (line 196): "**Facet, visibility and permission are three independent axes**", and Behavior line 52: "A facet **reveals** surfaces and makes the person discoverable in that capacity. It never authorises anything." Reinforced at the sub-domain level by 01.01-person-identity-roles-cx.md rejected pair R-02 (line 104): "An alias does not grant or require a facet-as-permission. CX-04 records the *implication* (holding an alias implies a professional self); it is not a permission dependency. Kept distinct so downstream spec writers do not build a gate here." Note R-02 rejects a *permission* gate; it says nothing about a consent step, which is Axis A. |

#### DQ-01.1 — A — Disclosure shape: how the user learns the implied facet is being written at alias creation  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**At alias creation, is the implied facet written with a notice AFTER the write (01.01.01 D-12), with a disclosure BEFORE the write inside the same single Create action, or behind a blocking consent step that can be declined (01.01.02 D-10)?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — Auto-add, notice after the write (01.01.01 D-12 governs as written; 01.01.02 D-10 is rewritten)** | Alias creation writes the implied facet unconditionally inside the same all-or-nothing transaction that mints the alias and its handle (01.01.02 Happy Path step 6, E-15). No consent step, no gate, no branch where the alias exists without the facet. After creation the user sees a visible, undoable notice naming what changed — the shape 01.01.01 line 84 specifies ("auto-added with a visible, undoable notice") and which line 123 renders for a neighbouring case: "Sam Reeves credited you as a performer on 3 tracks. We've added Performing to your account — remove it if that's not you." The exact affordance of the notice (persistent banner, toast, inline card, dismissibility) is NOT specified by any source and is left to design; nothing in this option turns on it. | Adds no branch that has no defined behaviour. There is no decline path to specify, so 01.01.02 Happy Path step 6 and E-15's all-or-nothing creation contract are preserved untouched.; Reuses a pattern already written down rather than inventing one: 01.01.01 line 123 (shadow-party facets inherited on claim) and domain CX-13 (identity-profiles-organizations-cx.md line 250: other-asserted facets are "INHERITED by the claimant and are removable without a reason") are the same auto-add-plus-undo shape.; The undo is unblockable in this exact case. 01.01.01's live-obligation table (line 94) gives `performer` exactly one blocker — "Any confirmed booking is future-dated" — which a person who does not yet hold the facet cannot have. So the notice's offer of removal is always honourable at the moment it is made.; 01.01.02's own Role Lens (line 26) states the facet is implied by the act itself: "If a Fan creates an alias they have, by that act, asserted a professional facet (01.01.01) and are Musician-shaped from that point — there is no fan-with-an-alias state." A consent step asks the user to re-assert what the act already asserted. | The user's discoverability changes before they are told. 01.01.01 Behavior (line 52): "A facet **reveals** surfaces and makes the person discoverable in that capacity." `performer` reveals 04 Opportunities (as available player), 08 Jamming and 17 Live Booking (as act) per the facet table (line 64). The notice mitigates after the fact; it does not precede the exposure.; It leaves 01.01.02 D-10's stated objection ("a silent add changes the user's navigation without consent") only partly answered — navigation density changes at the moment of the write, and 01.01.01 DT-09 identifies navigation density as the multi-hyphenate's real pain. The remedy exists (per-facet hide, D-07 visibility axis) but is a second act the user must find.; Option D achieves everything this option achieves and also answers that objection, at a cost of one sentence of copy. This option is strictly dominated by D unless the owner positively wants no pre-write copy on the creation screen. | no |

Nothing you already have changes."), and the single Create press performs both. Abandoning the form is ordinary flow abandonment; no alias and no facet are written, which is already the behaviour E-14/E-15 (lines 78-79) and Happy Path step 6 commit to. The post-write undoable notice of D-12 still applies. | Satisfies both ratified decisions on their own terms without narrowing either: it is not a prompt (01.01.01 line 84's prohibition is on prompting), and it is not silent (01.01.02 D-10's objection is to silence). Neither file's rule has to be contradicted, only its worked example adjusted.; Answers Option A's first two cons directly: nothing about discoverability or navigation changes before the user has been told it will.; Requires no machinery any source lacks. No decline branch, no refusal state, no conditional. It is Option A plus one sentence.; The disclosure sentence already exists in ratified prose. 01.01.01 Happy Path step 3 specifies exactly this copy for the `seller` case — "This adds Selling to your account. Nothing you already have changes." This option reuses that sentence without reusing step 3's blocking confirm, which is the part 01.01.01 reserves for explicit, user-initiated adds (line 80) rather than implied ones (line 84).; Consistent with how the tree already distinguishes reversible from irreversible disclosures. E-18 (line 82) requires a *blocking* pre-disclosure because "after delivery the inference is permanent and un-retractable". A facet add is reversible by D-06 and unblockable here (line 94), so the same reasoning yields disclosure without a block. | It edits both files rather than one: 01.01.01's line 84 example and D-12's parenthetical gain a pre-write disclosure that D-12's text does not currently mention, and 01.01.02 D-10 loses its "one confirm" framing and its miscited precedent.; A user who reads nothing on the creation screen is in exactly Option A's position. The disclosure improves the informed case and does nothing for the skimmer — its value is real but bounded, and no source in the tree offers evidence about how often the line is read.; It is a slightly softer answer to D-10 than a true gate: the user consents to the alias and the facet in one press and cannot accept one without the other. | no |

D-01 is engaged here (a facet question IS a role question), though see the note under the recommendation — D-01's text bans a primary role, an account type and an onboarding fork, not questions in this flow generally. | **yes** |

D-01 ("No primary role, no account type, no onboarding fork") is squarely against reifying the distinction into a flow branch.; It still needs Option B's undefined decline branch, and needs it in the population least equipped to interpret it.; No source defines a conditional facet-add UX; the condition, its copy and its state row would all be invented.; Widest edit surface of the four — both children gain a conditional, plus the decline branch, plus a statement of when zero-facet counts as a distinct account shape, which reaches 01.01.01's Role Lens, Happy Path step 8 and Empty state. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: D — Pre-write disclosure inside the single Create action — D is the only option that leaves both ratified rules intact. 01.01.01 line 84 forbids a prompt; D has no prompt. 01.01.02 D-10 objects to a silent add; D is not silent. Every other option requires overturning one file's rule outright (A overturns D-10, B narrows D-12) or inventing a branch no source defines (B and C both need a decline path, and 01.01.02 Happy Path step 6 with E-14/E-15 commit alias creation to all-or-nothing, so a refusal state has nowhere to sit). D's incremental cost over A is one sentence of copy — the same sentence 01.01.01 Happy Path step 3 already specifies for `seller`. Two supporting notes, stated at their real strength rather than harder: (1) D-10's cited precedent is genuinely miscited — step 3's `seller` add is user-initiated ("They hit 'Sell something'", line 102), and 01.01.01 separates explicit adds (line 80) from implied adds (line 84), placing alias creation on the implied side by name; that removes D-10's authority for a blocking gate but does not by itself establish that nothing may be disclosed beforehand, which is why D rather than A. (2) The `personas.md` line D-12 leans on — "The design must make the lazy path the correct path" (line 70) — is real, but its source context is the Producer anti-persona failing to capture splits, not facet UX; D-12 generalises it. The friction argument stands on 01.01.01 DT-03 and Role Lens line 21 without needing that generalisation. If the owner prefers to change only one file, Option A is the fallback and is defensible; if the owner wants a separable yes, Option B is available only at the price of commissioning a decline branch and reconciling it with the all-or-nothing contract. *(precedent: 01.01.01 Happy Path step 3 supplies the disclosure sentence ("This adds Selling to your account. Nothing you already have changes.") without supplying the blocking confirm, which line 80 reserves for explicit adds. 01.01.02 E-18 (line 82) supplies the distinction that keeps the disclosure non-blocking: it blocks before publication precisely because "after delivery the inference is permanent and un-retractable", whereas a facet add is reversible under D-06 and unblockable here (line 94). On ownership, the author-where-owned rule points at 01.01.01 in this direction: 01.01.01's own axes table assigns "Facet (claim) — Does this person do X?" to "this file", and 01.01.02's Cross-Cut Note (line 137) calls its relationship to 01.01.01 a "trigger dependency", i.e. 01.01.02 triggers something 01.01.01 owns. So the rule for HOW a facet is written belongs in 01.01.01 and 01.01.02 should reference it — the reverse claim (that 01.01.02 owns the rule because it owns the flow) inverts the rule and should not be used to support Option B.)*

#### DQ-01.2 — B — Facet identity: which facet alias creation implies  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does alias creation always imply `performer`, or does the implied facet depend on what the alias is for?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **B1 — Always `performer`, explicitly ratified** | Alias creation implies exactly `performer`, in every case, as both children already assume. The user may remove it immediately under the ordinary facet lifecycle. The parent CX map row's "performer/writer" phrasing is corrected to `performer` so the three files agree. | It is what both ratified children already say. 01.01.01 line 84: "Creating an alias (01.01.02) implies `performer`." 01.01.02 Happy Path step 4: "System detects they hold no `performer` facet". Ratifying it changes no behaviour, only removes an inherited assumption from the shadows.; `performer` is the facet that reveals "12 Release (as display artist)" per 01.01.01's facet table (line 64), which is the primary thing an alias exists to be.; The cost of being wrong is bounded and already specified: facets are self-asserted (D-02), removal is blocked only by live obligations (D-06), and a fresh `performer` has none (line 94). A user who did not want it can drop it immediately. | It over-asserts for a production-only alias. 01.01.02 Role Lens Producer (line 24) says a Producer "Holds a producer alias distinct from any artist alias — the industry norm (production credits under one name, artist releases under another)". Under B1 that person is written a `performer` claim, which per 01.01.01 line 52 makes them "discoverable in that capacity" in 04 Opportunities, 08 Jamming and 17 Live Booking. No file addresses this case.; It contradicts the parent CX map row, which says alias creation "implies a performer/writer facet" (01.01-person-identity-roles-cx.md line 13). That row is Medium confidence and its own detail section (line 85) says only `performer`, so the correction is defensible — but it is a correction, not a no-op. | no |
| **B2 — Infer the facet from what the creation flow already knows about the alias's purpose** | The implied facet depends on the alias's intended use — `performer` for a display-artist alias, `producer` or `writer` for one minted for production or writing credits. This requires the creation flow to carry a purpose signal, which no file defines; it would either be a new question in the flow or a new inference from the entry point that started it. | Matches the Producer-alias reality 01.01.02 Role Lens line 24 describes.; Inference-from-context is a pattern the tree already uses once: 01.01.01 line 123 infers a shadow party's facet "from the referencing flow (credited on horn → `performer`)". | It commissions machinery no source defines. The shadow-party precedent works because the referencing flow carries an instrument; alias creation carries no equivalent signal, so the purpose must either be asked (a new question, and a role question at that, which is what Axis A is trying to avoid) or derived from the entry point, which 01.01.02 Happy Path step 1 leaves deliberately plural ("from their settings, or from any release/credit flow").; It interacts with Axis A: a purpose question re-introduces exactly the role prompt 01.01.01 line 84 forbids, so choosing B2 partially re-opens Axis A.; 01.01.01 D-05's generation rule makes each facet a distinct surface set, so a wrong inference reveals the wrong markets — a worse failure than B1's over-assertion, which at least reveals a superset the user can trim. | **yes** |
| **B3 — No facet implied at creation; the facet is added at the first act performed under the alias** | Alias creation writes no facet. The facet is added when the alias is first used in a way that needs one — first release as display artist adds `performer`, first owned project adds `producer` — reusing 01.01.01's gate-at-the-act pattern from the minor edge case (line 129: "The gate is at the **act**: the first listing, the first lesson booking"). [CORRECTED: this option commissions new machinery — no source defines a per-act facet-trigger mapping, and 01.01.02 CX-04 states "There is no state where someone holds an alias but no professional facet".] | No facet is ever written that the user's behaviour has not demonstrated.; The gate-at-the-act pattern is already ratified prose in 01.01.01 (line 129), so the shape is not invented even though this application of it is. | It contradicts a statement both the child and the parent CX make. 01.01.02 Role Lens line 26: "there is no fan-with-an-alias state." 01.01-person-identity-roles-cx.md CX-04 (line 81): "There is no state where someone holds an alias but no professional facet — creating one crosses the Fan/Musician boundary by definition." Choosing B3 means rewriting that relationship statement, not just the children.; It creates the facet-less-alias-holder state that Axis A's Option B was rejected for creating, arriving at it by a different door.; 01.01.01 line 129's gate-at-the-act pattern gates an ACT while leaving the CLAIM free ("claims are free"); B3 inverts it, withholding the claim until the act. The precedent points the other way. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: B1 — Always `performer`, explicitly ratified, with the production-alias case recorded as a residual open question — B1 is the status quo of both ratified children and the only option that needs no new signal and contradicts no ratified statement. B2's inference needs a purpose signal that alias creation does not carry and that could only be obtained by asking — which collides with Axis A. B3 requires rewriting CX-04's relationship statement and inverts the one precedent it leans on. B1's real cost — a production-only alias getting a `performer` claim and the discoverability that comes with it (01.01.01 line 52, line 64) — is bounded by D-02 (facets are self-asserted, not credentials) and D-06 (removal blocked only by live obligations, and `performer` has none for a new holder, line 94), so the user can drop it in one act. This axis is surfaced separately rather than folded into Axis A because ratifying Axis A alone would close CX-04's PENDING marker while silently inheriting the `performer` narrowing from D-12 and D-10, neither of which argues for it against the parent CX row's "performer/writer" phrasing. Recommend recording the production-only-alias case as a named residual question against 01.01.02 rather than resolving it by inference now.

**Affected files**: `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/01-identity-profiles-organizations/01.01-person-identity-roles/01.01.01-person-record-role-facets.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/01-identity-profiles-organizations/01.01-person-identity-roles/01.01.02-artist-names-aliases-projects.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/01-identity-profiles-organizations/01.01-person-identity-roles/01.01-person-identity-roles-cx.md`


### DQ-02

> **File**: `01-identity-profiles-organizations/identity-profiles-organizations-index.md` · **Domain**: `01` · **5 open / 2 locked**

**When a band configures nothing, what authority does a confirmed member other than the creator hold — and is that authority bounded, capacity-scoped, and shared with non-band org types?**

**Why it blocks**: 01.03.03 Q-01 (mirrored as domain index Q-03) is the seed value for the mandate resolver. Until it is answered an implementer cannot: (a) decide what a membership edge carries at creation, so the edge schema's mandate attribute has no default and the resolver in the Roles & Permissions cross-cut has no answer to return for the case CX-01 calls dominant ('most bands will never open the mandate surface'); (b) write 01.03.03's Empty and Partial states, both of which currently read 'Default authority applies (Q-01)'; (c) size 01.03.01 D-14's succession machinery, because 01.03.01 Q-04 says in terms 'Answer 01.03.03 Q-01 first' and whether the unadministrable path is dead code or day-one infrastructure flips on the answer; (d) write 01.04.01's default-disclosure copy, which must state the platform default alongside the legal one; or (e) resolve 01.04 index Q-02 (is governance ever required), which is explicitly conditioned on 'a band that never configures it runs on 01.03.03's default mandate'.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| 1. Does the creator of a band they are in hold a full (owning) mandate? | 01.02-organizations-entity-model/01.02.02-organization-creation-lifecycle.md, D-07 and the creation-mode table. Table row: '\| **For myself** \| "I'm in this band" / "I run this studio" \| Membership edge (01.03.01) + **owning mandate** (01.03.03) \| `owned` \|'. D-07: 'Creating an org you are not in is not an exception — it is one of three creation modes, and modes 2/3 are shadow-org creation (01.05.01) with a **custodial** mandate, not ownership'. Reinforced by identity-profiles-organizations-cx.md CX-15: 'The creator becomes the first owning mandate (01.02.02)', and by 01.02.02 D-12: 'The first owning mandate always lands on a human, never on the acting-context org'. |
| 2. Does any seed attach to an unconfirmed (asserted or disputed) membership edge? | 01.03-membership-representation-mandate/01.03.01-membership-records-lifecycle.md, Behavior rule 2: 'an unconfirmed edge is a **claim about the past**, never a **grant of power**. It carries zero mandate, yields no acting context, and is invisible on the named party's own profile until they confirm it (D-08, DT-05).' D-02: 'Membership requires **consent to authority**; consent may be pending. Authority never precedes it'. D-08: 'An asserted membership is **invisible on the named party's profile** until they confirm it'. |

#### DQ-02.1 — 3. Reconcile the coarse activity enum — seven activities or four?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is the coarse activity enum the seven items named in 01.03.03 D-01 (book / sign / spend / list / release / settle / administer), or the 'four coarse activities' that DT-02 in the same file argues for?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **3a — Ratify seven (D-01 as written); correct DT-02's prose** | The enum is book / sign / spend / list / release / settle / administer. DT-02's closing line ('Plain-language statements over four coarse activities will get used; a matrix will not') is edited to say 'a handful of coarse activities', since DT-02's argument is against a granular matrix, not for a specific count. | D-01 is the ratified Decision; DT-02 is a rejection annotation whose argument (a permission matrix is decorative) is untouched by the count.; 01.03.03 Behavior states the same seven-item list independently of D-01, so two of the three mentions already agree.; Leaves `release` and `settle` addressable, which axis 5's split option needs. | Seven items is closer to the matrix DT-02 rejected than four is; the plain-language surface D-02 requires has seven statements to render per member. | no |
| **3b — Reduce to four; re-derive which four** | Cut the enum to four coarse activities, folding the remainder into them (e.g. list+release into one publishing activity, settle into spend). | Matches DT-02's stated shape and produces the shortest plain-language authority statement (D-02). | No source says WHICH four. DT-02's own illustrative triple is 'can book, can spend, can sign' — three, not four — so the reduction has to be authored from scratch.; Folding `settle` into `spend` collides with 01.04.03, which treats receive/spend/distribute as distinct authorities with money attached.; Contradicts a ratified decision (D-01) to match a non-ratified annotation. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: 3a — Ratify seven — D-01 is the ratified item and Behavior independently repeats the seven. DT-02's count is incidental to its argument. This is a spec-hygiene reconciliation, not a product choice, but it must be closed first because axes 4 and 5 are stated over the enum. *(precedent: The Decisions table is the ratified surface of a feature file; Deep Think annotations record reasoning. Same relationship the tree uses throughout (e.g. 01.02.02 DT-11 → D-07).)*

#### DQ-02.2 — 4. What activity set is seeded on a non-creator confirmed band membership edge?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**On a `band`-type org with no explicit mandates, does a confirmed member other than the creator hold the full coarse activity set, none of it, or a named subset?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **4a — Peer seed: the full coarse activity set** | Every seeded confirmed band membership edge carries all seven activities (subject to axes 5 and 6). The creator's owning mandate (locked, axis 1) is the first instance of the same set rather than a superior grade. 01.03.03's Empty state says in plain language that everyone can currently do everything. | It is the only option that avoids what 01.03.03 DT-03 names as the cost of the alternative: 'makes whoever typed the name first the owner of the band — a structural injustice the platform would be inventing'.; It makes the lazy path the correct path — personas.md's Producer worst-accidental entry states 'The design must make the lazy path the correct path' — because a band that configures nothing behaves the way its members already assume it behaves.; It removes the failure 01.03.01 costed explicitly: 'Under "all members, all authority" a multi-member band can never have a sole holder, so death never orphans it and the whole succession path is dead code for bands. Under "creator only", **every band is one death away from unadministrable**.' 01.03.03 D-04's always-one-full-holder invariant is then satisfied by construction and 01.03.01 Q-04 (succession selection) becomes rare-path.; It is consistent in direction with the legal default 01.04.01 commits the platform to DISCLOSING: 'in the absence of an agreement, the law likely treats them as an equal-shares general partnership, with every member able to bind the others.' Note the source's own hedges ('likely', 'in many jurisdictions') — the platform records and does not advise (01.04 index D-02), so this is a directional match, not an equivalence. | It maximally enables the behaviour personas.md names for the Producer: 'assigns themselves a larger split than agreed and pushes it through while contributors aren't paying attention.' Detection is after the fact (01.04.03 D-01 transparency + the Audit Trail cross-cut), never prevention.; 01.03.03 D-06 ('Over-limit actions escalate to a capable holder rather than dead-ending') is inert by construction: with no ceiling, nothing is ever over limit, so the escalation UX only begins working once a band configures ceilings. Its Happy Path steps 3-5 and its first Edge Case row are unreachable in the default state.; Seeding `administer` means any confirmed member can alter another member's mandate or invite/remove. That is wider than the partnership default 4a's fourth pro invokes, so the disclosure screen and the mandate screen do not say quite the same thing. Whether other activities are also wider than that default is a question of partnership law the sources do not settle and this entry does not assert.; Bands that begin life as a shared alias (CX-12: 'Skipping leaves the default mandate (Q-03) — so the design must make the skip legible, not silent') inherit peer authority for everyone named at capture, with no deliberate 'this person can sign for us' moment. | no |
| **4b — Custodial seed: nothing; authority is granted, never inherited** | Only the creator's owning mandate (axis 1) carries activities. Every other confirmed edge is presence without authority — the state sub-domain CX-01 already describes as normal ('Membership without mandate is presence without authority — which is the normal state for most band members'). Attempts route through 01.03.03 D-06 to the owning holder. | Fail-safe in the same direction as the ratified posture: 01.03.03 D-05 ('Resolution fails closed') and 01.03.01 D-02 ('Authority never precedes it').; D-06's escalation route is live from day one, and D-06 exists precisely so 'a refusal with no route' does not push the deal off-platform.; It contains personas.md's worst-intentional split-push at the point of authority rather than after the fact.; It matches the posture 01.03.03's Role Lens already describes for business orgs ('Configures mandates for studio staff', 'Configures venue staff mandates'), so one model would serve every org type. | 01.03.03 DT-03 states the cost directly: it 'makes whoever typed the name first the owner of the band — a structural injustice the platform would be inventing'. This is the on-point objection; the weaker objection that it reinstates 01.02.02 DT-02's land-grab does NOT apply, because D-07 ratifies the mode-1 creator's owning mandate.; 01.03.01 costed the operational failure: 'every band is one death away from unadministrable'. D-14's succession offer plus a 14-day contest window becomes load-bearing for ordinary bands, and 01.03.01 Q-04 (selection rule) escalates from a validate-stage question into a second forced product decision.; It makes 01.04.03's first Edge Case the default experience for any band whose creator is not the person who handles the money — the file calls that 'a common and frustrating state' that 'must not be a dead end', and D-05's context says 'The dead-end version is how bands go back to cash'.; It inverts personas.md's lazy-path rule for the case CX-01 calls dominant, and CX-12's common skip path then silently produces one-person control over a peer group. | no |
| **4c — Split seed: low-binding activities peer, high-binding activities reserved** | Every confirmed member is seeded `book` and `list`; `sign`, `spend`, `settle`, `administer` and `release` stay with the owning mandate holder. Attempts at the reserved set escalate via D-06 exactly as an over-ceiling action does. Note the correction to the earlier framing of this option: it is NOT a reversible/irreversible cut. `release` sits on the reserved side, because 01.02.02 D-10 treats catalogue and credits as perpetual entitlements that 'never block and always survive', so a release is not a reversible act. | Composes only ratified machinery — D-01's enum, D-06's escalation route, D-07's owning mandate.; Keeps the capture moment friction-free (any member can accept a gig or list) while the money-and-contract surface still needs a named holder.; D-06's escalation is live for exactly the acts where a refusal-with-a-route is the right answer. | It inherits 4b's DT-03 objection on the reserved half: the creator holds a privileged grade over sign/spend/settle/administer that nobody voted for.; 01.03.03 D-02 requires plain-language authority statements, and 'you can accept gigs but not sign for them, because your bandmate typed the name first' is the hardest of the three to state without the user asking who decided it.; No source in the tree proposes a split — 01.03.03 Q-01, DT-03, domain index Q-03, CX-12 and moscow-ledger.md line 224 all frame the decision as a binary — so the peer/reserved cut must be authored and ratified as new content in 01.03.03 rather than derived.; It reproduces 4b's mortality exposure on the reserved half: 01.03.01 D-14's unadministrable path and Q-04 stay live. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: 4a — Peer seed (paired with a default ceiling from axis 5) — The direction is carried by three items that survive re-reading. First, 01.03.03 DT-03 is the sub-domain's own verdict on the alternative — 'a structural injustice the platform would be inventing' — and it is the on-point objection to 4b/4c, not 01.02.02 DT-02, which D-07 rules out as a precedent here. Second, personas.md's Producer entry states the design rule directly: 'The design must make the lazy path the correct path', and CX-01 confirms the lazy path is the dominant path ('most bands will never open the mandate surface'). Third, 01.03.01's cross-cut note is the only place in the tree that costed the alternative operationally, and it costed it as 'every band is one death away from unadministrable'. The abuse objection is answered by machinery that is already ratified and free: 01.04.03 D-01 ('Authority and visibility are decoupled — every member sees every movement'), sub-domain CX-01 §4 ('silently *gaining* it is a security event'), and 01.03.03's own Edge Case that a member acting within mandate against the band's wishes is 'Not a platform failure — the mandate was granted. Governance (01.04.01) is the remedy, and the audit trail is the evidence'. 4a alone leaves D-06 inert, which is why this recommendation is conditional on choosing a default ceiling at axis 5 rather than 5a. *(precedent: personas.md (Producer worst-accidental: 'The design must make the lazy path the correct path') and 01.03.03 DT-03. It does NOT follow 01.02.02 D-01/DT-02 — that precedent points at parties the creator is not in, and D-07 grants the mode-1 creator an owning mandate outright. It also does not follow 01.03.03 D-05's fail-closed instinct: D-05 governs an unresolvable mandate (an error state, per the Error row of the States table), not an unconfigured one.)*

#### DQ-02.3 — 5. Does the seed carry a default value ceiling?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is seeded authority unbounded, or bounded by a default value ceiling above which the act escalates — and if bounded, to whom does it escalate?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **5a — No default ceiling** | Seeded activities carry no value bound. A band that wants ceilings configures them. | Simplest to state in plain language (01.03.03 D-02).; No number has to be authored, and no source supplies one. | Leaves 01.03.03 D-06 inert in the default state (see 4a con 2), so the escalation route the file was written around never fires for the population CX-01 calls dominant.; Leaves personas.md's worst-intentional case bounded only after the fact.; Discards one of the two scope dimensions 01.03.03 Behavior names as first-class: 'Scope dimensions, at breadth: **activity** ..., **value ceiling** (can accept a £300 gig, not a £30k tour)'. | no |
| **5b — Default ceiling; over-ceiling escalates to the owning-mandate holder** | Seeded activities carry a default value ceiling. An act above it is blocked at the call site and routed to a holder who can accept — under axis 1 that holder always exists, because the creator's owning mandate is uncapped. The owner must name the figure; no source supplies one, and it must be denominated in USD per meta/constraints.md's US-first revision (01.03.03's £300/£30k and 01.04.01's £2,000 are illustrative, pre-revision, and are one example band's agreed threshold rather than a default). | D-06 is live from day one, exactly as written: 'Over-limit actions escalate to a capable holder rather than dead-ending'. The escalation target is ratified (D-07's owning mandate), not invented.; Bounds personas.md's worst-intentional split-push at the point of authority for large amounts while leaving the ordinary capture moment friction-free.; Uses the second scope dimension 01.03.03 Behavior already defines, so it invents no mechanism.; 01.03.03's Happy Path (steps 3-5) and its first Edge Case row become reachable in the default state instead of only after configuration. | It reintroduces an asymmetry above the ceiling: the creator is the only uncapped holder, so 01.03.01's mortality path returns for the over-ceiling half — D-14's succession offer and Q-04's selection rule stay live rather than becoming dead code. Less severe than under 4b (the band is not paralysed; base-level authority survives), but it is a real cost of 5b over 5a.; The owner must author a figure that no source fixes, and the right figure differs enormously between a bar band and a touring act. | no |
| **5c — Default ceiling; over-ceiling requires co-approval by the other members** | Seeded activities carry a default ceiling, and an over-ceiling act escalates not to a superior holder but to collective approval by the other confirmed members — the shape 01.04.01's Happy Path step 3 records as what a real band actually agrees ('gigs any one can accept; anything over £2,000 needs three of four'). | No member holds a privileged grade at any value, so it takes none of the DT-03 objection.; Tracks 01.04.01's canonical example of what bands agree, which is also the example CX-06 uses.; Bounds the worst-intentional case at the point of authority without a superior holder. | It commissions machinery. 01.04.01's decision thresholds exist only once terms are recorded and confirmed by all members (D-01, D-02) — and by hypothesis this axis governs the band that recorded nothing. A collective approval gate with no recorded threshold requires a default threshold, a proposal/approval flow and a timeout, none of which any source defines.; It contradicts D-06 as written, which names the escalation target as 'a capable holder'. Choosing 5c amends D-06.; It pre-empts 01.04 index Q-02 ('Is governance ever required, or always optional?') by making a governance-shaped gate mandatory in the un-configured state.; The £2,000 in 01.04.01 step 3 is one band's agreed figure in a Happy Path narrative, not a default the platform could adopt — so 5c needs an authored number too, on top of the flow. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: 5b — Default ceiling, escalating to the owning-mandate holder — It repairs 4a's one structural defect (D-06 inert by construction) using only ratified parts: the value-ceiling dimension is already first-class in 01.03.03 Behavior, and the escalation target is already guaranteed to exist by 01.02.02 D-07 plus 01.03.03 D-04 ('Every org must retain at least one full mandate holder'). 5c is the more elegant answer on paper and should be revisited once 01.04.01 governance capture ships, but in the un-configured state it has no threshold to read and therefore commissions a default threshold, an approval flow and a timeout that no source defines — and it amends D-06's stated target. The honest cost of 5b, which the owner should weigh, is that 01.03.01 D-14's succession path stays live for the over-ceiling half instead of becoming dead code. *(precedent: 01.03.03 Behavior (value ceiling as a named scope dimension) and D-06 (escalate to a capable holder). No precedent supports a default figure — that is authored content the owner must supply.)*

#### DQ-02.4 — 6. Which membership capacities does the seed attach to?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the seed attach to every confirmed member regardless of capacity, or only to `permanent` capacity, with `touring` / `staff` / `honorary` carrying presence until explicitly granted?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **6a — Seed attaches to every confirmed member, any capacity** | All four capacities in 01.03.01 D-03 (permanent / touring / staff / honorary) receive the same seed. | Cleanest against the sub-domain's one ratified statement on this relationship — 01.03-membership-representation-mandate-cx.md R-02: 'Considered deriving mandate from capacity ("drummers can't sign"). Rejected: capacity describes what you *do musically*; mandate describes what you may do *commercially*. The band's drummer is frequently the one who handles the money. Deriving one from the other encodes a false stereotype into the authority model.'; One rule, statable in one sentence (01.03.03 D-02). | Seeds settle/sign/spend authority to an `honorary` member — an honorific — and to `staff`, which 01.03.01 D-03 explicitly models as a different kind of edge ('The same human is a permanent member of one band and staff at a studio, simultaneously').; The peer-default argument at axis 4 rests on symmetry among people who ARE the band; it does not obviously extend to a hired touring player or an honorary name. | no |
| **6b — Seed attaches to `permanent` capacity only** | `permanent` confirmed edges receive the seed. `touring`, `staff` and `honorary` receive presence with zero authority until explicitly granted. This is written as a rebuttable DEFAULT keyed to capacity, not as a derivation of mandate from capacity — a band can grant a touring member the full set, and the surface says so. | 01.03.01's touring row supports the direction, though narrowly: 'Their credits are real; their governance standing (01.04.01) is not the same. This is exactly why capacity is on the edge.' Note the scope — the row speaks to governance standing, and cites 01.04.01, not mandate.; Keeps the seed aligned with the group the axis-4 argument actually covers.; Bounds the blast radius of 4a: a touring hire does not inherit settle authority over the band's money on day one. | It sits in tension with R-02, which rejects deriving mandate from capacity. The tension is arguably narrow — R-02's example is the musical-role sense of capacity ('drummers can't sign'), while D-03's enum is a tenure-type sense — but the two uses of the word are not disambiguated anywhere in the tree, and 01.03.01's own Happy Path step 5 uses 'capacity' in the musical sense ('each choosing their capacity (drums; drums + backing vocals)'). Choosing 6b therefore REQUIRES amending R-02 with an explicit scope line saying it bars derivation, not a rebuttable default keyed to tenure type — otherwise the sub-domain contains a direct contradiction.; Adds a second sentence to every plain-language default statement. | no |

**Recommendation**: 6b — `permanent` only, conditional on amending R-02 — The justification for a peer seed at axis 4 is symmetry among the people who constitute the band; it does not reach a touring hire or an honorary name, and 01.03.01's touring row confirms at least that their standing is treated differently (for governance). But the earlier draft of this entry claimed this qualification was 'inherited from ratified content rather than invented' — that claim was wrong. The touring row speaks only to governance standing, and the on-point ratified item, R-02, cuts the other way and was not cited at all. So 6b is a genuine new ratification, and it is only coherent if R-02 is amended in the same pass to distinguish derivation (barred) from a rebuttable default keyed to tenure type (permitted). If the owner does not want to touch R-02, 6a is the consistent choice and its cost is bounded by axis 5's ceiling. *(precedent: None cleanly. 01.03.01's touring row is directional evidence about governance standing only; R-02 is contrary ratified content that must be reconciled rather than cited in support.)*

#### DQ-02.5 — 7. Do non-band org types inherit the same seed?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the seed apply to `band`-type orgs only — with studio / venue / label / shop / agency defaulting to the owning-mandate holder plus explicit grants — or to every org type uniformly?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **7a — Band only; other org types default to explicit grant** | A confirmed membership edge on a non-band org carries no seeded authority. The creator's owning mandate (axis 1, locked, and note D-07's mode-1 example is 'I run this studio') plus explicit grants are the whole model there. | 01.04 index D-01 supports treating the band as the special case: 'Governance is modelled for the **band** specifically, not for orgs generally ... A limited company has directors and articles; a studio has an owner. Neither needs modelling ... A band is the case where the legal default (general partnership) is invisible to its own members'. The peer-seed argument at axis 4 is built on exactly that legal default, so it does not transfer to a studio.; Matches how 01.03.03's Role Lens describes business orgs in practice — Producer 'Configures mandates for studio staff (an assistant can book the room, not issue refunds)'; Operator 'Configures venue staff mandates (a booker can hold and confirm; only the owner settles)' — i.e. deliberate configuration, and the Operator line even names the reserved activity.; Avoids seeding `spend`/`settle` to every staff member of a shop or venue, which the trader-status and payments surfaces would then have to unwind. | 01.04 D-01 is about whether GOVERNANCE is modelled, not about what a mandate defaults to, so it is supporting reasoning rather than a governing precedent — choosing 7a ratifies new content.; The Role Lens rows describe what an Operator DOES configure, not what the system does when they configure nothing, so no source states the business-org default either.; Two default models to explain instead of one. | no |
| **7b — Uniform seed across all org types** | Whatever is seeded for bands is seeded for studios, venues, labels, shops and agencies too. | One rule, one explanation, one code path — consistent with sub-domain D-01's reason for merging membership and representation ('specifying them apart guarantees two divergent authority models').; identity-profiles-organizations-cx.md CX-15 already notes that 'A mandate covers the org UNIFORMLY today', so uniformity is the current posture in an adjacent respect. | Seeds `settle` and `sign` to every confirmed staff edge of a venue or shop, which the Operator Role Lens contradicts in spirit ('only the owner settles').; The axis-4 argument that justifies a peer seed — the invisible partnership default — is explicitly scoped by 01.04 D-01 to bands and denied for studios and companies.; CX-15's uniformity claim is about scope ACROSS TYPES OF ONE MULTI-TYPE ORG ('a member mandated for the shop can also edit the venue'), not about defaults across org types, and CX-15 itself flags that as undefined: 'Per-type mandates are not defined, left as Q-04 rather than invented.' Citing it for 7b would misread it. | no |

**Recommendation**: 7a — Band only — The whole argument for a peer seed is the invisible general-partnership default that 01.04.01 D-04 commits the platform to disclosing, and 01.04 index D-01 scopes that argument to bands in terms, denying it for studios ('a studio has an owner'). A studio, venue or shop already has an owner in the sense the seed would otherwise invent. The owner should note that this is a ratification, not an inheritance: no source states what a non-band org defaults to when nothing is configured, and the earlier draft of this entry smuggled it in as part of option A's definition rather than surfacing it as a choice. *(precedent: 01.04 index D-01 (band-specific scope, with the reason given as the invisible partnership default) — supporting, not governing. 01.03.03's Producer/Operator Role Lens rows are corroborating evidence of the explicit-grant posture for business orgs.)*

### DQ-03

> **File**: `02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.05-credit-visibility-embargo.md` · **Domain**: `02` · **4 open / 2 locked**

**When the Producer objects inside D-11's 72-hour evidence-based-lift window, decide four separate things: whether the objection must state a ground, whether it kills / pauses / ignores the lift, who resolves it if it is contested, and whether evidence can be re-submitted without limit.**

**Why it blocks**: 02.01.05's States table ships a 'Lift pending — objection window' row with no successor row for the objected case, and the file contradicts itself about what the Producer's objection is worth: the trigger-model sentence (line 59) and both user-facing copy strings (lines 122, 168) say the lift happens 'unless the Producer objects' (a veto), while D-11's own rationale (line 242), the payment-dispute edge row (line 129) and DT-14/Q-01's resolution claim (line 201, line 255) all assert the Producer is off the critical path (not a veto). Until the owner picks, an implementer cannot: (1) write the successor state(s) after an objection, (2) build the objection form (no ground / free text / enumerated grounds), (3) define the notification fan-out on objection raised and objection resolved, (4) scope the D-11 verification service's contract — Q-04 asks 'what verifies demonstrably public' and calls the 72-hour window 'the compensating control, and it is doing a lot of work', which is only true if the objection actually does something, and (5) decide whether 02.05's dispute engine and domain 24 take load from this path at all.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| L1 — Whether an objection can affect what the credited party themselves sees or exports | 02.01.05 D-09 (line 240): "**Embargo never hides a credit from the party it names.** The credited party always sees and can export their own credit". Reinforced by 02.01.05 D-18 (line 249, privileged non-publishing reads incl. "the credited party's own legal use") and the export edge row (line 132): "a participant may export the embargoed credits of sessions they participated in, and their own credits always". No option below may touch this: whatever an objection does, it acts only on public publication, never on self-view or participant-scoped export. This axis is named here because the superseded entry wrongly listed D-09 among the ratified items a Producer veto would contradict — a veto does not contradict D-09; it leaves both halves of D-09 intact. |
| L2 — Where a non-evidence ground ('the recording really is public, but this specific credit must never be') is allowed to land | 02.02.01 D-10 (line 217): "**Self-removal is an objection, not a delete**, and it asks which objection is meant: \"I wasn't there\" -> dispute (`02.05`); \"don't name me\" -> embargo (`02.01.05`)". The destination inside 02.01.05 is the Axis A table (line 39): "**Confidential** \| Per credit, permanent \| The credited party + the work's ledger owner \| Never, unless both parties agree to lift", and the ghost-production edge row (line 150): "Axis A's `Confidential` level is where it lands — per credit, permanent, requiring the assent of both the credited party and the work's ledger owner". So a permanent non-credit ALWAYS requires the credited party's assent and can never be produced unilaterally by a Producer's objection. Residual and NOT part of DQ-03: whether WeJammin supports `Confidential` at all is 02.01.05 Q-02(b), owner-owned, already deferred to `/ideate-validate`, with Q-05 gated on it. This lock is what makes the superseded entry's Option C unnecessary as a package option — its destination was never open; only whether the objection form offers that ground (axis A2 below) is. |

#### DQ-03.1 — A2 — Must a Producer objection state a ground, and is the ground space open or closed?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the objection control require the Producer to say why, and if so, is the reason free text or a closed list of grounds?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G1 — Bare objection, no ground stated** | The Producer taps 'object' inside the window. Nothing is captured beyond the fact and the timestamp. Participants are told an objection was raised. | Literally matches the three shipped copy strings, which promise participants only 'unless <Producer> objects' (line 122) / 'Publishes in 71h unless <Producer> objects' (line 168) and state no ground requirement; Requires zero platform judgement, the cleanest fit with domain D-10 (credits-attribution-index line 104): "**The platform does not adjudicate truth** ... It records who asserted what, with accountability" | An unreviewable objection forecloses axes A3 and A4: with no stated ground there is nothing for a re-verifier or an adjudicator to act on, so G1 effectively pre-decides that the objection is a veto; It does not even record 'who asserted what', which is the accountability half of domain D-10 that the same argument leans on; Gives DT-10's anti-persona (line 197: `personas.md`'s Producer who "withholds stems as leverage in a payment dispute") a zero-cost, zero-explanation lever | no |
| **G2 — Free-text reason required, logged and shown to every participant, never validated** | The objection form requires a written reason. The platform does not parse, classify or judge it. It is logged to the record and displayed to all participants alongside the objection. | Exact in-file precedent: D-10 (line 241) already does this one step earlier in the same flow — "the decline is logged and shown to the requester", and the edge row (line 128) adds "Decline -> logged, shown to the requester with the decline reason if given"; Costs the platform no judgement at all, so it is compatible with every answer to axis A3; Supplies the reputational signal that any veto-shaped answer depends on for its only check | Unstructured text cannot be routed automatically — a re-verification service (R1) or an adjudicator (R2) needs a human to read and classify it first; Does not distinguish an evidence challenge (the URL is wrong) from a confidentiality claim (the work is public but this credit must not be), which land in completely different places per lock L2 | no |
| **G3a — Closed ground list, restricted to grounds about D-03's existing predicate** | The form offers exactly: (i) the evidence identifies a different recording; (ii) the URL/identifier is not publicly reachable; (iii) other — free text, which routes nowhere automatically. Grounds (i) and (ii) are both assertions that D-03's ratified predicate is not in fact satisfied. | Both enumerated grounds are challenges to a check the platform is already assigned: D-11 (line 242) says "the platform verifies" and D-03 (line 234) sets the predicate as "**the recording is demonstrably public**" — so re-running that check adds no new kind of judgement; Makes axes A3 and A4 answerable, because each ground has an obvious correct handler; Keeps 'other' as an honest escape hatch that is treated as G2 free text rather than as a hidden new predicate | Leaves the one substantive real-world case unhandled: the recording is genuinely reachable but the availability is a leak or bootleg rather than an authorised release; Adds a form and two new copy strings that no current spec text describes | no |
| **G3b — G3a plus an 'available, but not an authorised release' ground** | Everything in G3a, plus a fourth ground asserting the recording is publicly reachable but the availability is unauthorised (leak, bootleg, premature store listing outside DT-07's 7-day revert). Sustaining this ground requires the platform (or an adjudicator) to decide authorised-vs-unauthorised availability. | Covers the one factual situation the file never addresses anywhere, and where a lift would genuinely breach a live confidentiality; The premature-listing neighbour of this case is already partly modelled: DT-07 (line 194) and D-13 (line 244) give a 7-day revert when a store 'honours the release date wrong' | Commissions a predicate no source defines. D-03's ratified predicate is "the recording is demonstrably public", which an unauthorised upload literally satisfies — authorised-vs-unauthorised is a judgement about a client relationship the platform has never seen, which sits much closer to what domain D-10 and 02.01.05 D-10 both refuse than a reachability re-check does; Widens Q-04's already-flagged forgery surface in a second direction: Q-04 (line 258) says the objection window is "the compensating control, and it is doing a lot of work" for verification the platform cannot currently do reliably; 12 Release & Distribution would have to become the authority for 'authorised release' (12.07 assigns identifiers at delivery, but the file itself notes "an off-platform release mints its own"), which is a new hard dependency, not an existing one | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: G3a — Closed ground list, restricted to grounds about D-03's existing predicate — It is the cheapest structure that makes the remaining axes decidable, and every ground it admits is a challenge to a check D-11 already assigns the platform, so it takes on no new adjudication. G3b should be a separate, explicit yes from the owner, because choosing it commissions an authorised-vs-unauthorised test that no file in the tree defines and that D-03's predicate does not license. *(precedent: 02.01.05 D-10 (line 241): a decline in the adjacent early-lift flow is "logged and shown to the requester" with its reason — the file already treats a Producer's refusal as an accountable, reasoned act rather than an anonymous one. This precedent supports requiring a reason; it does not by itself support enumerating grounds, which is the increment G3a adds over G2.)*

#### DQ-03.2 — A3 — What a timely objection does to the pending lift  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does a Producer objection filed inside the 72 hours cancel the lift outright, pause it pending a resolution, or merely get logged while the lift proceeds?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **E1 — Veto: the objection is terminal for that evidence submission; credits return to their prior embargoed state** | The lift is cancelled. The record returns to 'Embargoed — on release' or 'Lift-stalled'. The objection (and its reason, per axis A2) is logged and shown to participants. Fresh evidence may be submitted per axis A5, opening a new window the Producer may object to again. | Only option that requires no rewrite of shipped text: it is exactly what line 59 ('lifts after a **72-hour objection window** unless the Producer objects'), line 122 ('Credits publish in 72 hours unless <Producer> objects') and line 168 ('Publishes in 71h unless <Producer> objects') already say; Requires zero platform judgement — the cleanest fit with domain D-10's "the platform does not adjudicate truth"; Matches D-12's ratchet direction (line 243): "any Producer ... may impose embargo; only the session owner may relax it ... Under disagreement the restrictive value stands", and D-03's asymmetric-cost logic that over-restricting costs a conversation and under-restricting costs a client; Adds no load to Q-04's forgery-prone verification mechanism | Contradicts D-11's own rationale in the same row that grants it (line 242): "Removes the Producer from the critical path without ever overriding a live confidentiality" — a terminal veto puts the Producer back on the critical path; Falsifies the payment-dispute edge row (line 129), which lists as one of three defences "once the work is public they lift it themselves (D-11) without the Producer", and DT-10's summary (line 197): "Embargo may delay publication; it may never conceal a credit from the person it names" — note this sentence is DT-10's and the edge row's, NOT D-09's, and its 'conceal from the person it names' half survives E1 intact because of lock L1; Narrows DT-14/Q-01's resolution claim (line 201, line 255) rather than breaking it: Q-01's terminal form is the Producer's account being deleted, and an absent Producer still cannot object, so E1 still resolves the stated case — what it does not resolve is a present, hostile Producer, which Q-01 never claimed to cover but DT-10 explicitly does; Hands DT-10's anti-persona the exact lever D-11 was written to remove, at zero cost per objection | no |
| **E2 — Pause: the objection suspends the pending lift at the status quo (still embargoed) until resolved per axis A4** | A new state — 'Lift pending — objection contested' — holds the record embargoed while the objection is resolved by whichever resolver axis A4 selects. If the objection fails, the lift completes; if it succeeds, the record returns to its prior embargoed state and new evidence may be submitted per axis A5. Participants are told an objection was raised and told the outcome. | The only option that leaves D-11's rationale, the payment-dispute edge row's three-defence list and DT-10 all true, because the Producer gets a challenge, not a decision; Holds at the status quo ante while contested, which is what the domain already does with unilateral filings — credits-attribution CX-16 (line 31 / line 285): "a contest is an objection, not evidence", "Dispute outputs (amendment, removal) change the page; pendency does not", and 02.05 D-02: "public outcome changes only after adjudication"; The reader needed to resolve it already exists: 02.01.05 D-18 (line 249) provisions a logged, non-publishing scoped read for the dispute adjudicator and Trust & Safety over embargoed records | Rewrites ratified text and two shipped user-facing strings: line 59's 'unless the Producer objects', line 122's 'Credits publish in 72 hours unless <Producer> objects' and line 168's 'Publishes in 71h unless <Producer> objects' all become wrong; Requires a new state row in the Axis A × Axis B table, a defined resolution SLA, and at least two new notification fan-outs (objection raised, objection resolved) that no current spec text describes; The status quo it holds is embargoed, so a resolver that is slow or never fires is a de facto veto — E2 is only meaningful paired with a bounded answer on axis A4; Breaks D-11's own cost claim that "the window costs three days of nothing" (line 59, line 242): a contested lift now costs the resolution period, not three days | **yes** |
| **E3 — Non-blocking: the objection is logged and shown to everyone, and the lift proceeds on schedule** | The 72 hours become a notice period, not a veto period. The Producer's objection is recorded permanently against the record and displayed to participants; publication happens anyway. | Strongest reading of D-03's single predicate and of D-10's "Post-release the question evaporates: D-11 needs no permission" (line 241); Simplest state machine — no successor state, no resolver, no SLA | Removes the compensating control the file explicitly relies on: Q-04 (line 258) says a store URL is "user-supplied and forgeable" and that "the 72-hour objection window is the compensating control, and it is doing a lot of work" — under E3 a forged URL publishes NDA credits with no human able to stop it; Publishing early is the unrecoverable direction the whole file is built around (D-03 / DT-03: "publishing a day late costs nothing, publishing a week early ends a client relationship"), and the retraction edge row (line 124) states plainly that this is "unrecoverable"; Makes the window's own copy ('unless <Producer> objects') a lie the Producer discovers only after the leak | no |

**Recommendation**: E2 — Pause at the status quo pending resolution — The file is internally inconsistent here and BOTH E1 and E2 rewrite ratified text — that is the honest framing, and the superseded entry got it backwards by presenting only the veto as the rewrite. E1 rewrites D-11's rationale, the payment-dispute edge row and DT-10's summary; E2 rewrites line 59 plus two user-facing copy strings. The tie-break is that E1's casualties are the reasoning the feature was built on (the anti-persona defence D-11 exists to provide), while E2's casualties are one clause and two strings whose replacement is a copy change. E2 also keeps Q-04's compensating control intact, which E3 destroys. Owners who weight 'the platform must never judge anything' above the anti-persona defence should pick E1 — it is defensible and it is what the shipped copy currently promises — and then must pick S3 on axis A5 to stop the indefinite-veto loop. *(precedent: credits-attribution CX-16 and 02.05 D-02, read strictly and in the correct direction: a unilateral filing produces no state change while pending, and the outcome changes only after due process. Note this precedent supports pausing toward the STATUS QUO ANTE, and here the status quo ante is embargoed — so it supports E2's hold-at-embargo, and it does NOT support the superseded entry's use of it as a pro-lift argument.)*

#### DQ-03.3 — A4 — Who resolves a contested objection (live only if axis A3 = E2)  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**If an objection pauses the lift, who decides whether it stands — the platform re-running its own check, or the dispute engine?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1 — Platform re-verification, reachability only** | The platform re-runs the same public-availability check D-11 already assigns it, against the objection's stated ground. If the recording is still demonstrably public, the lift completes; if not, the submission is rejected. No human adjudicator, no authorisation judgement. | Identical in kind to the act D-11 already assigns: "the platform verifies, notifies the Producer, and lifts" (line 242), against D-03's predicate "the recording is demonstrably public" (line 234); Fast — keeps the lift close to D-11's 'three days of nothing' cost claim; No new operational load on domain 24 | Can only resolve grounds (i) and (ii) from G3a; it is structurally incapable of resolving a G3b authorisation ground; Puts the entire outcome on the mechanism Q-04 already flags as forgeable and unspecified, with the compensating control now downstream of itself; If the same automated check runs twice with the same inputs it will return the same answer, so R1 only has content if the objection supplies new information — which requires axis A2 = G3a or better | no |
| **R2 — Route to the dispute engine (02.05, riding the domain-24 cross-cut), with R1 as the free first rung** | The platform re-checks reachability inline (R1). If that does not settle it — or if the ground is not a reachability ground — the objection is filed as a contested assertion on the shared dispute engine. A named adjudicator reads the record under the existing privileged grant and decides; the lift completes or is cancelled on that outcome. | The reader already exists and was provisioned for exactly this: 02.01.05 D-18 (line 249) and the privileged-access table (line 99) give the dispute adjudicator "a logged, non-publishing grant" "scoped to the credit under dispute; logged to the case; visible to the parties", even over embargoed records; Direct in-tree routing precedent the superseded entry never cited: 02.02.01 D-10 (line 217) splits a participant objection by kind — \"I wasn't there\" -> dispute (`02.05`); \"don't name me\" -> embargo (`02.01.05`) — and 02.02.01's edge row (line 122) says an unresolved objection "becomes a dispute (`02.05`)"; No new engine: 02.05 D-01 "Rides the platform dispute cross-cut; does not build its own engine", and the cross-cut table in credits-attribution-cx (line 338) already lists the Dispute Case Engine as serving domains 02, 05, 09, 13, 14, 17, 19, 24 and requiring "a general privileged-read grant (D-18)"; 02.05 DT-02 says the platform's honest claim is that "disputes about work we captured are cheap" because it is "*reading a contemporaneous record*" — a store URL or ISRC is exactly that kind of record, so this is the cheap rung of the ladder, not the expensive one | 02.05 D-03's ladder is "direct -> witness -> adjudicated -> legal", and each rung adds latency far beyond 72 hours to a lift the file claims "costs three days of nothing"; Imports per-objection operational load into domain 24, which no current spec budgets for; If the owner also chose G3b, the adjudicator is still deciding an authorisation question no source defines — routing it to a human names a decider but does not supply the test | no |
| **R3 — Two-stage window: the objection buys a fixed extension, and silence resolves it into lift unless the Producer escalates** | An objection pauses the lift for a second fixed period (e.g. a further 7 days). If the Producer does nothing further, the lift completes. To stop it permanently the Producer must themselves file on the dispute engine, paying the escalation cost. | Puts the cost on the party asserting the exception, which is the shape D-11 was reaching for; Keeps the automatic path automatic — no adjudicator is consumed unless someone pays for one | Composite invented here: no source defines a two-stage window, a second-period length, or a silence-resolves-to-publish rule for this flow; Silence-resolves-to-publish is the shape D-03/DT-03 reject elsewhere ('the date never publishes anything'); the timer, not the predicate, would be doing the deciding; A Producer willing to file a dispute still stalls, so R3's protection depends entirely on axis A5 | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: R2 — Route to the dispute engine, with R1 as the free first rung — It is the only resolver that names an accountable decider without inventing one, and every part of it already exists in the tree: the engine (02.05 D-01, domain cross-cut), the privileged non-publishing read over embargoed records (02.01.05 D-18), and the routing habit (02.02.01 D-10). R1 alone cannot resolve anything an automated check did not already resolve on the first pass; R3 is an invented timer that decides by silence. The cost to accept honestly is latency: a contested lift stops being a three-day matter. *(precedent: 02.02.01 D-10 and its edge row: a participant objection is classified by kind and, unresolved, "becomes a dispute (`02.05`)". This precedent points the right way for routing an objection into 02.05; it does NOT speak to whether the paused record should sit embargoed or published while the dispute runs — that comes from CX-16 / 02.05 D-02 (pendency changes nothing), which is why E2 holds at embargo.)*

#### DQ-03.4 — A5 — Whether re-submission of evidence after an objection is bounded  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**After an objection defeats an evidence submission, may participants re-submit without limit, or does the path bound or escalate after repeated objections?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S1 — Unbounded: any participant may submit fresh evidence at any time, each opening a new window** | No cap, no cooldown, no escalation. The cycle can repeat indefinitely. | Nothing is ever permanently lost, which matches the shape of the existing Lift-stalled path (edge row line 121: repeated 90-day nudges, then participants are offered the evidence-based lift); No new thresholds to invent | Under axis A3 = E1 this is the indefinite-veto loop: objecting is free and re-submission achieves nothing, so the credited party's career evidence stays unpublished while the record is on a store — precisely DT-10's anti-persona scenario; Under A3 = E2 + A4 = R2 the resolver bounds it instead, so this con largely disappears — S1's safety is entirely dependent on the A3/A4 answers | no |
| **S2 — Bounded count, then automatic escalation to domain 24 as an abuse pattern** | After N objections against evidence on the same recording, the next submission bypasses the objection window and routes to Trust & Safety as a suspected abuse pattern. | Directly targets the anti-persona DT-10 names, using a domain (24) that already owns "serial-abuse patterns" per 02.05's cross-cut note | N is unsourced — no file in the tree defines a repeat-objection threshold for anything; Commissions a new domain-24 abuse pattern definition and an enforcement policy for it; Treats a possibly-legitimate repeat objector (a Producer whose client keeps a leak live) as an abuser by arithmetic | **yes** |
| **S3 — Objector-cost escalation: the first objection is free; a second objection on the same recording must be filed by the Producer as a dispute** | One free objection per recording. Any further objection against new evidence on that same recording obliges the Producer to file on the 02.05 engine and carry the escalation cost, rather than blocking for free. | Uses the existing ladder rather than a new threshold: 02.05 D-03 "Escalation ladder: direct -> witness -> adjudicated -> legal" with "Each rung is more expensive and less reliable"; Removes the zero-cost repetition that is the whole substance of the indefinite-veto objection, without ever telling the Producer they are wrong; Works as a repair for A3 = E1, which otherwise has no brake at all | The 'first one free, then escalate' rule itself is composed here; no source states it; A well-resourced Producer can still pay the escalation cost repeatedly; Redundant if A3 = E2 and A4 = R2, since every contested objection is already on the engine | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: S1 — Unbounded, CONDITIONAL on axis A3 = E2 and axis A4 = R2 — If a contested objection already goes to an adjudicator, the resolver bounds the loop and no counting rule is needed, so S1 adds nothing to invent. If the owner instead picks E1 (veto) on axis A3, this recommendation flips to S3 — E1 with unbounded free objections is the one combination that leaves DT-10's anti-persona with a permanent, costless weapon, and S3 is the cheapest brake that uses only machinery 02.05 already defines. S2 should be chosen only if the owner wants a hard abuse threshold, and choosing it commissions that threshold in domain 24.

**Affected files**: `02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.05-credit-visibility-embargo.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01-credit-graph-discography-cx.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.02-session-capture/02.02.01-session-roll-call.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.02-session-capture/02.02.02-per-track-contribution-log.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding/02.03.02-claim-inbox-suggested-claims.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.05-credit-dispute-resolution.md`, `02-credits-attribution/credits-attribution-cx.md`, `02-credits-attribution/credits-attribution-index.md`, `12-release-distribution/12.07-identifier-assignment-at-delivery.md`, `24-trust-safety-disputes/trust-safety-disputes-cx.md`


### DQ-04

> **File**: `02-credits-attribution/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding/02.03.03-claim-adjudication.md` · **Domain**: `02` · **2 open / 3 locked**

**When two people claim the same credit, does the credit stay on the first claimant's public page while the contest is open (detaching only if the contest closes as unresolvable), or does it detach the moment the second claim is filed — and once it is unattached, does it fall back to the public unclaimed-shell page it came from, or live only on the work's ledger?**
