**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot build the Contested state of claim adjudication (02.03.03). Two ratified texts give opposite instructions for the same moment: 02.03.03's Happy Path step 2 says "System holds the credit — neither claimant gets it while contested" and 02.03.03 D-03 says "Contested credits are suppressed from public discography pending resolution", while 02.01.02's authoritative reconciliation says "the public discography line is unchanged and never hidden" and names 02.03.03 D-03 as corrected to that rule. Until the owner splits and answers this, nobody can write the state machine for a contested claim, the query that decides which party page a held credit renders on, or the copy either claimant sees. It also blocks the 02.03.02 Contested row ("Held; adjudication") and the 02.03-cx CX-02 evidence pointer, both of which cite behaviour that does not yet exist unambiguously.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| Work-ledger visibility of a claim-contested credit (is the credit ever removed from the work's public ledger because a claim is contested?) | 02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.01-credit-record-contribution-ledger.md D-11: "**Contest marks; it never suppresses.** A contested record stays on the work's ledger, at zero weight in derived surfaces" — context column: "Suppression-on-filing makes a contest a censorship tool — one click to remove any inconvenient true credit." Reinforced by 02.01.02's Cross-Cut Note to 02.05: "**Authoritative reconciliation (resolves 02.04.02 Q-06): a contested credit is NEVER suppressed from the public discography.**" 02.03.03 never contradicts this: its edge case reads "Contested credit is on a public discography \| Suppressed while contested \| Removed from both pages pending resolution" — "both pages" is the two claimants' discography pages, not the work ledger. |
| Public disclosure of the contest itself (does a visitor ever see that a claim is contested?) | 02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.02-public-discography.md D-06: "**A contest is not published.** A contested credit stays on the public page at its existing derived tier with no public marker; the contested marker is a record-view affordance for participants and the credited party." And credits-attribution-cx.md CX-16: "'Contested' is a state of the *record*: a filed dispute produces no public page state". 02.03.03 asks for nothing more public than this — its Contested state says "Held; both told" (the two claimants), and its Q-02 treats even claimant-to-claimant identity disclosure as unresolved. |
| Terminal attachment when a contest closes as Unresolvable | 02-credits-attribution/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding/02.03.03-claim-adjudication.md D-02: "Unresolvable contests leave the credit unattached \| Awarding on weak evidence hands one person the other's career on a guess. Unattached is honest and reversible; wrongly awarded is neither." Its States table agrees: "Unresolvable \| No corroborator exists (import) \| Unattached, permanently marked contested". 02.03-cx CX-03 makes this the majority outcome ("Imported credits produce contests that are structurally unresolvable — no corroborator exists inside the platform") and its interim rule holds `unresolvable` terminal pending OQ-01. |

#### DQ-04.1 — Attachment during pendency — while the contest is open and a corroborator path may still resolve it  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**While a claim contest is open, does the credit stay attached to the first claimant (public line unchanged, contest visible only on the record view) until the contest closes, or does it detach from them the moment the second claim is filed?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P1 — Stay attached until close: the credit remains on the first claimant's page for the duration; it detaches only if the contest closes Unresolvable** | Filing a second claim changes no public state at all. The credit keeps rendering on the first claimant's discography at its existing derived tier; both claimants and the credited party see the contested marker on the record view only. The corroborator route runs as 02.03.03 D-01 specifies ("Resolution is by asking the party who was present"). On Resolved, the credit attaches to the identified claimant. On Unresolvable it detaches to the unattached terminal state that D-02 already locks. Because 02.03.03's Behavior section says a session-captured credit's corroborator "knows. Ask them. Resolved" and an imported credit has no corroborator at all, the pendency window this option protects is short for captured credits and effectively nil for imported ones. | Removes the free-filing removal weapon entirely: under this option a unilateral second claim can never take a line off a rival's page even temporarily, which is the same harm shape 02.01.01 D-11 names ("Suppression-on-filing makes a contest a censorship tool").; Matches what 02.01.02's reconciliation already asserts is true of 02.03.03 — "the public discography line is unchanged and never hidden ... `02.03.03` D-03 aligns to the same rule" — so no ratified sentence has to be reversed on the publication side.; Follows 02.03.03's own DT-02, which rejected merging claim adjudication with credit dispute but recorded that "They share the contested state and the suppression behavior" — 02.05 D-02's suppression language was corrected to "marked for participants, not suppressed and not publicly annotated", so the shared behaviour lands here as marking too.; The cost of being wrong is bounded and already priced by 02.01.02 D-06: "The cost of not publishing is that a possibly-wrong credit stays at the tier it already had — the status quo ante."; A party who believes a credit on someone else's page names them wrongly is not without remedy in the case 02.01.02 D-06 anticipates: "A party contesting a credit *naming them* sets it Private unilaterally" (02.01.05). | Reverses 02.03.03 Happy Path step 2 ("System holds the credit — neither claimant gets it while contested") and 02.03.02's Contested state row ("Held; adjudication") for the pendency window. Both must be rewritten; neither has been superseded on disk.; For the window the contest is open, a possibly-false first claimant keeps a public line they may not own, on a page that is sales collateral (02.01.02 Role Lens, Producer: "their discography is sales collateral, the thing a client checks before booking").; The window is only short if the corroborator path is fast; 02.03.03's "Corroborator (Producer) is gone" and "Corroborator is themselves a claimant" rows both fall back to Unresolvable, and no source sets a response SLA for claim corroboration.; Leaves an asymmetry a reader must be told about: the true owner sees a stranger's page carrying their credit and has no unilateral control over it, because 02.01.05 Private is available to a party named on the record, not to a party who claims it should have named them. | no |
| **P2 — Detach on filing: the second claim immediately removes the credit from the first claimant's page for the duration of the contest** | Filing a second claim reverts the attachment to its pre-claim state at once: the credit leaves the first claimant's identity and becomes unattached (see the next axis for what an unattached credit renders on). The work-ledger line is untouched and stays public per D-11. No public contest marker. On Resolved the winner's claim attaches it; on Unresolvable it simply stays where it already is. The detach mechanism is verbatim in 02.03.02: "Unclaim reverses the merge: credits detach from the wrong identity back to an unclaimed shell, the claimant's discography loses those entries ... Triggered by contest resolution (`02.03.03`) or the claimant self-correcting". | Preserves 02.03.03 Happy Path step 2 and 02.03.02's Contested "Held" exactly as written — no reversal on the attachment side.; One behaviour for pendency and terminal state, so the imported majority case (02.03-cx CX-03) never changes state twice.; Uses only ratified machinery: 02.03.02's unclaim/detach row already names contest resolution as a trigger. | Requires reversing the 02.01.02 reconciliation as applied to claim contests — "the public discography line is unchanged and never hidden ... `02.03.03` D-03 aligns to the same rule" becomes literally false for the first claimant's page.; Recreates, on the claimant's page, the harm 02.01.01 D-11 exists to prevent: filing a claim is free and unilateral (02.03.02: "Two people claim the same credit \| Contested"), so any rival can strip a line from a professional's sales page with one click and no evidence. Nothing in the sources prices this for claim contests — 24 owns "serial false claimant" patterns (02.03.03 edge case) but no rate limit or standing rule for a first filing exists in any spec.; For imported credits the contest is unresolvable by construction (02.03.03 Behavior: "for imported credits, adjudication has **no reliable mechanism at all**"), so a possibly-correct claimant's loss is permanent — the concern 02.03.03 Q-03 already flags ("imported credits with common names may be permanently unattachable. Is that acceptable...?"). | no |

**Recommendation**: P1 — Stay attached until close — With the terminal axis locked to unattached (D-02), P1 can no longer permanently award a credit to whoever clicked first, which was the only serious objection to leaving it in place. What remains is a straight comparison of two exposures: under P2 a free unilateral filing strips a line from a professional's public page with no evidence and no abuse control defined anywhere in the specs; under P1 a possibly-wrong line stays up for the length of a corroboration round-trip — explicitly priced as acceptable in 02.01.02 D-06 ("the status quo ante") and short by construction, since a captured credit's corroborator "knows. Ask them. Resolved" and an imported credit reaches Unresolvable with no window at all. Stated plainly, and against the original entry's framing: this is not a reading under which nothing is reversed. P1 reverses 02.03.03 Happy Path step 2 and 02.03.02's Contested row for the pendency window; P2 reverses the 02.01.02 reconciliation clause for claim contests. One of the two must give, and the owner should pick which. *(precedent: 02.03.03 DT-02 — the file's own reasoning that claim adjudication and credit dispute "share the contested state and the suppression behavior" — read together with 02.05 D-02, whose suppression language was corrected to "marked for participants, not suppressed and not publicly annotated". The shared behaviour was corrected on one side only; P1 finishes the correction. Note what this precedent does not cover: 02.05's reciprocal note holds this feature at arm's length ("different question (who is this person, versus what happened)") and scopes its rule to "Credit-dispute pendency", so it is evidence, not a lock.)*

#### DQ-04.2 — Party-side public render of an unattached credit (reached under every branch, because Unresolvable is locked to unattached)  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**When a credit is unattached because of a claim contest, does it fall back to the public unclaimed-shell page it arrived on, or does it render publicly only on the work's ledger until someone successfully claims it?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S1 — Shell fallback: the credit returns to the unclaimed shell it came from and renders wherever a shell renders** | The credit reverts to exactly its pre-claim state — attached to the unclaimed shell, showing the asserted name string with no link to either claimant, reachable at the shell's public page. This is the state 02.03.02 already describes for the never-claimed case ("Credits stay attached to an unclaimed shell; the discography exists but belongs to nobody \| Public shell page; 'claim this profile'") and the destination its unclaim row already names ("credits detach from the wrong identity back to an unclaimed shell"). Whatever the shell publishes is governed by 02.03.02 Q-02, not by this decision. | Zero new state and zero new render mode: it is the pre-claim status quo, and for an imported credit that nobody has claimed it is already the live behaviour.; Keeps the credit discoverable as a party-shaped fact, not only as a line inside a work page — so the claim CTA that drives the growth loop (02.01.02 DT-01; 02.03.02 DT-01) stays on the surface where the true owner would find it.; Consistent with 02.03.02 D-01, "Claiming verifies the person, never the credit": suspending an identity attachment returns the credit to the identity-less state, and leaves the fact untouched. | Inherits a live upstream dependency the owner has not answered: 02.03.02 Q-02, "What is publicly visible on an unclaimed shell before anyone claims it? Full discography maximises the pull; it also publishes a professional profile of a person who never signed up" — owner-held, deferred to `/create-prd-security`. If shells end up publishing little or nothing, S1 collapses toward S2 in practice.; For a contest between two real people with the same name, the shell page becomes a public page about a disputed identity for as long as the contest lasts — and the interaction with 02.03.03 Q-02 (whether claimants see each other) is unexamined in any source. | no |
| **S2 — Ledger-only: an unattached credit renders publicly on the work's ledger and on no party page** | The credit keeps its public existence solely through the work's ledger (02.01.01 D-11), with no party-shaped page carrying it while unattached. The shell either does not exist as a public surface or does not carry contested/unattached credits. | No dependency on 02.03.02 Q-02 — this option is answerable today and does not pre-commit `/create-prd-security`.; Nothing is hidden: the work-ledger line still satisfies 02.01.01 D-11 and the 02.01.02 reconciliation's never-suppressed rule. | Weakens the acquisition surface the domain depends on: 02.03.02's Unclaimed row and 02.01.02 DT-01 both treat the public shell page with its claim CTA as the mechanism that pulls the true owner in, and this removes it for exactly the credits that most need a claimant.; For the imported majority (02.03-cx CX-03) the terminal state is permanent, so this is a permanent reduction in how a contested credit is discoverable, not a temporary one.; Requires deciding what happens to the shell page's other credits if only some are contested — no source describes a partially-suppressed shell, so the render rule for that page has to be written from scratch. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: S1 — Shell fallback — It is the pre-claim status quo rather than a new behaviour, and it is the destination 02.03.02's unclaim row already names for exactly this trigger. S2 would have to define a new partial-render rule for shell pages that no source describes. The one caveat to state to the owner: S1 does not answer 02.03.02 Q-02 and must not be read as answering it — S1 says the credit sits on the shell, and Q-02 still decides what a shell page shows the public. *(precedent: Drafting precedent only, on where each rule is authored rather than which way it goes: attachment semantics belong to 02.03 (claiming and adjudication) and public-render semantics belong to 02.01.02, so 02.03.03 D-03 should state the attachment rule and reference 02.01.02 for render, and 02.01.02 should reference 02.03.03 rather than asserting on its behalf that it "aligns to the same rule". That misplacement is what produced this conflict.)*

**Affected files**: `02-credits-attribution/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding/02.03.03-claim-adjudication.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.02-public-discography.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding/02.03.02-claim-inbox-suggested-claims.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding/02.03-claiming-cold-start-seeding-cx.md`, `02-credits-attribution/credits-attribution-cx.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.05-credit-dispute-resolution.md`


### DQ-05

> **File**: `02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04.01-attestation-request-confirmation.md` · **Domain**: `02` · **2 open / 2 locked**

**When a role on an attested credit is corrected, what exactly counts as a change big enough to throw away the confirmations that were given — a flat rule (any role change at all) or a semantic rule (the confirmation survives if the new role is 'weaker' than what was confirmed)?**

**Why it blocks**: Two ratified decisions give an implementer opposite answers on the same event. 02.04.01 D-10 says "A material amendment — party, role or work — invalidates every attestation on the row and requires re-asking. Nothing else does". 02.04.02 D-13 says "An amendment preserves attestations only if the new claim is entailed by the attested one; anything else invalidates and re-asks". On an intra-family role narrowing these disagree, so nobody can write the invalidation predicate, the provenance-tier re-derivation, or the amendment approval set. Four downstream files (02.01.06, 07.03.01, 12.01.03, and the release delivery gate) already cite one or the other by name, so the ambiguity is already propagating. Separately, D-13 as written is not one test but two — DT-08 and D-13 state it in strength language ("strictly weaker or equal"), while 02.04.02's own edge-case row states it in containment language ("narrows within a family ... or widens") — so even choosing D-13 does not yield a single predicate.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| 2. Sequencing — does the amendment land first and then invalidate, or must the attesters agree before it lands? | `02.01.06-credit-correction-amendment.md` — D-01 ("Amendment is supersession, never in-place edit"), the Behavior approval rule ("Correcting an **attested** credit — everyone who attested it must agree. Their attestation was of the *old* fact; it cannot silently transfer to a new one."), D-05 ("Successor's provenance tier is re-derived from who agrees, never inherited"), DT-03 ("when a required attester *agrees* to the successor, that agreement **is** a fresh attestation of the new fact, so the successor's tier is **re-derived** from who agreed — not carried over"), D-06 (non-response reminders day 3 / day 10, auto-escalation to dispute day 14, "it never auto-applies and never freezes"), and the ratified state machine Proposed → Awaiting approval → Applied / Escalated. 02.04.01's own cross-cut note assigns this ownership: "02.01.06 owns the amendment, this feature owns the invalidation." |
| 4. The PARTY facet named in 02.04.01 D-10 | `02.01.06-credit-correction-amendment.md` D-03: "Changing the credited *party* is never an amendment — It moves reputation (and potentially income) between two humans. That is definitionally contested even when both parties are agreeable." Echoed at `02.01.01-credit-record-contribution-ledger.md`: "Removal is an amendment (`02.01.06`) requiring the credited party's agreement — and changing the *party* is never an amendment at all (`02.01.06` D-03), it is a dispute", and at 02.01.06's own edge case "Correction changes the *party* (wrong person credited) \| Not an amendment — it is a deletion plus a new credit ... Forced through dispute". |

#### DQ-05.1 — 1. The materiality test on the ROLE facet of an attested credit  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**When an amendment changes the role on a credit that already carries attestations, is the invalidation test a flat identity test (any role change invalidates every attestation on the row), a containment test over 02.06 D-06's base+modifier structure (dropping a modifier within one base carries; anything else invalidates), or a semantic strength test (the attestation carries whenever the new role claim is strictly weaker than or equal to the attested one)?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1 — Flat identity test. 02.04.01 D-10 is the single boundary; 02.04.02's carrying cases are restated as an instrument-axis carve-out** | One materiality boundary, authored once in 02.04.01 D-10 (the feature that 02.04.01's own cross-cut note says "owns the invalidation") and referenced everywhere else: any change to the role or the work invalidates every attestation on the row; nothing else does. Because instrument is explicitly not in the record's uniqueness key — 02.01.01 D-03 "At most one record per (party, role, work)", restated at 02.06 D-16 "Instrument is multi-valued on one credit record; role is not" — an instrument accretion or refinement is not a change to the credit's claim identity and attestations stand untouched. 02.04.02 D-13 and DT-08 are rewritten as consequences of D-10 rather than as a competing test. | It needs no machinery that no source defines. A role-strength predicate has no substrate: 02.06 gives the role axis only `base` + optional `modifier` (D-06) with a search-time rollup, and confines hierarchy to the instrument axis (D-07: "Instruments carry a functional hierarchy (keys / guitars / drums & percussion / strings / brass / woodwind / voice / electronic production)"). 02.04.02's cross-cut note to 02.06 is candid that entailment is a request, not a fact: "the taxonomy must support 'is claim B entailed by claim A', which is a demand on its shape, not just its vocabulary". I read 02.06 in full: it contains no strength ordering, no entailment decision, and no cross-cut note back to 02.04 at all — the demand is unanswered.; It satisfies the demand 02.04.01 makes on itself: "the materiality boundary must be defined once, in one place, or the two will drift into a hole". The place is 02.04.01 D-10, which already exists and which the two most recent owner ratifications already treat as domain 02's canonical materiality definition.; It makes three files agree rather than two. 02.01.06 D-05 already rules "Successor's provenance tier is re-derived from who agrees, never inherited", and its DT-03 that "when a required attester agrees to the successor, that agreement is a fresh attestation of the new fact". That is D-10's position, not D-13's. Under A1 all three files say one thing; under A3 (and partly A2), 02.01.06 D-05 and DT-03 have to be reopened.; It resolves 02.04.02's own internal incoherence instead of inheriting it. DT-08 says in one sentence "strictly weaker or equal. Widening and intra-family narrowing carry; any strengthening or lateral change invalidates" — but a narrowing IS a strengthening, so the sentence contradicts itself unless "intra-family narrowing" is read as the instrument axis. Its own worked example confirms that reading: the edge-case row's pair is "Drums" → "Drums — brushes", and Drums is an instrument (02.06 D-07's drums & percussion family), not a role. 07.03.01 renders the same fact as "Performer · Drums" — role Performer, instrument Drums.; Both downstream OUTCOMES survive unchanged. 07.03.01's worked pair still resolves the same way — "Performer · Drums" → "Performer · Drums, Percussion" carries (instrument accretion, 02.06 D-16), "Performer · Drums" → "Producer" invalidates (a role change) — and 12.01.03 D-02, which deliberately implements no mechanism of its own, keeps projecting a single upstream rule. Their CITATIONS still need editing (see cons).; It closes the laundering paths with no judgement call. 02.04.01 DT-07's amend-after-attest attack and 02.04.02 DT-08's "tambourine" → "produced by" path are both role changes, so both are caught without anyone having to rank role strength correctly. | The genuine "Producer" → "Additional Production" correction — 02.01.06's own opening example of a routine, non-adversarial fix — invalidates attestations and costs a fresh ask. 02.04.01 DT-07 names this cost explicitly: "too broad and every typo fix triggers a re-ask storm that burns the rate limits and the goodwill".; It gives up the one user-visible promise 02.04.02's edge-case row made for the role axis: the entailed amendment where the attester sees "Nothing". Under A1 the attester is always asked, bounded by 02.04.01 D-09's cadence (1 request + ≤2 nudges per credit × attester; ≤5 outstanding and ≤10 per rolling 30 days per recipient) and D-21's collapsing of concurrent requests into one ask.; It requires rewriting a ratified decision (02.04.02 D-13) and its Deep Think annotation DT-08, not merely scoping them — the ideation record must show D-13 subsumed, not quietly deleted.; Four downstream citations must be edited even though their outcomes do not change: 07.03.01's counter-attested edge case and its 02.04 cross-cut note both name "02.04.02 D-13" as the governing rule, and 12.01.03 D-02 and DT-04 do the same. These are reference-only edits, but they are edits. | no |
| **A2 — Containment test over 02.06 D-06's base+modifier structure only** | The carry rule is restricted to the structure 02.06 actually ratifies. A role is `base` + optional `modifier` (D-06, modifier set: Additional, Assistant, Co-, Vocal, Executive, Associate). An amendment that drops a modifier while keeping the base carries the attestation; any change of base, and any addition or substitution of a modifier, invalidates. No role-strength ordering is defined and no new 02.06 decision is required, because D-06 already supplies the structure and D-06's traversal rollup already reasons over it. | It needs no new 02.06 decision. D-06 is ratified structure, and D-14's admission test already parses roles this way: "'Assistant Engineer' fails the second — it is Engineer + Assistant". So A1's decisive objection to A3 ("requires machinery no source defines") genuinely does not reach A2.; It is mechanical and auditable — a string-structure comparison, not a semantic judgement, so it does not mint the platform-detected materiality judgement that DEC-027 and DEC-031 rejected. | It carries in exactly the wrong direction. Dropping a modifier is a widening in containment terms but a STRENGTHENING in industry terms: "Assistant Engineer" → "Engineer" and "Additional Production" → "Producer" both carry under A2. That is the amend-after-attest hole 02.04.01 DT-07 and D-10 exist to close, re-opened structurally rather than one taxonomy row at a time.; It does not deliver the benefit A3 was wanted for. 02.01.06's own opening example is "a role recorded as 'Producer' that should be 'Additional Production'" — that ADDS a modifier, so A2 invalidates it and re-asks, exactly like A1. A2 pays the laundering cost of a carry rule and does not buy the correction path.; Every source-quoted example of a carrying amendment is on the instrument axis ("Drums" → "Drums — brushes", "Performer · Drums" → "Performer · Drums, Percussion"), which A1 already handles by construction. A2 has no worked example anywhere in the specs that A1 does not already cover. | no |
| **A3 — Semantic strength test. 02.04.02 D-13 is authoritative and 02.06 must supply a role-strength ordering** | Attestations survive any amendment whose new role claim is entailed by the attested one — strictly weaker or equal — and 02.06 takes on a new obligation it has not accepted: a partial order over roles (base + modifier) that can answer "is Additional Production weaker than Producer", "is Co-Producer weaker than Producer", "is Mix Engineer weaker than Engineer". 02.04.01 D-10 is demoted to a special case of D-13 for the work facet. | It preserves the cheapest correction path for exactly the class of fix 02.01.06 exists to serve — a role recorded too strongly, corrected downward — without spending an attester's attention or the requester's relationship capital (02.04.01 D-08, D-09).; It keeps the two downstream files that cite D-13 by name (07.03.01, 12.01.03) literally correct with no edit.; It matches DT-08's stated epistemics: an attestation of "Producer" does entail "Additional Production", so re-asking is asking a question already answered. | It requires machinery no source defines, and choosing it commissions that machinery as new work. 02.06 has no strength ordering over roles and takes no decision toward one; D-06's modifier set is structure for a search rollup, and the only hierarchy it ratifies (D-07) is on the instrument axis. Writing that order is a new product decision with litigation-adjacent stakes — DT-08 itself: "'produced by' vs 'co-produced' vs 'additional production' is the canonical example, and it has ended friendships and produced litigation".; The order is a permanent laundering surface. Every pair the platform ranks wrongly is a silent carry of a real attestation onto a stronger claim, and 02.06's own axis table records that the role axis has "High" deprecation pressure — "job titles are invented, split and retired continuously" — so the order would need maintaining forever.; It contradicts the direction of the two most recent owner ratifications on this question shape. DEC-027 (P-03) rejected the semantic reading of materiality in favour of a mechanical predicate, its downstream noting that this "avoids minting a third project-wide materiality definition alongside `07.03.03` D-07 and `02.04.01` D-10". DEC-031 (P-07a) made materiality "owner-declared, never platform-detected" and "reuses D-07's existing definition rather than minting a fourth materiality concept". A3 mints a platform-computed semantic materiality test.; It reopens 02.01.06 D-05 and DT-03, closed in Step 5 with an explicit anti-laundering rationale: "Inheriting the tier would let a bad actor launder a fabricated value into a high-confidence record by amending a well-attested one." | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A1 — Flat identity test; 02.04.01 D-10 is the single boundary — A3 is the only option that requires inventing machinery, and the machinery is a semantic strength ordering over role names that 02.06 does not define, does not decide toward, and would have to maintain against what its own axis table calls High deprecation pressure. A2 avoids that invention but carries in the wrong direction — dropping a modifier ("Assistant Engineer" → "Engineer") is a strengthening, so A2 re-opens the exact hole D-10 and DT-07 were written to close, while still invalidating 02.01.06's own worked correction ("Producer" → "Additional Production", which ADDS a modifier). A1 also makes three files agree instead of two: 02.01.06 D-05 already rules that a successor's tier is "re-derived from who agrees, never inherited", which is D-10's position and not D-13's. And A1 is the reading 02.04.02's own worked example already supports — its carrying pair "Drums" → "Drums — brushes" is an instrument change, and instrument is excluded from the (party, role, work) key by 02.01.01 D-03 and 02.06 D-16, so D-10's "nothing else does" already lets it stand with no entailment rule at all. The re-ask cost A1 accepts is bounded by machinery that exists: 02.04.01 D-09's cadence and per-recipient caps and D-21's collapsing of concurrent requests. *(precedent: DEC-027 (P-03, 2026-07-21) and DEC-031 (P-07a, 2026-07-21), both ratified on the same question shape. DEC-027 chose a mechanical predicate over a semantic materiality judgement, its downstream reading verbatim: "Rejecting the semantic reading also avoids minting a third project-wide materiality definition alongside `07.03.03` D-07 and `02.04.01` D-10" — which both rejects the semantic reading and names 02.04.01 D-10 as one of the project's two existing materiality definitions. DEC-031 reads verbatim: "Materiality is **owner-declared, never platform-detected**" and "it reuses D-07's existing definition rather than minting a fourth materiality concept." DEC-028 (P-04, "authored where owned and referenced where not ... Referencing validates presence and surfaces the owner's verdict; it never restates the requirement") also applies, and it points at 02.04.01, not 02.01.01: 02.04.01's own cross-cut note assigns ownership — "02.01.06 owns the amendment, this feature owns the invalidation" — so the boundary is authored in 02.04.01 D-10 and referenced, never restated, in 02.04.02, 02.01.06, 07.03.01 and 12.01.03.)*

#### DQ-05.2 — 3. Does 02.06 D-11's one retroactive role re-resolution (first mapping of a pending alias) invalidate attestations?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**When the platform performs the single retroactive re-resolution 02.06 D-11 permits — the first mapping of a pending alias onto a canonical role, which changes the stored role on credits that already exist — does that count as a material role change that invalidates the attestations on those credits, or is it a label completion that leaves attestations untouched?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C1 — Pending-alias first mapping never invalidates; it is resolution completion, not a role change** | 02.06 D-11's exception is declared non-material for attestation purposes and stays what 02.06 already treats it as: an announced, informational, per-party notification. 02.04.01's cross-cut note claim that "a taxonomy change that re-maps an existing role is a material amendment and invalidates attestations" is narrowed to say that no taxonomy operation ever invalidates, because D-11's general rule already guarantees stored credits never re-resolve. | It matches what 02.06 already ratified. D-11: "Resolution happens once, at assertion. The record holds the canonical it resolved to plus the literal — never a live alias reference. Existing credits never re-resolve." D-04: "Deprecation never rewrites existing credits." And the notification cross-cut note treats the D-11 re-point as "one notice **per party**, deduplicated, informational, never an action request".; It is honest about what the attester saw. 02.06 D-03/D-13 keep the requester's literal on the credit line, so the attester confirmed the literal that is still displayed; only the machine-readable canonical was filled in.; It has a defined remedy when the mapping is wrong, already ratified: 02.06's edge case "Credited party disagrees with the mapping their pending alias received — The vocabulary is not the venue. They amend the credit's role through `02.01.06`", which then goes through Axis 1 and Axis 2 normally. | It leaves one path where a stored role changes without any attestation consequence, so an admin mapping error silently changes what a canonical role says on an attested credit until someone notices and amends.; It requires editing 02.04.01's cross-cut note, which currently asserts the opposite. | no |
| **C2 — Pending-alias first mapping invalidates attestations on the affected credits** | The re-point is treated as a material role change under Axis 1's test, so every attestation on every affected credit is invalidated and re-asked. | It keeps 02.04.01's cross-cut note literally true and gives one uniform answer: any change to the stored role, from any source, invalidates.; It removes the admin-error blind spot in C1. | It collides at scale with the ceiling 02.04.01 D-09 sets. 02.06's own edge case contemplates "A pending alias accumulates 4,000 credits before it is mapped", and mass invalidation would push hundreds of fresh attestation asks through a channel capped at ≤5 outstanding and ≤10 per rolling 30 days per recipient — DT-11's harassment ceiling.; It punishes credits for a deferral the platform chose. 02.06 D-03/D-19 commit the credit at full tier precisely so the vocabulary never blocks the fact; invalidating later makes that promise conditional.; It is in tension with the notification cross-cut's ratified framing of the re-point as "informational, never an action request". | no |

**Recommendation**: C1 — pending-alias first mapping never invalidates — 02.06 already ratified the general rule that existing credits never re-resolve (D-11) and that deprecation never rewrites credits (D-04); the pending-alias exception exists only because the alternative is "credits permanently unexportable and unqueryable" (D-11 context). Treating that completion as a material role change would send mass asks through a channel 02.04.01 D-09 caps at ≤5 outstanding per recipient, on 02.06's own 4,000-credit example, for a change the attester cannot even see — the displayed literal is unchanged (02.06 D-03, D-13). The wrong-mapping case has a ratified remedy that routes back through Axis 1 properly: 02.06's edge case sends it to 02.01.06 as an amendment. *(precedent: DEC-028 (P-04): 02.06 owns the vocabulary and its resolution rules (02.06 D-05, "Domain 02 owns the vocabulary; other domains consume it"), so 02.04.01's cross-cut note is a reference that must surface 02.06's verdict rather than restate a contradicting one.)*

**Affected files**: `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04.01-attestation-request-confirmation.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04.02-provenance-tiers-credit-confidence.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence-cx.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.06-credit-correction-amendment.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.01-credit-record-contribution-ledger.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/02.06-credit-role-instrument-taxonomy.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/02-credits-attribution/credits-attribution-cx.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/07-music-projects-collaboration/07.03-contributors-access-confidentiality/07.03.01-contributor-roster-role-assignment.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/12-release-distribution/12.01-release-builder/12.01.03-delivery-readiness-gate.md`


### DQ-06

> **File**: `02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04.02-provenance-tiers-credit-confidence.md` · **Domain**: `02` · **2 open / 4 locked**

**When ring detection demotes an attestation edge, does that demotion move the credit's categorical provenance rung — the public label AND the rung-gated consumers that read it — or only the internal 0.0-1.0 confidence score?**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot write the tier derivation at all. Specifically: (1) they cannot fix the input signature of 02.04.02's rung derivation — whether it reads 02.04.04's negative multiplier or only the evidence set; (2) they cannot write 02.04.04's output contract to 02.04.02, because option B would require a rung-gate cut value that exists nowhere in the tree and that 02.04.04 Q-04 does not defer (Q-04 defers only the score-side demotion cut); (3) at least nine assertions across six files in three domains currently state the opposite of 02.04.02's own score-input table, so whichever behaviour is implemented will contradict written spec text until this is ratified; (4) 06.02.02's rung>=5 credential-block membership rule and 02.08's re-emission staleness trigger list are both stated in terms of demotion and both change meaning depending on the answer.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| Axis 3 — Is the credited party notified when a demotion lowers their standing? | 02.04-attestation-credit-confidence-cx.md CX-03 Q4: 'None, deliberately. A credited party is not told their tier dropped from demotion, even though 02.04.03 D-01 tells them when it drops from retraction. That inconsistency is intentional (retraction has a nameable cause; suspicion does not).' Reinforced by 02.04.02's edge case row: 'Credited party notified with a reason for retraction/amendment; silently for demotion (CX-03 Q4 — deliberate asymmetry)'; by 02.04.04 D-01 ('Demote silently; never accuse') and D-02 ('Invisible to all four personas'); and by credits-attribution-cx.md CX-05 item 4: 'Tier drops notify only when caused by retraction, never when caused by ring demotion (02.04 CX-03 Q4).' NOTE: 02.01.02's edge-case cell 'Label change; credited party notified (02.04.02)' contradicts all four and is a defect to correct regardless of how Axis 1 and Axis 2 resolve. |
| Axis 4 — How does demotion present in traversal / search results? | .memory/wiki/decisions.md DEC-020 (ideation D-42, CQ-04 Option C, ratified 2026-07-20), recorded at 02.01.03 D-05 and 02.04 index D-06: 'An otherwise eligible edge follows ordinary publication, viewer-visibility, embargo, provenance-floor, role, query-shape, fan-safety, and normal result-window gates. If returned, its path renders normally while the existing per-attestation-edge-derived demotion affects only ordinary ranking. No collusion-specific threshold, hiding, label, annotation, rationale, tooltip, notification, detector metadata, or unweighted bypass exists.' This axis is not reopened by DQ-06 and must not be overwritten by whatever is chosen on Axes 1 and 2 — note that under Axis 1 option C the traversal demotion path breaks unless a replacement contract is written, because traversal consumes 02.04.02's score. |
| Axis 5 — What may cross the 02.04.04 -> 02.05 dispute boundary? | .memory/wiki/decisions.md DEC-010 (CQ-03 Option B, ratified 2026-07-19), recorded at 02.04.04 D-07, 02.04 index D-05 and credits-attribution-cx CX-18: 'CollusionEvidenceConstraintV1 contains only contractVersion, opaque attestationEdgeId, per-edge negativeMultiplier, and literal requiresNonTopologicalCorroboration: true. Unknown versions are excluded. No raw score, topology signal, cluster/witness identity, trust score, ring flag, hard exclusion, or enforcement recommendation crosses the boundary.' The field's own consumer rule reads 'Multiplies this edge's contribution to dispute confidence.' This contract is dispute-facing only and neither governs nor is governed by the tier question in Axis 1. |
| Axis 6 — What SHAPE does the demotion signal have (per-edge vs per-cluster; weight vs cap)? | 02.04.04 D-05: 'Demotion is per attestation edge, never per cluster... A per-edge penalty devalues only the specific edges the topology implicates, and the tier reads it as one negative multiplier term.' And 02.04.02 D-12: 'Demotion is a weight, never a cap' (DT-14: 'A cap makes new evidence a no-op for a flagged cluster — which is a probe oracle'). Both are settled; DQ-06 decides only WHICH derivation reads that per-edge weight, not its shape. |

#### DQ-06.1 — Axis 1 — Does ring-detection demotion enter the RUNG derivation, or only the internal score?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does 02.04.02's rung derivation read 02.04.04's per-edge negative multiplier (so a demoted credit's categorical rung falls), or does the multiplier enter only the continuous internal score, leaving the rung a pure function of the kind of active evidence?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — Score-only: the rung is invariant to detection; only the internal score moves** | The per-edge negative multiplier enters the derivation exactly where 02.04.02's score-input table already places it: inside the continuous internal score (0.0-1.0). The rung stays a pure function of the KIND of active evidence — imported / self-asserted / owner-asserted / session-logged / confirmed / captured-and-confirmed. A demoted credit keeps its rung-6 label and its Fan sentence; its score, and therefore traversal rank (02.01.03 D-01/D-05) and every other score consumer, falls. The rows that say otherwise are corrected: they bundle demotion with retraction and amendment-invalidation, which genuinely remove active attestations and therefore genuinely change the evidence kind. | It is where 02.04.02 itself files the input. The score-input table row reads verbatim: 'Ring-detection demotion (02.04.04) \| Negative multiplier, never a cap (DT-14) \| Detection never writes the rung or the score directly (CX-03 Q1); it supplies one penalty term the derivation reads.' The row is in the SCORE section, not the rung section.; Every rung gate in the rung table is defined on a kind of evidence, not a quantity — rung 5 requires 'at least 1 co-present party, who is neither the asserter nor the credited party, confirmed the contribution'. A multiplier removes no confirmation of any kind, so under kind-gating there is nothing for it to move.; It preserves the property the file calls load-bearing: 'This is what removes the adversary optimises to clear the next rung and stops hazard: the rungs are not grindable, because they are not thresholds on a number.'; It closes a probe oracle. A public categorical label dropping with no nameable cause is visible to the credited party on their own page and to every visitor. That is a louder signal than the ambiguous score movement DT-14 was already unwilling to permit, and it contradicts 02.04.04 D-02 ('Invisible to all four personas') and D-01 ('Demote silently; never accuse').; It keeps the label honest under D-02 ('Labels describe the evidence, not the conclusion'). CX-03 already concedes what a demotion-driven rung drop actually does: 'The tier claims to describe evidence quality and in those cases describes a suspicion instead.'; It makes rung-at-time-T reproducible. DT-16 says rung-at-T follows from the append-only evidence log 'but only if every input is historised, including the demotion score.' Under A the rung has no mutable input, so rung-at-T holds regardless of what the detector does. (This buys back the RUNG only — the internal score still has a mutable input and is still not reproducible at time T unless the detection score is historised. DT-16's requirement on 02.04.04 is narrowed, not removed.) | A fully corroborated fiction keeps a top-rung public label indefinitely. 02.04.02's collusion edge case currently ends 'Nothing, until detection demotes' — under A the public page never changes and that cell must be rewritten.; CX-03 calls demotion 'the tier model's only defence against fully-corroborated fiction'. Under A that defence is real but never visible on the page: it protects ranking, search and dispute weighting, not the label a fan or a booker reads.; It is the largest textual correction of the three options measured in files touched: at least nine assertions across six files in three domains say the rung drops on demotion (02.04.02 States row and 'Rung drops after being public' edge case; 02.04.04 States row, Happy Path step 4, edge cases 1 and 3; 02.04-cx CX-03 relationship paragraph; 02.01.02 edge case and cross-cut note; 01.06.02 'Rung drops after a booker relied on it'; 06.02.02 'Credit is silently demoted by ring detection').; It settles nothing about the rung-GATED consumers on its own — see Axis 2. Choosing A without answering Axis 2 silently makes every rung gate invariant to detection too. | no |
| **B — Rung-moving: a sufficiently demoted edge stops satisfying a rung gate, so the rung falls** | The negative multiplier is applied to each attestation edge before the rung gate is evaluated, and an edge whose weighted contribution falls below a cut no longer satisfies the independent-co-present-confirmation gate. A demoted rung-6 credit falls to rung 4 and a demoted rung-5 credit to rung 3, silently, on a public page. This is what the written States rows in 02.04.02 and 02.04.04, and the edge cases in 02.01.02, 01.06.02 and 06.02.02, literally say today. | It is what the written rows across three domains actually assert, so it requires the fewest textual edits.; It gives the tier a visible defence: a corroborated fiction's public label eventually weakens, which is the intuitive reading of CX-03's 'the tier model's only defence against fully-corroborated fiction'.; It keeps the rung and the score telling the same story, so a consumer that reads only the rung is not misled about a suspected edge. | It requires a rung-gate cut that no source defines. 02.04.04's scoring skeleton defines exactly one cut — 'The demotion cut is the score above which an edge's weight is multiplied down' — and Q-04 defers only that score-side value. Nothing anywhere defines a threshold at which an edge stops counting as a confirmation for rung purposes. Choosing B commissions that machinery.; It contradicts the tier-side rule that demotion is 'a weight, never a cap' (02.04.02 D-12, DT-14) as applied to the rung: a gate an edge can fall out of is a cap on the rung by another name.; It re-grinds the rungs. Making a rung gate sensitive to a continuous weight makes the rungs thresholds on a number again, which 02.04.02 says is exactly the hazard kind-gating exists to remove.; It is a probe oracle by construction, defeating 02.04.04 D-02 and the DT-14 argument: the demoted party sees a categorical label change on their own public page with no cause given, learns they are flagged, and learns roughly when the detector fired.; It lands maximum severity on the acknowledged dominant false positive. 02.04.04 DT-02 says a real tight scene and a ring are 'nearly identical' and 'The detector cannot be confidently right'; its edge case 1 calls the false positive 'The dominant false positive, by a wide margin'; 02.01.02 DT-08 says these pages are cited 'in press, sleeve notes, court filings and academic work'. B silently rewrites a cited public claim about a real professional on a signal the specs say cannot be confidently right.; It breaks rung-at-time-T unless the mutable detection score is fully historised — DT-16 already calls the unhistorised case 'a lie the platform tells under oath'. | **yes** |
| **C — Detection is excluded from 02.04.02 entirely: it weights no rung and no score** | The negative multiplier never enters 02.04.02's derivation at all. Both the public rung and the internal confidence score become pure functions of evidence, untouched by topology. Every consumer that should feel the demotion receives the multiplier through its own contract, on the CollusionEvidenceConstraintV1 pattern that 02.05 already has. | Cleanest separation: the confidence model measures evidence only, which is the purest form of D-02 ('Labels describe the evidence, not the conclusion') and of 02.04 index D-03 ('Evidence sets the tier; it never gates the credit').; Removes DT-16's mutable-input problem completely — for the score as well as the rung.; The per-consumer contract shape is precedented: DEC-010 / 02.04.04 D-07 already ratifies exactly this shape for the 02.04.04 -> 02.05 boundary. | It breaks traversal demotion unless re-plumbed. Traversal does not receive the multiplier directly today: 02.04.02's cross-cut note says 02.01.03's 'D-01 (tier-weighted traversal, no unweighted mode) consumes the score, not the rung', and the two-axis table lists traversal as a consumer of the score. Removing the demotion input from the score removes it from traversal, which would contradict the ratified DEC-020 / 02.01.03 D-05 contract unless a new direct traversal contract is written. C therefore commissions machinery too.; It deletes a written score-input row in a [DEEP] file and rewrites CX-03's framing of demotion as the tier model's defence — a larger change than A for no gain on the question actually in dispute.; Every other consumer of the confidence score (marketplace 05 credibility, any future domain 10 gate) would see an undemoted score, so the suspicion has to be re-plumbed per consumer — more surface, more places the detector can leak. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A — Score-only: the rung is invariant to detection; only the internal score moves — A is the only option that adds no machinery and no contradiction with a ratified rule. 02.04.02 files ring demotion in its score-input table, not its rung table, and every rung gate is written as a kind of evidence rather than a quantity — a multiplier removes no evidence of any kind. B needs a rung-gate cut value that exists nowhere in the tree (02.04.04 Q-04 defers only the score-side demotion cut), and a gate an edge can fall out of is a cap on the rung, which 02.04.02 D-12 / DT-14 forbid on the tier side. B also converts the public label into the probe oracle DT-14 and D-12 were written to prevent, and lands it on the false positive 02.04.04 calls dominant by a wide margin. C is directionally right but breaks the traversal demotion path that DEC-020 ratified, because traversal consumes the score. The nine rows that say 'lower rung' each bundle demotion with retraction or amendment-invalidation — triggers that genuinely delete active attestations and so genuinely change the evidence kind — and then state the consequence of the bundle. That is an editing defect, but only the owner can say which side of it is the product. *(precedent: DEC-020 / 02.01.03 D-05 / 02.04 index D-06 (CQ-04 Option C, ratified 2026-07-20) is a POSTURE precedent, not a governing one: it answers the same shape of question for the sibling consumer — 'An otherwise eligible edge follows ordinary publication, viewer-visibility, embargo, provenance-floor, role, query-shape, fan-safety, and normal result-window gates. If returned, its path renders normally while the existing per-attestation-edge-derived demotion affects only ordinary ranking. No collusion-specific threshold, hiding, label, annotation, rationale, tooltip, notification, detector metadata, or unweighted bypass exists.' Its stated problem is traversal presentation and its downstream names only 02.01.03 D-05/Q-03 and 02.04 D-06/CX-03; it does not decide the discography rung. Extending the same posture to the rung is the consistent extension, not a resolution. Also genuinely applicable: 02.04.04 D-01 ('Demote silently; never accuse'), D-02 ('Invisible to all four personas'), and 02.04.02 D-02. NOT applicable, and previously mis-cited: 02.04.04 D-07 / CollusionEvidenceConstraintV1 — its negativeMultiplier field reads 'Multiplies this edge's contribution to dispute confidence', and D-07 scopes it as 'the only dispute-facing detection contract'. It governs the 02.04.04 -> 02.05 boundary, not the 02.04.04 -> 02.04.02 tier boundary, and carries no force here.)*

#### DQ-06.2 — Axis 2 — If the public rung is invariant to detection (Axis 1 = A), do the surfaces that GATE on rung also become invariant?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**The rung is simultaneously a public label and a gate value read by non-presentation consumers. If demotion no longer moves the rung, do those gates read the same invariant rung, or does a separate demotion-adjusted value exist for them?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 — Single rung: every gate reads the same detection-invariant rung** | One rung, one value, one meaning. A suspected-ring rung-6 credit stays inside 06.02.02's rung>=5 credential block on a teacher's income-generating tuition page, stays above a professional viewer's tier-floor filter in 02.01.02's archive view, and (should domain 10 ever set a registration floor) would sit above it. Detection reaches those surfaces only through whatever they read off the internal score, which today is none of them. | Preserves 02.04.02 D-06's ratified two-axis model exactly as written — 'Two axes: a categorical rung (public, kind-gated, totally ordered) and a continuous score (internal, 0.0-1.0)' — with no third value.; Maximum invisibility, which is what 02.04.04 D-01 and D-02 ask for. No gate a demoted party can probe.; Keeps 06.02.02's stated contract intact: D-04 makes it 'a read-only consumer of domain 02' that 'can never be more permissive than its source'. If the source rung does not move, neither does the block, with no new logic on 06's side.; It changes nothing about 02.08's RIN eligibility either way — 02.08 D-06 excludes 'low-tier (import / self-asserted)' credits, i.e. rungs 1-2, which demotion cannot reach from rung 5 or 6 under any option. | This is the option that makes Axis 1's strongest objection bite hardest: a fully corroborated fiction keeps not only its public label but its place in a paid-teaching credibility block on a minors-facing page, indefinitely and invisibly.; 02.08's re-emission staleness trigger currently lists demotion alongside amendment and retraction ('amendment, retraction and demotion each fire a post-mutation event carrying the changed credit ID'). Under Axis 1 = A plus this option, demotion changes nothing a RIN carries, so it should drop off that trigger list — an edit, and a small shrink of domain-index Q-08. | no |
| **2 — Split rung: a second, demotion-adjusted gate value alongside the invariant public rung** | The public label rung is invariant exactly as in Axis 1 option A, but a parallel gate-facing rung value, derived from the same evidence set with the demotion multiplier applied, is what rung-gated consumers read. A demoted credit's discography line is unchanged while it silently leaves 06.02.02's credential block and falls below 02.01.02's professional tier-floor filter. | Answers Axis 1 option A's strongest objection without creating option B's public probe oracle: the label a fan or a booker reads never changes.; Keeps a real, if invisible, defence against fully-corroborated fiction on the surfaces where a credit converts to money. | Reopens 02.04.02 D-06, which locks the model at exactly two axes. Adding a third derived categorical value is a new decision on top of this one.; It is not fully unobservable. 06.02.02's teacher sees a private view of their own block ('3 credits aren't confirmed yet. Ask someone who was there'), so a credit silently leaving the block is weakly probe-observable to its own subject; and a professional can run 02.01.02's tier-floor filter over their own archive. That is a weaker oracle than option B's public label change, but it is not zero, and 02.04.04 D-02 asks for invisibility to all four personas.; Commissions new machinery: a second derivation, a rule for which consumers read which value, and the same rung-gate-cut problem option B has (an edge must stop counting for the gate rung, and no cut value exists).; It does not decide domain 10. Whether a rung floor gates royalty registration is explicitly 10's call (02.04.02 cross-cut note; ideation-cx item 20), so a gate rung would be offered to 10, not imposed on it. | **yes** |
| **3 — Per-consumer demotion contract: each rung-gating consumer receives its own constrained per-edge multiplier** | No second rung. Each consumer that wants demotion sensitivity gets a versioned, constrained per-edge contract of its own, on the shape already ratified for 02.05, and decides for itself what to do with it. 06.02.02 would receive one and could drop a suspected edge from its block; 02.01.02's archive filter and domain 10 would each decide separately whether to take one. | The shape is precedented and ratified: DEC-010 / 02.04.04 D-07 established exactly this for the 02.04.04 -> 02.05 boundary, with 'contractVersion', opaque 'attestationEdgeId', 'negativeMultiplier', and literal 'requiresNonTopologicalCorroboration: true'.; Respects ownership boundaries: each consumer domain decides its own gate policy rather than inheriting one silently from domain 02 — which is what 02.04.02's own cross-cut note requires for domain 10.; No third derived value, so 02.04.02 D-06's two-axis lock survives. | Every new contract is another place the detector can leak, which is the reason 02.04 index D-04 keeps detection internal-only. D-07's own design deliberately strips scores, flags and identities precisely because of this.; Commissions one new contract per consumer, and each needs its own ratification, its own corroboration constraint, and its own probe-oracle analysis.; 06.02.02's contract would be the sharpest case: it feeds a minors-facing paid-teaching page, so a topology suspicion the specs say 'cannot be confidently right' would silently affect a teacher's income. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: 1 — Single rung: every gate reads the same detection-invariant rung — For v1, one rung with one meaning. Options 2 and 3 both commission machinery and both reintroduce a probe surface, weaker than option B's but real, on exactly the party the specs say the detector is most likely to be wrong about. The gate that would matter most in practice — 02.08's RIN low-tier exclusion — turns out to be unreachable by demotion under every option, since it fires on rungs 1-2 and demotion cannot reduce a rung-5 or rung-6 credit that far even under option B. The one gate with real money behind it, 06.02.02's rung>=5 credential block, sits behind 06's own D-04 read-only-consumer rule, and the honest reading of that rule is that if the source rung does not move, the block does not either. And domain 10's possible registration floor is not this decision's to make: 02.04.02's cross-cut note and ideation-cx item 20 both put it on 10. Recommending option 1 is therefore a recommendation to leave those gates where their owners put them, not to overwrite them. If the owner finds the corroborated-fiction-in-a-credential-block outcome unacceptable, option 3 is the better of the two alternatives, because its shape is already ratified and it keeps each domain deciding its own gate. *(precedent: 02.04.02 D-06 (ratified two-axis model: exactly one categorical rung and one continuous score) supports option 1 by supporting the absence of a third value. It does NOT support options 2 or 3. Conversely, DEC-010 / 02.04.04 D-07 supports option 3 and not option 1 — it is precedent for per-consumer constrained detection contracts, and it points away from the recommendation here. Both directions are stated so the owner can weigh them rather than inherit one. 02.04.02 DT-04's rejection of 'two encodings that drift apart' is sometimes read as forbidding option 2; on re-reading it does not, because its stated reason is that rung and score are 'two functions over one evidence set', and a gate rung would be a third function over a wider input set. DT-04 makes option 2 uncomfortable, not prohibited.)*

**Affected files**: `02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04.02-provenance-tiers-credit-confidence.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04.04-attestation-ring-collusion-detection.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence-cx.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence/02.04-attestation-credit-confidence-index.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.02-public-discography.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.01-credit-graph-discography/02.01.03-credit-search-graph-traversal.md`, `02-credits-attribution/credits-attribution-cx.md`, `02-credits-attribution/credits-attribution-index.md`, `02-credits-attribution/02.08-credit-export-ddex-rin.md`, `01-identity-profiles-organizations/01.06-portfolio-media-epk/01.06.02-credit-backed-portfolio.md`, `06-education-lessons-mentorship/06.02-teacher-discovery-profiles-trials/06.02.02-verified-credentials-credit-backed-credibility.md`


### DQ-08

> **File**: `07-music-projects-collaboration/music-projects-collaboration-index.md` · **Domain**: `07` · **4 open / 1 locked**

**The DAW bridge (07.09) needs software on the producer's machine, and meta/constraints.md never decided whether WeJammin is allowed to ship any such software — you are deciding four separate things: whether a non-web client is ever authorised, how v1 delivers the capture prompt, whether 07.09 keeps `Should` or becomes `Won't`, and whether v1's public thesis is restated to match what v1 can actually do.**

**Why it blocks**: meta/constraints.md's Project Surfaces table reads `\| Desktop \| — \| — \| Not in scope. No directive. \|` — an absence, not a rule. Domain 07 Q-01, 07.09 index Q-01/Q-02, 07.09.01 Q-01, 07.09.03 Q-02/Q-03, 07.04.01 Q-05 and 07.06.02 Q-09 are all live and all point at this one gap. Until it is closed: (1) /create-prd-stack cannot know whether to design for agent distribution, code signing, notarisation and auto-update (the domain CX Not-Product row routes 'Local Agent Distribution, Signing, Auto-Update & Security Model' to /create-prd, which cannot answer whether the thing exists); (2) /write-be-spec cannot specify where the 07.06.02 Tier 1 contributor card is delivered, and D-09 requires it within 5 s of close, ungated; (3) whoever writes the v1 marketing/positioning copy has a thesis ('present at the moment of creation') that nothing in the v1 build delivers, with no ruling on whether that is allowed to stand. Note what is NOT blocked: no v1 code depends on this, because no 07.09 feature is phased into v1 (see axis 1).

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| 1. v1 build content — does any 07.09 bridge feature ship in v1? | specs/feature-ledger.md rows 273–275 read `\| Should \| [PARTIAL] \| — \| — \| — \| — \| — \| — \|` for 07.09.01/.02/.03, against the file's own header rule (line 5, governed by D-31): "`—` = not phased (phasing applies to Must-haves only; Should/Could/Won't get no release assignment)". Reinforced by ideation-index.md D-28 (User, 2026-07-18): "**Mobile** = native app is **phase 2** (v1 = web + PWA; classification stays `single-surface`, native tracked as a future surface, backend must be API-first)". CONSEQUENCE, and it reframes this whole decision: no bridge feature reaches v1 under ANY answer to the axes below. This decision is about post-v1 authorisation and about what v1 is allowed to claim — it is not about v1 build content. Any option that presents itself as 'protecting v1' is describing a state that already holds. |

#### DQ-08.1 — 2. Is a non-web client on the producer's machine ever authorised — and if so, on what evidence?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does WeJammin authorise software that runs on the producer's own machine (a watch-folder agent, later possibly a DAW plugin) as a post-v1 surface — prohibit it outright — defer it behind a named surface-specific evidence gate — or decline the client and instead commission a server-side ingest path that never touches the producer's machine?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — Authorise: Desktop becomes a tracked future surface, using the exact treatment the table already gives Mobile (native)** | Rewrite the Desktop row of meta/constraints.md Project Surfaces from "Not in scope. No directive." to a named, phased future surface (a local capture agent, post-v1), mirroring the Mobile (native) row's shape: "**Phase 2** (owner-confirmed …) … Tracked as a **future surface**, so v1 classification stays `single-surface`". **Recommendation**: C — Defer behind a named, surface-specific evidence gate — A and B decide the same strategic question in opposite directions on the same missing evidence: domain Q-08 and 07.09.02 Q-03 both state that the track-name premise — the thing that makes a client worth buying — is unverified and should be tested against real sessions. D changes the cost profile genuinely (it removes the signing/notarisation/install costs outright), but it is unspecified anywhere in the tree, keeps the parser cost, kills 07.09.03's ratified delivery surface, and widens the read-scope problem D-06 exists to control — so choosing it should be a deliberate commissioning act, not a default. C is the only option that closes the ambiguity (the Desktop row stops being an absence) without spending a commitment on unevidenced ground, and it costs nothing in v1 because axis 1 already excludes every 07.09 feature from v1. If you would rather not carry an open gate at all, B is the honest second choice — but pick B only together with axis 4 = `Won't` and axis 5 = restate, or you have prohibited the mechanism while still shipping its promise. *(precedent: Shape only, and stated as such: 07.09 D-04 / ideation-index D-37 (Owner, 2026-07-20) gates DAW support on evidence — and the P-01 stage-vocabulary ratification (audits/remediation-state.md r-44[0], owner 2026-07-21) gates enum enforcement on practitioner evidence plus explicit owner approval. Both establish that this project defers commitments behind named evidence rather than guessing. Their CONTENT does not transfer: D-37 resolves "without choosing DAWs, bridge delivery, or a new surface", so its parser gate says nothing about the surface, which is why C's reopen trigger enumerates its own four items.)*

#### DQ-08.4 — 5. Is the v1 thesis wording restated to match what v1 can actually do?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**For the v1 window — in which no 07.09 feature ships under any answer above — is the public claim restated from 'capture at source / present at the moment of creation' to 'capture at the first sharing moment', or does the capture-at-source claim stand?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Restate for the v1 window** | Amend vision.md's thesis wording, meta/problem-statement.md's framing and meta/competitive-landscape.md's capture-vs-reconstruct differentiation so that what v1 claims is what v1 does: capture at the first sharing moment (the review link, 07.05.02) plus the close prompt, with capture-at-source described as the direction, not the current claim. Reversible if axis 2 later reopens and the bridge is built. | It is what domain 07's own law requires. D-06: "**Honest claims over false assurance** — the platform never overclaims what it cannot do", with the reason given: "These users are professionals; an overclaim discovered later is an unrecoverable trust breach."; It answers a question the sources ask directly rather than leaving it hanging: 07.09 Q-02 — "Without a bridge, WeJammin is absent from the DAW and present only at the review link (07.05.02) — which is a real position, but it is 'capture at the *first sharing* moment', not 'capture at source'. Is that enough, and should the thesis be restated honestly if so?"; It is separable from every other axis: it holds under axis 2 = A, B, C or D, because under all four v1 ships without capture-at-source (axis 1). | It touches the project's positioning documents, which is the highest-visibility edit in this decision and the one most likely to have consequences the ideation tree does not model.; If axis 2 = A or C and the bridge later ships, the wording has to change back — two edits instead of none.; Whether the restated claim is still competitively differentiating is a market judgement no source in the tree makes; meta/competitive-landscape.md would need re-reading against the new wording before the edit is finalised. | no |
| **Leave the thesis as written** | No change to vision.md, meta/problem-statement.md or meta/competitive-landscape.md. Capture-at-source remains the stated thesis through the v1 window. | Zero edits, and no risk of restating a position that a later bridge would make true again.; Defensible as an expression of product direction rather than a claim about shipped functionality — if, and only if, the v1 surfaces themselves do not repeat it to users. | It is the state domain D-06 names as the failure: claiming what the product cannot do for the whole v1 window.; It leaves 07.09 Q-02 unanswered, which is one of the questions this finding is supposed to close.; It is riskiest under axis 2 = B, where the mechanism behind the claim has been explicitly prohibited. | no |

**Recommendation**: Restate for the v1 window — Axis 1 establishes that no bridge feature ships in v1 regardless of what you choose elsewhere, so for the entire v1 window the product captures at first sharing. Domain D-06 and 07.09 Q-02 both make that a stated obligation rather than a preference. Restating is cheap now and expensive later — an overclaim discovered by a professional user is what D-06 calls an unrecoverable trust breach. Reverting the wording if the bridge is later authorised is a smaller cost than carrying the overclaim. *(precedent: Domain 07 D-06, which the domain index records as independently reached four times — 07.03.03 D-05, 07.05.02 D-04, 07.06.03 D-01, 07.08.04 D-05 — each time in the direction of narrowing a claim to what the mechanism actually delivers. That is the same direction as this recommendation.)*
