### DQ-09

> **File**: `11-music-licensing/11.04-licensing-policy-preferences/11.04.03-policy-conflict-resolution.md` · **Domain**: `11` · **3 open / 0 locked**

**Two ratified files disagree about what an auto-approve ceiling is a ceiling ON: 11.04.01 says the owner's own share of the deal, 11.04.03 says the lowest owner's number applied to the whole deal — you are deciding which, and separately what the fold shows co-owners and how the 90-day structuring window is denominated.**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot write the auto-approve gate at all. Given a £1,000 request on a work where owner A holds 10% with a £500 ceiling and owner B holds 90% with a £2,000 ceiling, the two ratified texts return opposite verdicts (auto-approve vs fall through to a human), so the gate has no defined behaviour. Downstream: 11.02.01 consumes the fold's output and cannot be typed until it is known whether the fold emits one comparable number or a per-owner verdict; 11.04.01 D-06's trailing 90-day cumulative total cannot be summed with the request value until both are in the same denomination; and the authoring copy ('up to £500 of my share') cannot be written.

#### DQ-09.1 — A. Gate denominator — what value each owner's authored ceiling is compared against  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**When the auto-approve gate tests an owner's authored ceiling, is it tested against that owner's own share of the deal value, or against the deal's total value?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1 — Own share (11.04.01 D-17 stands unamended; 11.04.03's minimum clause is corrected)** | For each owner, the gate compares that owner's share of the deal value against the ceiling that owner authored. Auto-approve of the scope still requires every owner to pass, because 11.04.03 D-02 makes permissions conjunctive — that conjunction is untouched by this axis. In the worked example (£1,000 deal; A holds 10% with £500, B holds 90% with £2,000) A is tested at £100 and B at £900, both pass, and the request auto-approves. 11.04.03's clause 'Thresholds take the minimum — two owners with £500 and £2,000 ceilings yield £500' and its matching edge-case row are rewritten, because ceilings denominated on different shares are not directly comparable quantities. | It is what the owning file says, four times over, and that file is [DEEP] while the contradicting one is [PARTIAL]. 11.04.01 Behavior: the key space includes 'a value threshold on that party's own share of the deal'; again at 'The threshold is on the party's own share. It therefore requires the split to be resolved.'; Happy Path 7: 'This party's threshold checked — the request's share value, plus the trailing 90-day auto-approved total ... against the ceiling'; D-17: 'evaluated on the party's own share value'.; It preserves the policy-vs-veto distinction that 11.04.02 D-02 exists to protect. 11.04.02 Behavior: 'A veto is a constraint over everyone's shares authored by one party, which is what distinguishes it from a policy (11.04.01, which governs only its author's share).' Under A1 each authored number is applied only to its author's own money; the work-level effect arises from conjunction of independent per-share tests, not from one owner's quantity being applied to everyone else's share value.; It keeps the master-vs-publishing economics that 11.04.01 already ratified reachable. Producer Role Lens: 'their thresholds are typically an order of magnitude higher than the publishing side's'; edge case line 109: 'thresholds apply per share — a £500 publishing ceiling is not a £500 master ceiling'. Under A1 the master-side ceiling governs master-side value. (See A2's cons for what happens to it there.); The Musician Role Lens copy — 'up to £500 of my share' — is already written for this reading and needs no change.; Follows P-04/P-05 (author where owned, reference where not): the denominator is an attribute of the policy object, and 11.04.01 owns the policy object (its D-01, 'Policy attaches to a share, never to a work'). 11.04.03 owns the fold, not the unit its inputs are denominated in. | It is the strictly more permissive reading. Because total <= min(ceilings) implies share_i x total <= ceiling_i for every owner, everything A2 auto-approves A1 also auto-approves, and A1 auto-approves more. That enlarges the exposure of parent Q-02 ('Does auto-approve constitute valid consent for a licence the owner never saw?'), which is still open and escalated to /create-prd-security.; It makes threshold force proportional to share size, which is the instinct 11.04.03 DT-02 rejected in its own file: 'The most restrictive owner should not be able to govern a work they own 5% of — there should be a tiebreak' was REJECTED. A 5%-owner's £500 ceiling now clears deals up to £10,000. DT-02's protective core survives (their veto stays absolute and unweighted, and they are still asked when their own share exceeds their ceiling), but the honest reading is that DT-02's disposition points the other way on this axis.; 11.04.01's own worked examples are written without a share fraction and read naturally as deal values — DT-05: 'a buyer wanting £4,500 of use sends ten £450 requests and every one auto-approves. The owner set a £500 ceiling and licensed £4,500 without being asked once', and the edge-case copy 'this request takes them over your £500 ceiling'. They are silent on co-ownership so they do not refute A1, but under A1 that copy must be restated in share terms.; Requires a resolved split for every threshold test, so cold-start and provisional-split works keep falling through. Already ratified in D-17, but A1 is what makes it load-bearing.; Three ratified sentences must be corrected as wrong rather than merely refined (11.04.03 Behavior, its edge-case row, and the sub-domain CX synthesis Q5). | no |
| **A2 — Total deal value (11.04.03's minimum clause stands; 11.04.01 D-17 is amended)** | Each owner's ceiling is compared against the deal's whole declared value; the scope auto-approves only if the total is at or under every ceiling, i.e. at or under min(ceilings). 11.04.01 D-17 is amended from 'evaluated on the party's own share value' to 'evaluated on the deal's total value', both Behavior statements of the per-share base are rewritten, and the Musician Role Lens copy 'up to £500 of my share' is rewritten. In the worked example the £1,000 deal falls through to a human because A's £500 binds. | It is the literal text of 11.04.03 D-02 and its edge case, and it is the reading two other files already assert by citation — 11.04.01's own edge-case row 'Two owners set different auto-approve ceilings \| Lowest wins (11.04.03)' and the sub-domain CX synthesis Q5 'lowest wins, per 11.04.03'.; It is the strictly more restrictive reading, which matches the domain's stated disposition to fail toward slow: 11.04.01 D-04 ('Evaluation failure or budget overrun falls through to a human, never to auto-approve') and 11.04.03 D-05 ('Fold failure fails closed'). It minimises the blast radius if parent Q-02 later lands restrictively.; One number, one named owner — exactly the surface 11.04.03's Populated state and D-04 describe, with no derived arithmetic to explain to a co-owner.; Everything is denominated in the deal's currency, so the D-06 trailing 90-day total needs no per-owner revaluation and axis C collapses to a single shared running total. | It re-values an authored number against money that is mostly not the author's: a 90% owner who typed £2,000 finds a co-owner's £500 governing a deal in which their own stake is £900. Their exposure narrows rather than widens, so this is not the DT-14 harm (see challengeResponses) — but the number they typed no longer means what 11.04.01's Behavior and Role Lens say it means, so both must be rewritten.; It makes the higher master-side ceiling unreachable on any request that touches both rights sides, because min() will always take the publishing-side number that 11.04.01 line 24 says is 'an order of magnitude' lower. That renders the ratified rationale of line 109 ('a £500 publishing ceiling is not a £500 master ceiling') economically inert.; It leaves a further question unanswered that A1 does not raise: whether min() is taken across all owners or per rights side. 11.04.02 D-07 bounds a veto's reach to 'the rights side(s) where its author holds standing'; nothing bounds a threshold. Choosing A2 makes that a required follow-on decision.; It maximises fall-through, and fall-through rate is this feature's own success metric — 11.04.01 Overview: 'Its whole value is measured in requests that never reach a human', and 11.03.01 D-04 makes fall-through rate the shared measure. On a work with several owners the lowest ceiling usually binds.; It puts one party's authored quantity in charge of every other party's auto-approve outcome via a single work-level number, which is the shape 11.04.02 D-02 draws a line around ('a veto is a distinct object from a policy: it constrains all shares, not its author's'). It is not a veto — the deal still routes for human consent rather than being refused — but the fold's threshold output stops being attributable to each owner's own share. | no |
| **A3 — Owner declares the basis per ceiling** | Each authored ceiling carries an explicit 'of my share' / 'of the whole deal' flag. Share-based ceilings test against that owner's share, deal-based ceilings against the total, and auto-approve requires all tests to pass. Neither ratified text has to be declared wrong. | Nobody's number is reinterpreted — the basis is set by the only party with authority over it.; The flag lives on the policy object 11.04.01 already owns; it does not add an axis to the scope grammar (contrast Q-05's buyer allow-list, deferred in DT-11 partly because buyer identity 'is a second key space'). | It commissions machinery no source defines: a new policy field, an authoring step, and fold semantics for mixed bases that nothing in 11.04.03 contemplates.; It fights the feature's stated authoring economics — 11.04.01 Happy Path 1, 'a handful of decisions, not a form', and D-02, which exists because 'nobody will configure 200 works'. It also adds exactly the configuration burden Q-01 already worries about for category granularity.; In mixed cases there is no single figure attributable to one owner, which is the output 11.04.03 D-04 and its Populated state assume.; It exports the ambiguity to every co-owner reading the effective policy, who must now reason in two denominations. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A1 — Own share — Both texts are ratified, so the tie breaks on ownership and load-bearing-ness, not on force of principle. The per-share base is stated four times in the [DEEP] file that owns the policy object (Behavior twice, Happy Path 7, D-17), is the literal product promise in its Role Lens ('up to £500 of my share'), and carries a dependent rule (unresolved splits fall through). The minimum clause appears once in the sub-domain's only [PARTIAL] file, has no Deep Think derivation behind it (11.04.03's DT-01/02/03 concern the fold's product surface, the rejection of tiebreaks, and silence), and its two apparent corroborations are citations back to it rather than independent findings. It is also not well formed on its own terms: min(£500, £2,000) only names a work-level ceiling if both ceilings were denominated on the deal to begin with, which is the very thing in dispute. The independent argument is 11.04.02 D-02/DT-02: the ratified line between a policy and a veto is that 'a policy governs its author's share; a veto constrains everyone's', and A1 is the reading that keeps each authored quantity applied to its author's own share. Note the honest cost, which is not small: A1 is the more permissive reading, it makes threshold force share-proportional in a file whose DT-02 rejected share-weighted tiebreaks, and if parent Q-02 lands restrictively on auto-approve-as-consent, A2 is the safer place to have been standing. Explicitly NOT relied on: DT-14 and DT-03, both of which the previous draft used and neither of which discriminates between A1 and A2 (see challengeResponses). *(precedent: P-04/P-05 (author where owned, reference where not — never a second copy of another domain's requirement), applied narrowly to the denominator only: the unit a ceiling is denominated in is an attribute of the policy object, which 11.04.01 owns via its D-01 ('Policy attaches to a share, never to a work'); 11.04.03 owns the fold and legitimately owns how thresholds combine, but restated a base it does not own, and the restatement drifted. This precedent settles which file's sentence governs the denominator. It does not by itself settle what the fold emits — that is axis B.)*

#### DQ-09.2 — B. What 11.04.03 emits and displays for thresholds  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the fold publish a single work-level ceiling figure to co-owners, and if so is it a literal authored number or a derived one?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **B1 — Derived single figure: the largest deal value that clears every owner, attributed to the binding owner** | The fold computes and displays one number: min over owners of ((their ceiling minus their D-06 window total) divided by their share), naming the owner whose test binds. In the worked example the displayed figure is £2,222 (B's £2,000 over B's 90%), attributed to B. This formula appears in no source file; choosing B1 ratifies it here. | Gives 11.04.03's Populated state and D-04 the single attributed figure they describe, without any owner's authored number being applied to other people's money.; Lets the fold hand 11.02.01 one comparable quantity. 11.02.01's cross-cut note requires it to consume 'the effective work policy (the fold), never the individual share policies. Reading individual policies here would fork the fold', and a single derived figure satisfies that literally.; Consistent with 11.04.03 D-01, 'The aggregate is derived and never editable' — this is a derived figure, not a co-owner's authored one. | It commissions a mechanism no source defines. 11.04.03 D-04 attributes a constraint to an owner; it does not contemplate a divisor-derived quantity, and adopting one silently reopens that attribution guarantee.; The displayed number is one nobody typed and it moves whenever shares move, which is harder copy than a literal figure lifted from a co-owner's policy.; It only stays a single number if the fold absorbs the D-06 trailing window; otherwise the figure is buyer-and-media-specific and there is no one work-level ceiling to display at all. | **yes** |
| **B2 — No synthetic work-level ceiling: an attributed per-owner threshold set, plus a per-request binding-owner answer** | 11.04.03's Populated state shows each owner's threshold with attribution, and for any quoted deal value the fold answers pass/fall-through and names which owner's ceiling binds. No single work-level ceiling number is published. The fold still performs the evaluation, so 11.02.01 continues to consume a verdict from the fold rather than reading individual policies. | Invents nothing. Every element already exists: per-owner thresholds are the fold's inputs, and naming the binding owner is exactly 11.04.03 D-04's 'Owners are told when their own policy is inert, and by whom'.; Keeps the display decision severable from the gate decision — it works unchanged under either A1 or A2.; Survives the D-06 window without contortion, because there is no standing number that has to stay true across buyers and media.; Preserves D-04's attribution in its ratified form: a constraint attributed to an owner, not a derived quantity. | 11.04.03's Populated state currently promises 'Effective policy per scope, with attribution for each constraint' and its edge case promises 'Both see the effective ceiling and whose it is' — a set of thresholds is a weaker answer to 'what is the ceiling' than one number, and that copy must be rewritten.; Co-owner-facing comprehension is worse in the abstract: an owner asking 'what can this work auto-clear?' gets a rule rather than a figure, and must supply a deal value to get an answer.; It puts the burden on the fold's API to expose per-owner threshold data to the co-owner view; that is a display surface, not 11.02.01's gate path, but the boundary must be stated explicitly or it risks reading as the fork 11.02.01's cross-cut note forbids. | no |
| **B3 — Retain the literal minimum of authored ceilings as the displayed figure** | The fold publishes min(ceilings) and names its author, exactly as 11.04.03 Behavior and its edge-case row already say. | Zero change to 11.04.03; the ratified copy stands as written.; Simplest possible co-owner surface — a real number a real person typed, with a name attached. | Only coherent under A2. If axis A resolves to A1, min(£500, £2,000) compares quantities in different denominations and the displayed figure would be arithmetically meaningless and materially misleading (it would understate what the work can auto-clear by the reciprocal of the binding owner's share). | no |

**Recommendation**: B2 if axis A resolves to A1; B3 if axis A resolves to A2 — B2 is the only option that requires no new object and no reopening of a ratified guarantee, and it is the option that stays valid whichever way axis A lands — which is the point of severing the axes. B1's formula is arithmetically correct but is a new display object being introduced inside a gate-semantics decision, and it reopens D-04's attribution in the same way A3 does. Under A2, B3 is free and correct and B2 is unnecessary. *(precedent: 11.04.03 D-04 ('Owners are told when their own policy is inert, and by whom') and D-01 ('The aggregate is derived and never editable'), plus 11.02.01's cross-cut note that the consumer reads the fold and never the individual policies — B2 satisfies all three without adding a quantity none of them defines.)*

#### DQ-09.3 — C. Denomination and share-basis of the D-06 trailing 90-day cumulative window  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Under per-share gating, are an owner's past auto-approved deals valued at the share they held when each was approved, or revalued at their current share?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C0 — Not a free choice: the window's denomination is forced by axis A** | 11.04.01 Happy Path 7 adds the running total to 'the request's share value' and compares the sum to one ceiling, so the window must be denominated the same way the ceiling is. Under A1 the window is per-owner and share-denominated; under A2 it is a single deal-value total. This is arithmetic coherence, not a decision — recorded here so it is not mistaken for one. | States plainly what an implementer would otherwise have to infer.; Under A2 this closes axis C entirely — a shared deal-value total has no per-owner revaluation question. | Under A1 it leaves the real residual question below unanswered. | no |
| **C1 — Historic: each past auto-approval keeps the share value it had when it cleared** | Owner i's 90-day total is the sum of the share values actually auto-approved for them, at the shares in force at each approval. A later split correction or partial transfer does not restate history. | Serves D-06's stated purpose. DT-05's exploit is that 'a buyer wanting £4,500 of use sends ten £450 requests and every one auto-approves' — the window is a record of what actually cleared, and what cleared cleared at the share then in force.; This is where DT-14 genuinely applies, in its correct direction: revaluation at a reduced current share would lower the running total and therefore silently widen the remaining headroom under a ceiling the owner never touched — 'a ceiling that moves without the owner acting is a ceiling that silently widens'.; No dependency on split history reconstruction at gate time. | An owner whose share grew mid-window carries a total computed at their old, smaller stake, so their headroom is proportionally generous for up to 90 days.; Requires the auto-approval record to persist the share value used, not just the deal value. | no |
| **C2 — Current: past auto-approvals are revalued at the owner's share as it stands at request time** | The window total is recomputed from the deal values of the past 90 days multiplied by the owner's current share. | One consistent basis across the ceiling and the whole window at any instant.; Stores only deal values; no per-approval share snapshot needed. | A share reduction silently increases remaining headroom under an untouched ceiling — the widening DT-14 rejects, arriving through a split change instead of an FX move.; Makes the structuring defence weaker exactly when ownership is in flux, which is when D-15's dispute suspension shows the domain is already most cautious.; It restates history: the digest's record of what was approved would no longer match the figures the gate uses. | no |

**Recommendation**: C0 plus C1 (C0 alone if axis A resolves to A2) — The denomination follows axis A by arithmetic necessity and should be recorded as a consequence rather than voted on. The genuinely open part — historic vs current share — is decided by D-06's own purpose (a record of what actually cleared) and by DT-14 read in the direction the source states it: revaluing downward widens headroom nobody authorised. *(precedent: DT-14, used here in its stated direction — 'A ceiling that moves without the owner acting is a ceiling that silently widens' — which is a genuine fit for C2's failure mode and, as the challenge correctly established, not a fit for axis A.)*

### DQ-10

> **File**: `11-music-licensing/music-licensing-index.md` · **Domain**: `11` · **3 open / 3 locked**

**The commercial licence buyer — the music supervisor, brand, agency and monetised creator who drive domain 11's buy-side surfaces — matches none of the four ratified personas, and every buyer-facing feature file says its primary user is therefore unspecified; you are deciding what written artifact describes that buyer, how finely it is segmented, and how wide the ratification reaches.**

**Why it blocks**: A spec writer opening 11.01.02 (Supervisor Search), 11.02.01 (Clearance Verdict), 11.03.02 (Quote Requests) or 11.08.02 (Certificate Issuance) finds a Role Lens whose four rows are all seller-side or spectator rows, plus a note stating the real primary user has no persona. 11.01.02 Q-01 records this as "Blocking for this sub-domain" and 11.02.01's Role Lens says the buyer-facing rendering "cannot be specified until Q-01 is answered". With nothing to consume, /write-fe-spec for the buy side either stalls or invents a user. Note the ratified release scope: D-31 places all of domain 11 in phase 2+, so the FE block is a phase-2 block, not a v1 one — the urgency is that D-19 and personas.md are consumed by all 24 domain Role Matrices and by /create-prd, so the persona set gets harder to touch the further the pipeline runs.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| A4 — Where buyer identity and authority live (party model, org type) | 11-music-licensing/music-licensing-cx.md (CX-18 and the shared-state note): "buyer blocks cannot fold (CX-18) — they resolve against a buyer identity (01) at request time" and "**Buyer blocks** (11.04.02) — key on party identity (01); default *empty*; **not** foldable (CX-16, CX-18)". The org-type half is locked by 01.02-organizations-entity-model/01.02.01-organization-types-attributes.md D-13: "The type set may be empty. A party with a name and members and no type is a **collective** ... nothing requires the set to stay non-empty", together with the file's rule that "Type is a capability declaration, not an attribute bundle". Authority is locked to the ratified `Roles, Permissions & Delegated Authority` cross-cut (ideation-cx.md): "Entity-scoped RBAC plus delegated authority/representation ... delegation explicit, scoped, time-bound, revocable, audit-logged". |
| A5 — Whether the buyer is one party or two (purchaser vs licensee) | 11-music-licensing/11.08-licence-instrument-lifecycle/11.08.02-licence-certificate-issuance.md D-11: "**Licensee ≠ purchaser.** The instrument names both; the purchaser warrants authority to bind the licensee", per DT-04: "an ad agency purchases on behalf of a brand; the party who must be licensed is the brand ... So the instrument names purchaser **and** licensee, separately (D-11)." |
| A6 — Release phase of the buy-side surfaces | ideation-index.md D-31: "**Release split into v1 + v1.5** ... **v1 = session spine** (01, 02, 05, 07, 09-capture) ~45 Musts ... **v1.5 = the 3 marketplaces** (13, 14, 15) ~26 Musts ... Phase 2+ = the remaining ~124 Musts." Domain 11 is in neither v1 nor v1.5. |

#### DQ-10.1 — A1 — What written artifact describes the commercial licence buyer (and does D-19's persona count change)?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Do we mint a fifth persona for the commercial licence buyer, author a six-field buyer profile that is explicitly NOT a persona, author nothing and let each feature describe its own buyer, or defer the question again?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1-a — Fifth persona: amend D-19 and add a Buyer column to every Role Matrix** | Author a fifth persona in meta/personas.md with the same six fields as the existing four (Name+Role, Primary Pain Point, Current Workaround, Success Criteria, Switching Trigger, Unique Constraint) plus Workflow and Anti-Persona Behavior, covering the commercial buyer of licences. Amend D-19 in ideation-index.md from "4 primary personas" to five. The instrument names both; the purchaser warrants authority to bind the licensee") is a two-party fact a single persona column cannot express, so D-11 stays load-bearing regardless.; It creates pressure to mint further personas for the other non-persona actors on disk (curator, dealer, local stagehand, Admin, insurer, accountant, manager, function-hire buyer, fee-paying parent) — several of which existing decisions resolved specifically by NOT minting one (06.10 D-01: "An academy is an Operator, not a fifth persona"; 03.06 D-05 stewards; 23.02.03 DT-03 accountant). | no |

The buyer-facing Role Lens notes now in 11.01.02, 11.02.01, 11.01-sync-licensing-index, 11.06 and 11.08.02 change from "this feature's actual primary user has no persona" to a reference to that profile. Identity and authority are not restated there — they come from the locked A4 answer below. This introduces a new document type under meta/ (an authoring convention), not a new system mechanism. | It supplies the exact thing the blocked files ask for. 11.01.02 Q-01 says the problem is that "this feature's primary user is unspecified, and /write-fe-spec will have no Role Lens to consume" — a six-field profile referenced from the Role Lens resolves that without touching D-19.; It matches the tree's established handling of actors who are real users but not personas: personas.md Q-02 and 02.06 D-20 keep vocabulary-curation authority with "an admin/governance role that is not a persona", and 23.02.03 DT-03 records the accountant as "a user of WeJammin who is not one of the four personas". Naming a non-persona actor is already normal here; what has never been done is writing that actor's product detail down, which is precisely the gap.; Zero-edit on the 24 Role Matrices and on D-19, so the '4 personas, coverage verified across all 24 domains' invariant survives intact.; Follows the author-where-owned rule (D-50 / P-04): the buyer description is authored once, in meta/ where actor definitions live, and referenced — never restated — by each 11.x Role Lens. That is the correct direction of that rule here, since meta/ owns actor definitions and domain 11 consumes them. | It introduces a second class of actor document, so a future author must know to look in two places; without a pointer from personas.md the profile is easy to miss.; It does not satisfy the argument recorded in 06 D-07 that "the fee-paying parent is a BUYER, and buyers are personas" — an owner who agrees with that sentence should pick A1-a instead. (See the honesty note in the recommendation: D-07 states that sentence and then declines to resolve it.); Role Matrix tables stay four-column, so a reader skimming only the table still sees no buyer; the correction depends on the prose note beneath it being read. | no |
| **A1-c — No product artifact: buyer behaviour authored feature by feature** | Rule that no buyer lens document is written at any level. Each buyer-facing feature (11.01.02, 11.01.03, 11.03.01, 11.03.02, 11.02.01, 11.06.01, 11.08.02) describes its own buyer inline in its Behavior and edge-case sections, drawing identity and authority from the locked A4 answer. personas.md gains a note that commercial buyers are deliberately outside the persona set, mirroring how Q-02 treats Admin. | Cheapest to write and changes no ratified decision.; Some buyer detail is already feature-local and correct where it sits — 11.08.02's checkout sequence and its agency-buys-brand-is-licensed edge case do not need a persona document to be complete. | This is the option the tree has already run, and disk shows the result: 24.01.03 Q-01 says of the Admin actor treated exactly this way that "The actor's identity, permission model, and console endpoints cannot be specified until this resolves." Leaving an actor outside the set without describing it has demonstrably not unblocked the specs that need it.; 11.06's index names the concrete failure mode this produces: with no shared buyer description "a spec writer will read 'Fan: Full' and build for a music enthusiast instead of a small-business operator."; Seven features would each invent their own buyer independently, which is how the strained 11.06 Fan mapping arose in the first place. | no |
| **A1-d — Defer the question again to a named later stage** | Leave 11 Q-01, 11.06 Q-01 and 11.01.02 Q-01 open, re-owned from /ideate-validate (which has already run) to a specific later stage, and mark the buyer-facing features as not specifiable until then. | Costs nothing now and D-31 already places all of domain 11 in phase 2+, so no v1 or v1.5 Must depends on the answer.; Domain 11's own Q-04 questions whether the sync buy side is even the domain's beachhead, so buyer detail written now may be written for the wrong buyer. | This is the option that already failed once. Deferring to another stage repeats the mechanism that produced this entry.; Two MUST features are buyer-facing and would be left describing a user nobody has defined: 11.02.01 (MUST) whose Role Lens says buyer-facing rendering "cannot be specified until Q-01 is answered", and 11.08.02 (MUST) whose happy path opens "Buyer commits at checkout".; D-19 and personas.md get consumed by /create-prd and every downstream layer in the meantime, so the cost of any later change rises monotonically. | no |

**Recommendation**: A1-b — Buyer counterparty profile in meta/, explicitly not a persona — The blocked files ask for one specific thing — a described primary user for the Role Lens to consume — and A1-b delivers exactly that at the smallest edit surface, leaving D-19 and 24 Role Matrices untouched. A1-d is the mechanism that already failed at /ideate-validate on 2026-07-18. A1-a is defensible and the owner may prefer it — it is the only option that puts the buyer in the table rather than in a note — but it costs a full 24-domain column pass and creates precedent pressure toward personas for eight other non-persona actors that existing decisions resolved by refusing exactly that. Honesty note on the strongest-sounding argument for A1-a: 06 D-07's headline does read "A persona gap is escalated: the fee-paying parent is a BUYER, and buyers are personas", but the same row ends "**Not resolved here** — D-19 is ratified and the owner chose 4 over 5 deliberately", and 06 Q-01 carries it forward as still open, owner = User. It is an agent-authored escalation stating a principle, not an owner ratification of it, and it should be weighed as an argument rather than cited as settled law. *(precedent: personas.md Q-02 with 02.06 D-20 and 05.01.02 Q-02 — an actor that is genuinely needed can sit outside the persona set by decision rather than by omission ('an admin/governance role that is not a persona'). And D-50 / P-04, author-where-owned: the buyer description is authored once in meta/, which owns actor definitions, and referenced by each 11.x Role Lens rather than restated in seven files. 23.02.03 DT-03 is cited only for the narrower point that non-persona users of WeJammin are an accepted category — its delegation mechanism does not transfer, because a licence buyer acts for themselves and is not delegated authority by any platform user.)*

| **A2-b — Two artifacts: professional licence buyer, and creator micro-licence buyer** | Two six-field descriptions. The professional buyer (supervisor / brand / agency) is referenced by 11.01, 11.02, 11.03 and 11.08; the creator buyer (podcaster, streamer, small-business channel) is referenced by 11.06 and by 11.02.01's Fan row, which currently describes that population as 'silently governed'. | Matches what the sources actually say. 11.06's index: the creator buyer 'is running a **business** that happens not to be a music business. They have commercial intent, a monetised channel, and a real dispute appetite — none of which personas.md's Fan has' — a description that does not fit a brand's agency either.; Fixes the specific mis-build 11.06 predicts, where 'Fan: Full' leads a spec writer to build for a music enthusiast instead of a small-business operator, because the Role Lens now points at a described small-business operator.; Keeps each artifact specific enough to survive the rubric's Persona Specificity test. | Two documents to keep consistent, and they overlap on the parts of the buy side that are genuinely common (checkout, verdict rendering, the licence instrument).; It leaves an unstated boundary case — a small production company that is neither an agency nor a creator — that a single broader artifact would have absorbed. | no |

**Recommendation**: A2-b — Two artifacts — The 11.06 index does not merely note a difference of degree; it records the two gaps as different in kind and names the concrete downstream harm of blurring them. Writing one artifact broad enough to cover both would reproduce the Fan mapping's defect one level up. If the owner picks A1-a instead, the same reasoning argues for two personas rather than one — which is a further cost of A1-a worth weighing before choosing it.

#### DQ-10.3 — A3 — Scope of the ratification: the licence buyer only, or a general rule for every non-persona actor?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does this decision resolve the commercial licence buyer alone, or is it ratified as the general answer to 'does the 4-persona model cover non-musician users?' and applied to all the other non-persona actors on disk?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A3-a — Narrow: licence/commercial buyer only, with an explicit non-closure list** | The decision is labelled as covering the commercial licence buyer. It closes 11 Q-01, 11.06 Q-01 and 11.01.02 Q-01, and states in terms which questions it does NOT close: personas.md Q-01 (professional dealer, 13/14/15 — a seller, not a buyer), personas.md Q-02 with 24.01.03 Q-01 (Admin), 21.02 Q-01 with 21 D-03 (curator / journalist / radio / DSP gatekeeper), 13.09 D-03 with 13.13 (dealer counterparty; 'Operator ... poor fit'), 18.08.01 DT-04 with 18 Q-03 (local stagehand), 15.05 (insurer, off-platform), 01.01.03 Q-04 (manager/agent with 10–20 contexts), 04.01.01 Q-01 (function/private-hire buyer), 06 D-07 / Q-01 (fee-paying parent). | Accurate to the evidence: those actors are heterogeneous. A curator receives pitches, a stagehand sells four hours of labour, an insurer is off-platform, Admin is internal — none of them is a buyer of licences, so a buyer artifact answers none of them.; Each of those questions is owned in another domain's index; a domain-11 ratification can make them answerable but cannot close them without a propagation pass, and claiming closure would leave nine indexes carrying answers nobody wrote. | 11 Q-01's own text bundles itself with personas.md Q-01 and 11.06 Q-01 — 'all three are one question' — so a narrow ratification explicitly declines part of what that row asks, and personas.md Q-01 (the dealer) stays open.; Nine open actor questions remain, several of which are independently blocking their own surfaces (24.01.03 Q-01 in particular). | no |
| **A3-b — Framework: ratify a general rule for all non-persona actors and apply it everywhere at once** | Decide the general question D-19's coverage check left open and apply the chosen form (persona / non-persona profile / nothing) uniformly to buyer, dealer, curator, gatekeeper, stagehand, insurer, accountant, manager, Admin and the fee-paying parent in one pass. | Answers 11 Q-01 exactly as that row frames it, and would close the largest number of open questions in a single decision.; Prevents the same argument being re-run per domain, which is what has happened four separate times already. | It is a second axis collapse: applying one form to ten heterogeneous actors is the defect this entry was rewritten to remove. Several of them already have contrary domain rulings — 06.10 D-01 ('An academy is an Operator, not a fifth persona'), 03.06 D-05 (stewards), 01 Q-12 (the dealer 'reads as a Musician in a selling context') — and a uniform rule would overwrite them without re-reading their evidence.; It requires reading and reconciling material across at least nine domains before the owner can answer, which is far more than this decision needs in order to unblock domain 11. | no |

**Recommendation**: A3-a — Narrow, with an explicit non-closure list — The nine other actors are not buyers and are not alike; a buyer artifact is not their answer, and several already have domain rulings pointing the opposite way. Writing the non-closure list into the decision is what stops a downstream reader treating this as 'the persona-gap decision' and assuming the dealer, the curator and Admin were handled. *(precedent: D-61 (DQ-MG-01), whose recorded problem was that a queue entry 'collapsed four independently-locked axes into one scalar' and which was resolved by deciding each axis separately rather than by one uniform ruling.)*
