### DQ-11

> **File**: `12-release-distribution/12.02-ddex-delivery-messaging/12.02.02-per-partner-profile-conformance.md` · **Domain**: `12` · **5 open / 3 locked**

**Two ratified specs define the partner expected-response window differently; the owner must settle five open sub-questions about it (precedence, alarm statistic, copy statistic, absolute escalation ceiling, resolution key) — three further axes people have been arguing about are already locked and are removed from the question.**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot compute the expected-response window at all. 12.02.02 D-12 says contract-stated first, else P90 of the last 20 acks once >=5 acks are seen, else no norm, with an unconditional platform escalation at 10 business days. 12.02.03 D-05 says observed p95 over >=30 observations in 180 days first, then contractual, then a seeded 5 partner-business-days, with copy from p50 at N>=10. 12.03.02 D-11 says an expectation needs >=20 of our own deliveries and uses a median, keyed 'at this state'. Each of the first two claims to have closed the other's question (12.02.02 DT-14: '(Answers 12.02.03 Q-01 from this side ...)'; 12.02.03 D-05: 'Resolves prior Q-01') and neither cites the other. Until this is settled, nobody can write the code that fires Overdue, the escalation ladder that chases a partner, the artist-facing 'usually N days' clause, or the analytics job that accumulates the observation history — and release-distribution-cx.md CX-13 point 4 has already propagated one of the two formulations ('no ack in PC-10 P90') a level up, so the divergence is spreading.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| A1 — Author of record: which file writes the window's definition, and which files reference it | 12.02-ddex-delivery-messaging-cx.md CX-02 detail: 'Choreography has no transport logic of its own — it executes what the profile describes: which transport, in what order, with what naming, what an ack means, and how long a response should take. The last is the interesting one: the expected-response window that makes `overdue` a real state (12.02.03 D-01) is per-partner profile data, not a global timeout.' Corroborated three more times: release-distribution-cx.md CX-13 synthesis point 1 — 'The partner-knowledge store (CX-02) owns lead times, response windows (PC-09/PC-10) and date semantics; the schedule reads them'; 12.02.02 PC-10 defines the slot — 'Which ack types exist, what each maps to in the normalised vocabulary ... and the expected-response window per stage'; and 12.02.03's own Behavior preamble concedes it — 'Choreography is per-partner and per-profile, and it has no transport logic of its own (CX-02): it executes what the pinned profile (12.02.02) describes — which transport, in what order, with what naming, what an ack means, and how long a response should take.' 12.02.03's own cross-cut note adds 'response windows and partner non-working days (DT-13) are all profile data, pinned per delivery. The observation history that makes a window `measured` accumulates against the profile (CX-04).' DEC-028 (2026-07-21, 'A handoff spec is authored where owned and referenced where not') keys on ownership of the OBJECT — its worked example authors mastering/mix in 07.08.01 because 07.08.01 owns them and references domain 12 for the DSP destination spec because domain 12 owns it. Applied here it authors the window in 12.02.02 PC-10. CAVEAT the owner should see: the four statements above lock ownership of the window VALUE and its observation history; no ratified line says in so many words that the derivation ALGORITHM is authored in 12.02.02. It follows from DEC-028 plus 12.02.03's own preamble, and the whole point of DEC-028's rationale (generalising 07.07.03 D-04: 'two copies of third-party requirements would drift, and one would be wrong') is that exactly one file states it. Whichever mechanics win on A2-A7, they are written down once, in 12.02.02 PC-10; 12.02.03 D-05 and 12.03.02 D-11 become references. This is a file-placement consequence, not a substantive choice, and it does not favour either file's numbers. |
| A5 — Counting clock for the window | 12.02.03 D-07: 'Windows count partner business days in the partner's timezone; artist-facing elapsed time is wall-clock' — context: 'An elapsed-hours window cries wolf every weekend — on the domain's flagship honesty feature, at peak load, because Friday is global release day (DT-13).' The underlying calendar is profile data per 12.02.03's cross-cut note ('response windows and partner non-working days (DT-13) are all profile data, pinned per delivery'), which is consistent with the A1 lock. This is not in dispute: 12.02.02 does not contradict it — its '10 business days' simply names no time base at all, which is an omission rather than a rival rule. Consequence for the owner: every number settled on A3, A4 and A6 is denominated in partner business days in the partner's timezone, and the 10-business-day figure in 12.02.02 must be restated on that clock when it moves into PC-10. |
| A8 — What an unevidenced window may drive: internal chase yes, artist-facing claim no | All three files already agree, so this is not a choice. 12.02.02 D-12: 'Expected-response windows carry evidence; absence yields no norm. Internal escalation uses a fallback (10 business days); artist-facing claims never do' — and its table: 'The fallback exists to wake an operator, never to make an artist-facing claim.' 12.02.03 D-05: 'no expectation claimed when unmeasured'; its ladder row states 'Automated chase via the partner's stated support channel, quoting the message reference. `Overdue` is surfaced only where the window is observed or contractual; a seeded cold-start window remains internal.' 12.03.02 D-11: 'On cold start: elapsed time only, no `. overdue` flag, and we say we don't know yet' — its table: 'Neither — cold start \| "Received by Apple 6 days ago. We don't have enough history with Apple to say whether that's normal yet." Elapsed time only. No `overdue` flag is raised, because there is nothing to be over.' This is also the domain's ratified 'Never claim more than you know' pattern in release-distribution-index.md. NOTE for the owner: this axis was previously presented as an option to choose ('formalise the internal/artist-facing split'). It is not open. It is already ratified in all three files, and the 2026-07-22 pass (f5f6d17) is what aligned 12.02.03's ladder and cold-start edge case to 12.03.02 D-11. The only real work here is that PC-10 must record two values (the escalation-window and the evidenced expectation) plus its evidence marking, which is a field-shape edit to 12.02.02, not a policy decision. |

#### DQ-11.1 — A2 — Precedence: when a partner's stated SLA and our own observations disagree, which one is the window?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the observed window outrank the partner's contractually stated window, or does the stated window outrank observation?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A2-i — Observed outranks contractual (12.02.03 D-05's ordering)** | Where enough observations exist the window is the observed value, marked `measured`. A partner's stated SLA is used only where observation is insufficient, marked `stated`. A partner who is inside our observed window but outside their own written SLA is not late by the window; per 12.02.03 D-05 that divergence is 'recorded, routed to partner management, and never surfaced to the artist, whose board should say "this is unusual for Apple", not "Apple is in breach of a contract you are not party to"'. | The window's stated job is detecting abnormality, and 12.02.03 D-05 argues normal is what a partner does rather than what they said.; 12.02.02's own file supplies the evidence for this direction twice over: DT-07 — 'A profile certified in March is wrong in July and nothing tells us — except an artist's rejection'; and DT-15 — 'DDEX standardises the message; it does not publish the dialect ... the divergences are each partner's private implementation choices, are often undocumented even internally, and change without announcement.' Partner documentation is, by 12.02.02's own account, stale and thin.; The commercial-signal routing for the divergent case is already written down in 12.02.03 D-05, so choosing this does not leave the contractual comparison undefined — it relocates it out of the artist's view. | The contractual comparison becomes a second path someone has to build and route to partner management; it is described in 12.02.03 D-05 but has no owner, no surface and no threshold anywhere.; For a partner with a published SLA and few deliveries, we ignore a real number we already have in favour of nothing until the sample floor is met (interacts with A3). | no |
| **A2-ii — Contractual outranks observed (12.02.02 D-12 / DT-14's ordering)** | Where the partner's contract or spec states a window, that value is the window. Observation is used only where no stated window exists. 12.02.02 DT-14: 'Contract-stated => use it. Else observed P90 of the last 20 acks with >=5 samples => use it. Else no norm exists'. | A written SLA is the partner's own commitment and the least arbitrary number available; it exists from day one, before any observations, which matters because 12.02.02 DT-02 identifies cold start as this domain's largest deficit.; It avoids the odd result of the platform treating a partner as normal while they are in breach of what they signed.; It reads consistently with 12.03.02 D-11's attribution rule, which surfaces a partner-published window as the partner's claim: 'A window published or contracted by the partner, carried in their profile (12.02.02) \| "Received by Apple 6 days ago — Apple's stated window is 5 business days." Attributed to them, not to us.' | A stated SLA a partner routinely beats produces a window far too loose to detect abnormality; a stated SLA a partner routinely misses produces a permanently-firing alarm.; 12.02.02 never justifies this ordering against its own DT-07/DT-15 findings on documentation staleness. | no |
| **A2-iii — Split by purpose: observed drives the alarm, stated drives artist-facing attribution** | The alarm/escalation window is the observed value where measured. Where a partner has published or contracted a window, it is additionally shown to the artist attributed to the partner, exactly as 12.03.02 D-11 already writes it. The two coexist rather than one overriding the other. | It is the only option under which no ratified line has to be argued away: 12.03.02 D-11 already renders a partner-published window as an attributed statement rather than as our own claim.; It removes the need for the unowned 'route contractual divergence to partner management' path in A2-i, because the stated number is already on screen, attributed. | Two numbers can appear on one row ('usually 2 days' from observation; 'Apple's stated window is 5 business days'), which is more copy than any source currently specifies for one status line.; It leaves undefined what happens when only a stated window exists and the alarm therefore has nothing measured to fire on — it still needs A2-i or A2-ii's ordering as a fallback, so it is a partial answer. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A2-i — Observed outranks contractual — The direction is supported from inside the file that argues the other way: 12.02.02 DT-07 and DT-15 both say partner documentation is stale, thin and changes without announcement, which is precisely the argument against treating a stated SLA as authoritative. 12.02.03 D-05 also already specifies what to do with the divergent case rather than dropping it. Note explicitly what does NOT support this: 12.02.02's contested-entry rule ('the observed behaviour takes effect after human review, the documented value is retained alongside it') is only an analogy here, not an argument — it fires on '2 independent rejections on 2 distinct releases', a falsification EVENT, and a timing divergence produces no rejection, so the rule does not literally reach the window. Anyone citing it as if it decided this axis is overreaching.

#### DQ-11.2 — A3 — Alarm statistic, sample floor and recency bound for the evidenced window  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**What statistic, over how many observations, within what recency bound, produces the window that fires the chase and escalation ladder?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A3-i — p95 over >=30 observations in the last 180 days (12.02.03 D-05)** | 12.02.03 line 121: '>=30 completed `signal-complete -> first terminal ack` observations for that (partner x message type) within the last 180 days. Window = observed p95, rounded up to the next whole partner-business-day, floor 1 business day', marked `measured`. Its own justification table gives 'p95 minimum sample \| N >= 30 \| The 95th-percentile order statistic does not meaningfully exist below N=20; ~30 makes it non-absurd' (Reasoned) and 'Observation half-life \| 180 days \| Partners change behaviour without notice (12.02.02); older observations describe a partner that no longer exists' (marked 'Tunable guess'). | It is complete: statistic, floor, recency bound, rounding rule and floor value are all stated, so an implementer needs nothing further.; The 180-day bound directly answers 12.02.02's own DT-07 concern that a partner's March behaviour does not describe them in July.; The rounding rule and 1-business-day floor prevent a sub-day window on a fast partner, which nothing else in the cluster addresses. | N>=30 within 180 days is a high floor. For a long-tail direct partner it may never be met, leaving that partner permanently `unmeasured` — which, combined with the locked A8 rule, means no artist-visible expectation and no artist-visible Overdue for that partner indefinitely. Honest, but permanently quiet on what 12.02.03 calls the domain's most-viewed screen.; The 180-day half-life is flagged in its own source as a 'Tunable guess', so choosing this ratifies one guessed number. | no |
| **A3-ii — P90 over the last 20 acks once >=5 acks are seen (12.02.02 D-12 / DT-14)** | 12.02.02 line 114: 'No stated window, >=5 acks observed at that stage \| Observed P90 of the last 20 acks'. Line 115: 'No stated window, <5 acks observed \| No norm exists'. No recency bound, no rounding rule, no floor value. | A floor of 5 means a norm exists far earlier in the cold start — the period 12.02.02 DT-02 and 12.03.02 DT-12 both identify as when the board can say least and trust is being set.; Fewer partners are ever stuck permanently unmeasured. | The statistic is degenerate at its own floor. Under nearest-rank, the P90 of 5 samples is the 5th order statistic — the sample maximum — so at N=5 the window is 'the slowest response we have ever seen from them' and the alarm cannot fire during exactly the cold-start window it was lowered to serve.; 'The last 20' has no recency bound, which contradicts 12.02.02's own DT-07 ('A profile certified in March is wrong in July and nothing tells us').; It specifies no rounding rule and no minimum window, so a fast partner can be assigned a sub-day window with no stated floor. | no |
| **A3-iii — p95 with the floor lowered to N>=20, keeping the 180-day bound, rounding and 1-day floor** | Everything in A3-i except the sample floor, set to 20. This is sourced from 12.02.03's own threshold justification ('does not meaningfully exist below N=20') and happens to equal the number 12.03.02 D-11 already uses ('>=20 of our own completed deliveries to this partner at this state'), so all three files land on one figure. | Reaches `measured` sooner than A3-i, easing its permanent-silence con for long-tail partners.; Harmonises the sample floor across all three files at one number, which removes the divergence outright rather than overruling it.; Still keeps the recency bound, rounding rule and floor that A3-ii lacks entirely. | 20 is the point 12.02.03 calls the bare minimum at which a p95 'meaningfully exists'; its own text says '~30 makes it non-absurd'. Choosing 20 knowingly sets the alarm statistic at the edge of meaningfulness.; The number matched from 12.03.02 D-11 is a floor for a MEDIAN, not for a p95 — the two statistics do not need the same sample size, so the harmony is cosmetic rather than statistical. | no |

**Recommendation**: A3-i — p95 over >=30 observations in 180 days, with rounding and the 1-business-day floor — A3-ii is not buildable as written: its statistic is the sample maximum at its own floor, it has no recency bound in a file whose DT-07 says staleness is the central hazard, and it specifies neither rounding nor a minimum. A3-i is the only formulation in the cluster that an implementer can code without inventing a rule. A3-iii is a legitimate owner call if the permanent-silence con of A3-i is judged worse than a weaker statistic — but note the harmony it buys with 12.03.02's 20 is cosmetic, because that 20 is a floor for a median. Whichever is chosen, one number must be written once in 12.02.02 PC-10 per the locked A1, or this defect recurs.

#### DQ-11.3 — A4 — Artist-facing expectation copy: statistic and sample floor for the 'usually N days' clause  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**What statistic, over how many observations, backs the artist-facing 'usually N days' clause — and is that number the same one that fires the alarm?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A4-i — A separate copy statistic: p50 at N>=10 (12.02.03 D-05)** | 12.02.03's threshold table: 'p50 minimum sample \| N >= 10 \| Median is stable at small N. Below this, no expectation clause is shown at all.' The copy number is therefore distinct from the alarm number and reaches evidence sooner. | A median is what 'usually' means in plain language; a p95 is not, and would read as an inflated promise.; A lower floor than the alarm statistic is statistically justified in-source ('Median is stable at small N'), so the copy can appear before the alarm is armed.; Keeps the alarm and the sentence decoupled, so tuning one does not silently change the other. | It overrides 12.03.02 D-11's stated floor of 20 for the same sentence, so that file must be amended.; Two thresholds per partner is more state than 12.02.02 currently models — PC-10 today holds one window value per stage, not a value pair. | no |
| **A4-ii — Median at N>=20 (12.03.02 D-11)** | 12.03.02 line 123: '>=20 of our own completed deliveries to this partner at this state \| "Received by Apple 6 days ago — usually 2." Median of our own observations.' Same statistic as A4-i, higher floor, and keyed per board state rather than per message type. | It is the file that owns the screen the sentence appears on, and it already carries the cold-start copy ('We don't have enough history with Apple to say whether that's normal yet'). | 12.03.02's own note undercuts the number: 'The 20-delivery threshold is a default, not a law — a median from fewer observations is noise ... The exact number is tunable; the rule that an expectation requires evidence is not.' The file itself does not defend 20 specifically.; It delays the honest 'usually N days' sentence for no stated statistical reason, on a screen 12.03.02 D-03 says is 'the entire product'. | no |
| **A4-iii — One number for both: the alarm window is also the copy number** | Whatever A3 settles is both the value that fires the ladder and the value quoted to the artist. No second statistic and no second floor. | Simplest possible model — one value in PC-10, one threshold, nothing to keep in sync.; Removes any possibility of the board saying 'usually 2 days' while the alarm is set at 9. | Under A3-i or A3-iii the quoted number becomes a p95, so 'usually' would report a near-worst-case time and systematically overstate how long a partner takes. Under A3-ii it becomes a P90, with the same defect.; It couples the two thresholds so a tuning change to the alarm silently rewrites artist-facing copy. | no |

**Recommendation**: A4-i — p50 at N>=10, distinct from the alarm statistic — A4-iii is the one option that produces an actually wrong sentence: quoting a p95 or P90 after the word 'usually' misdescribes the partner. Between A4-i and A4-ii the statistic is identical (median) and only the floor differs, and 12.03.02's own text calls its 20 'a default, not a law' whose 'exact number is tunable' — so adopting 10 overrides nothing that file defends. This axis is cheap to settle and settling it removes one of the three competing sample floors outright.

#### DQ-11.4 — A6 — The unconditional escalation ceiling: keep it or drop it  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does an absolute internal escalation fire at a fixed platform-wide elapsed threshold regardless of the partner's window, or does internal escalation fire only off the tiered window?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A6-i — Keep an unconditional ceiling alongside the tiered window** | 12.02.02 line 116 is preserved as its own rule, restated on the locked A5 clock: 'Any of the above \| Internal escalation fires at a platform-wide fallback of 10 business days regardless \| Nothing. The fallback exists to wake an operator, never to make an artist-facing claim.' The word 'regardless' is load-bearing — the row's source column is 'Any of the above', so it fires even when a contractual or observed window exists and has not yet been exceeded. It sits alongside, not inside, 12.02.03's tier stack, whose tier 3 seeded value applies only when 'Neither exists' (line 123). Result: three distinct values — a tiered window, a tier-3 seed for the no-evidence case, and an absolute ceiling. | It is the only rule in the cluster that bounds operator blindness independent of the window. Without it, a partner whose observed p95 is 45 days generates no operator escalation for 45 days.; It costs nothing artist-facing: 12.02.02 D-12 already scopes it internal-only ('Internal escalation uses a fallback (10 business days); artist-facing claims never do'), which is consistent with the locked A8 rule.; It is the safety property that a straight 'adopt 12.02.03 D-05 wholesale' reading silently deletes, because 12.02.03's seeded 5 partner-business-days is a tier-3 substitute value, not a ceiling. | A fixed ceiling below a legitimately slow partner's normal window generates recurring known-benign operator alerts — precisely the cry-wolf failure 12.02.03 DT-13 warns about, relocated from the artist to the operator.; The 10-business-day figure has no evidence behind it and 12.02.02 Q-05 lists it among the numbers deferred to /create-prd, so keeping it ratifies a guess. | no |
| **A6-ii — Drop the ceiling; escalation fires only off the tiered window** | 12.02.02's line-116 row is deleted. All internal escalation derives from the window, including the tier-3 seeded value where nothing is known, driven by 12.02.03's ladder ('Window exceeded (1x)' -> automated chase; '2x window' -> named human contact). | One mechanism instead of two; every escalation is explainable by reference to a single number.; No known-benign alerts for genuinely slow partners, because escalation is always relative to that partner's normal. | Deletes a stated safety property. For a partner with a very slow measured window, no operator is woken until 1x that window has elapsed.; 12.02.02 D-12's core claim — 'internal escalation needs a number; artist-facing claims need evidence' — is what this row implements; dropping it without argument removes the only always-defined number in the design. | no |
| **A6-iii — Keep a ceiling but make it relative rather than fixed** | Replace 'a platform-wide 10 business days' with a bound expressed against the window (for example, escalation at min(2x window, some absolute cap)). No source defines this shape; both existing sources state a fixed number or none. | Would scale with partner behaviour instead of firing on known-slow partners.; Fits 12.02.03's existing ladder, which already has a 2x-window rung. | No source defines it. Choosing it commissions a new rule that must be written from scratch, and the cap value is still an unevidenced number.; It reintroduces the same defect for a partner with no evidence at all, where there is no window to be relative to. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A6-i — Keep the unconditional ceiling, restated in partner business days per the locked A5 — The ceiling and the tier-3 seed answer different questions and only look like rivals: 'regardless' makes 12.02.02's row an always-on backstop, while 12.02.03's 5 partner-business-days applies only when 'Neither exists'. Keeping both preserves a real safety property at no artist-facing cost, and it is the part of 12.02.02 D-12 that survives A2 and A3 going the other way. Its numeric value is a guess by its own file's admission (12.02.02 Q-05), so it should be recorded as tunable alongside 12.02.03 Q-03's seeded 5 rather than ratified as final.

#### DQ-11.5 — A7 — Resolution key: what the window is computed per  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is the expected-response window held per (partner x message type), per (partner x ack stage), or per (partner x message type x ack stage)?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A7-i — Per (partner x message type), with the accepted-but-not-live wait kept as a separate expectation owned by 12.03.02** | 12.02.03 lines 113-115: 'The window is per-partner profile data (CX-02), per (partner x message type) — a takedown is frequently expedited for legal reasons while a new release sits in a queue, so one window per partner would be wrong in both directions.' The observation interval stays as defined: 'completed `signal-complete -> first terminal ack` observations'. The distinct problem 12.03.02 DT-15 identifies — 'overdue waiting for an ack (a transport or partner-integration problem, chase the pipe) and overdue waiting to go live (the domain's worst case, chase the store) are different problems with different escalations' — is handled where it already lives: 12.03.02 D-11 keys its expectation 'at this state', and the accepted-not-live wait is that file's, not this window's. | It is the only key under which 12.02.03's stated observation interval remains arithmetically valid; the N>=30 floor is defined over exactly this interval.; It satisfies DT-15's actual requirement — ack-wait and live-wait are different problems with different escalations — without decomposing the ack-wait at all, because the live-wait is not an ack window in the first place.; The takedown-vs-new-release justification is concrete and evidenced in-source. | It cannot distinguish a partner that receives fast and decides slowly from one that never receives at all — a distinction 12.02.03 itself calls 'a real signal about the partner (a partner that parses in 20 minutes and decides in 9 days has a different problem from one that never parses at all)'.; 12.02.02 PC-10's 'per stage' phrasing must be edited, not merely referenced. | no |
| **A7-ii — Per (partner x message type x ack stage)** | 12.02.02 PC-10's per-stage axis is combined with 12.02.03's message-type axis. This is NOT a free carry-across: 12.02.03's tier-1 observation is a single end-to-end interval ('completed `signal-complete -> first terminal ack` observations'), which does not decompose into per-stage intervals. Choosing this requires defining new per-stage observation intervals (signal-complete->received, received->parsed, parsed->accepted), which multiplies the sample requirement by the number of stages and invalidates the '>=30 completed observations' arithmetic as stated. No source defines those intervals. | It is the only key that captures the parse-fast/decide-slow signal 12.02.03 explicitly says it keeps internally.; It matches 12.02.02 PC-10 as literally written ('the expected-response window per stage'). | It commissions new machinery: per-stage observation intervals, per-stage sample floors, and a rule for what happens when a partner emits no intermediate acks at all (many do not — 12.02.03 notes the cloud-bucket shape where 'nothing responds at all until S7').; It makes A3's sample floor materially harder to reach per stage, sharpening A3-i's permanent-silence con rather than leaving it untouched.; 12.03.02 D-11 keys 'at this state', which is a board-state axis and not the same decomposition, so this does not automatically reconcile the third file either. | **yes** |
| **A7-iii — Per (partner x ack stage), no message-type axis (12.02.02 PC-10 as written)** | One window per partner per ack stage, with no distinction between a new release, an update and a takedown. | Fewest keys, so the sample floor is easiest to reach per key.; Matches 12.02.02 PC-10's current wording exactly, so PC-10 needs no edit. | 12.02.03 rejects this directly and gives the reason: 'a takedown is frequently expedited for legal reasons while a new release sits in a queue, so one window per partner would be wrong in both directions'. A takedown window inflated by new-release queue time is the case where a slow legal removal looks normal.; It still carries A7-ii's undefined per-stage observation-interval problem. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A7-i — Per (partner x message type), live-wait left with 12.03.02 — It is the only option that does not require inventing an observation model. The argument that the per-stage axis is 'orthogonal and therefore free' does not hold: the axes are orthogonal but the statistic is not axis-neutral, because 12.02.03's tier-1 interval is end-to-end and per-stage intervals are defined nowhere. And the third-source support usually cited for per-stage keying — 12.03.02 DT-15 — is actually about ack-wait versus live-wait, which A7-i already separates by leaving the live-wait to 12.03.02. If the owner does want the parse-fast/decide-slow signal turned into an alarm, A7-ii is the honest way to get it, and should be chosen knowing it commissions new per-stage observation intervals and new floors.

**Affected files**: `12-release-distribution/12.02-ddex-delivery-messaging/12.02.02-per-partner-profile-conformance.md`, `12-release-distribution/12.02-ddex-delivery-messaging/12.02.03-delivery-choreography-acknowledgement.md`, `12-release-distribution/12.02-ddex-delivery-messaging/12.02-ddex-delivery-messaging-cx.md`, `12-release-distribution/12.03-dsp-store-territory-management/12.03.02-per-store-delivery-status.md`, `12-release-distribution/release-distribution-cx.md`


### DQ-12

> **File**: `13-gear-marketplace/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure/13.02.01-condition-grading-scale.md` · **Domain**: `13` · **3 open / 3 locked**

**13.02.01's Grade Ceilings table is keyed on an axis + severity vocabulary that 13.02.02's per-item severity options do not carry, so the ceiling cannot execute; the owner must decide (a) what data element carries the projection from a severity option to a ceiling, (b) which artifact authors that projection, and (c) whether the existing allowance for a professionally-repaired unit to still be Excellent survives.**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot build the grade cross-check at all. 13.02.01's Grade Ceilings table is keyed on `(axis, severity)` where axis is `cosmetic \| functional \| structural \| missing-part` and severity is `light \| moderate \| heavy \| repaired \| unrepaired \| impaired \| fails \| non-essential \| essential`. 13.02.02 authors severity options per item in tech language (`Crack: finish-only / through-timber, stable / through-timber, repaired / active`) and carries no axis and no shared severity token. There is no join. Concretely blocked: 13.02.01 Happy Path step 2 (compute the ceiling, disable grades above it, annotate each with the blocking flaw), E-01/E-11 (the challenge and the live-edit block), the `ceiling_at_time_of_grading` field stored at step 4, 13.02.01 D-08's forced Non-Functioning rule, and 13.09.03 intake grading which shares the same ceilings. 13.02.01 itself admits the gap in-file and both its Cross-Cut Notes and the sub-domain CX file left it live; Step 6 routed it back to the sub-domain CX file (gear-marketplace-cx.md line 430) which contains no axis or severity vocabulary anywhere. Until (a) and (b) are answered there is no schema for the join, and until (c) is answered two rows of the ceiling table are unimplementable because nothing tells the system which of them applies.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| L1 — Shape of the seller-facing severity vocabulary | 13.02.02-mandatory-flaw-disclosure.md D-05 (with DT-11): "Severity options are authored per item, never a shared Light/Moderate/Severe scale \| Per DT-11. A generic scale is unanswerable, uninterpretable, and makes materiality uncomputable — which would force the seller to adjudicate it (violating D-06)." DT-11 gives the worked form: "Severity options are authored per item, in the language a tech actually uses: `Fret wear: light / grooved, needs level / needs refret`. `Crack: finish-only / through-timber, stable / through-timber, repaired / active`." |
| L2 — Who adjudicates what a severity means | 13.02.02-mandatory-flaw-disclosure.md D-06: "**Materiality is computed from the `(item, severity)` pair by the structural / functional / concealed test — the seller never decides it** \| Resolves Q-02. Asking a seller \"is this serious?\" asks them to assess their own liability, which no honest answer survives. The checklist author decides once, per category, for everyone." Any option that asks the seller to pick an axis, a severity token, or a ceiling is foreclosed by this. Note its literal scope is materiality; it does not by itself settle axis O3 (repair quality), which is adjacent rather than identical. |
| L3 — Whether the flaw set sets the grade or caps it | 13.02.01-condition-grading-scale.md D-08: "**`functional: fails` forces Non-Functioning; every other flaw sets a ceiling**", and DT-06 which rejects computing the grade outright on three independent grounds, including "The flaw set yields a **ceiling, not a value**: two units with identical disclosed flaws can honestly differ, and only the person holding the unit knows. Compute the ceiling, let the human choose beneath it, keep the liability where the knowledge is." No option may compute the grade from the flaw set. |

#### DQ-12.1 — O1 — Carrier: what data element projects a per-item severity option onto a grade ceiling  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**What does an authored severity option carry so that 13.02.01's Grade Ceilings table can execute against it?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — An authored (axis, severity-token) pair on each severity option; 13.02.01 keeps one ceiling table** | Every authored severity option gains one authored property alongside the materiality verdict it already produces: a pair drawn from 13.02.01's vocabulary — axis from `cosmetic \| functional \| structural \| missing-part`, token from `light \| moderate \| heavy \| repaired \| unrepaired \| impaired \| fails \| non-essential \| essential`. `Crack: finish-only` -> (cosmetic, light); `Crack: through-timber, stable` -> (structural, unrepaired); `Fret wear: needs refret` -> (functional, impaired). 13.02.01's Grade Ceilings table is unchanged and remains the single executable ceiling policy. The seller never sees the pair. | Adds no new actor and no new decision moment. 13.02.02 D-06 already establishes exactly this shape — a shared classification computed from the `(item, severity)` pair, with "The checklist author decides once, per category, for everyone." This is one more authored property on the same pair, resolved by the same actor at the same authoring moment.; Leaves L1 intact. The seller still picks tech language ("through-timber, stable") and never a generic scale, because the pair is authored metadata the seller neither sees nor selects — so 13.02.02 D-05/DT-11's rejection is untouched.; Preserves a single ceiling artifact, which 13.02.01 D-07 versions ("Grade definitions are immutable once published and versioned; listings store `(grade, definition_version)`") and which 13.02.01's own Cross-Cut Notes state 13.09.03 shares: "Same 8 grades, same ceilings, same definition versions" (restated at gear-marketplace-cx.md CX-20).; 13.02.01's Scale table survives unedited: its Requires column is written in the same tokens ("Cosmetic flaws <= `light`", "<= `moderate`", "<= `heavy`", ">=1 functional flaw at `impaired`, or structural `unrepaired`", "Missing-part flaw at `essential`", ">=1 functional flaw at `fails`"), so the vocabulary this option preserves is the one the grade definitions are already written against. | The axis must be authored, not derived. 13.02.02's materiality test is three *disjunctive* conditions producing a boolean material/cosmetic verdict ("an `(item, severity)` pair is **material** if it satisfies **any** of three conditions"), not a classifier emitting one label — a pair can satisfy two at once. And its condition names (structural / functional / concealed) overlap 13.02.01's four axes in only **two**: `concealed` is a materiality condition, not an axis, and `missing-part` has no materiality analogue at all.; Two authored facts then hang off one `(item, severity)` pair — the materiality verdict and the ceiling projection — and they can drift out of agreement unless one is derived from the other.; Adds authoring load to per-category content, and 13.02.02 Q-05 records that this content has no owner: "Who authors and maintains the per-category checklists, severity enumerations, materiality flags and consequence strings? This is a catalog-admin capability with **no persona that holds it**." 13.08.01 Q-06 records the same finding as "the **third** capability landing on 13.02.02 Q-05's finding". This con is largely dissolved if axis O2 resolves to the moderation-owned schema. | no |
| **B — Author the ceiling grade directly on each severity option; 13.02.01's table becomes non-normative guidance** | Each authored severity option carries a maximum achievable grade outright ("Crack: through-timber, repaired -> Ceiling: Excellent"). No intermediate axis or severity token exists anywhere. The executable ceiling is the lowest authored ceiling among the disclosed options; 13.02.01's Grade Ceilings table is demoted to worked guidance for authors, and D-08's forcing rule must be re-expressed as an authored flag on the option because no token remains to derive it from. | One authored fact per option instead of two, so no pair-versus-ceiling disagreement is possible.; Dissolves axis O3 by construction: an author who can tell professional from amateur repair in their category authors two options with different ceilings; one who cannot, authors one.; The author speaks in item language throughout and never translates into a shared vocabulary, which is the closest fit to DT-11's stated motivation. | Fragments one policy across every authored option in every category. A policy change ("unrepaired structural should cap at Fair, not Good") becomes an edit to every affected row rather than one table. DEC-028's downstream rule states the risk directly — "two copies of third-party requirements would drift, and one would be wrong".; Creates a second, independent price-relevant version stream. 13.02.02 states "A disclosure is a claim against the checklist version in force on its `as-of` date", while 13.02.01 D-07 versions grade definitions separately; a listing's `ceiling_at_time_of_grading` would then be keyed to two unrelated lifecycles.; Strands 13.02.01's Scale table, whose Requires cells for grades 3-8 are written in the severity tokens this option abolishes. Those cells would have to be rewritten, not merely re-keyed.; Deletes the only place the cosmetic gradient (light -> Excellent, moderate -> Very Good, heavy -> Good) is stated as one coherent rule, so cross-category consistency becomes an authoring convention rather than a specification.; Leaves no single shared ceilings artifact for 13.09.03 to inherit, contradicting 13.02.01's Cross-Cut Note and gear-marketplace-cx.md CX-20 ("same 8 grades, same ceilings, same definition versions"). | no |
| **C — Key ceilings off the materiality verdict 13.02.02 already computes, and delete the cosmetic gradient** | 13.02.01's Grade Ceilings table is rewritten against data that already exists: the structural / functional / concealed materiality verdict of D-06, plus the two rules needing no token (Mint requires zero disclosed flaws on any axis; `functional: fails` forces Non-Functioning). Cosmetic flaws stop producing a ceiling entirely, consistent with 13.02.02's framing that cosmetic flaws are "implied by the grade (13.02.01), disclosed for the record, and **never photo-gated**". No new authored property anywhere. | Zero new authoring, so it cannot be blocked by 13.02.02 Q-05's missing author.; Removes the repair-quality input (axis O3) entirely rather than resolving it.; Aligned with 13.02.02's own stated division of labour — cosmetic is implied by the grade, material is disclosed and gated — so the ceiling only fires on the set the disclosure file already treats as consequential. | Contradicts 13.02.01's Scale table, whose Requires cells for Excellent, Very Good and Good are written purely in cosmetic severity terms. Three cells would have to be **deleted**, not re-keyed — this is a grade-definition change, and grade definitions are versioned and immutable under 13.02.01 D-07.; Disarms the cross-check for the persona it was created for. 13.02.01's Role Lens names the Musician as "The persona most likely to over-grade their own gear — not dishonestly, but because they're fond of it — so they are the persona the flaw/grade cross-check (CX-01) exists to catch". With no cosmetic ceiling, disclosed heavy buckle rash and wear-through no longer block Excellent.; Removes the only listing-time cap on cosmetic over-grading, leaving 13.02.01 D-13's inclusive-upward facet default as the sole mitigation for the pressure DT-08 identifies ("the facet **is** the grade-inflation engine").; `concealed` is a materiality condition, not a grade axis, and has no natural ceiling — a concealed cosmetic flaw and a concealed structural one land in the same bucket. And because the test is disjunctive, a pair satisfying two conditions yields no single bucket at all.; Missing-part ceilings (`non-essential` -> Very Good, `essential` -> Poor) still need a severity distinction materiality cannot supply, so the vocabulary problem is reduced but not eliminated. | no |

**Recommendation**: A — An authored (axis, severity-token) pair on each severity option; 13.02.01 keeps one ceiling table — It is the only carrier that leaves every locked decision standing. L1 (per-item severity language) is untouched because the pair is never seller-facing. L2 is satisfied because the checklist author decides it once per category, which is literally what D-06 already says that actor does with this same pair. L3 is untouched because the table still caps rather than sets. 13.02.01's Scale table and its versioned grade definitions survive unedited, which neither B nor C achieves — B rewrites six Requires cells and C deletes three. B's cost is a fragmented policy plus a second price-relevant version stream colliding with D-07; C's cost is deleting the cosmetic gradient that 13.02.01's grade definitions are written in and disarming the cross-check for the exact persona CX-01 names. A's real cost is honest and should be stated to the owner: the axis is **not** substantially derivable from the materiality test (only two of four axes overlap, and the test is a disjunctive boolean, not a classifier), so this is genuinely one new authored property per severity option, not a formalisation of something already computed. *(precedent: 13.02.02 D-06 — an in-file, exact-shape precedent: a shared classification authored once per category off the `(item, severity)` pair, with the seller kept out of it. The tie-break test itself follows 13.02.03 D-06, which reasons verbatim that voiding on any change "needs **no ordering and no materiality definition** — zero invention", and DEC-041, which struck an escalation clause because "no source defines how the platform would observe" its trigger. Note the limit of that test here: A does author something new, so it wins on *fewest* new mechanisms rather than none.)*

CX-14 Q2's trigger chain already names the ceiling table as a schema-resolution output: "Category assigned via bind -> attribute schema resolved -> flaw checklist + component set + grade-ceiling table instantiated."; 13.02.02's own Cross-Cut Notes count this as the settled direction: "Three features now share this mechanism: attribute schemas (13.01.01), component sets (13.02.03), flaw checklists (13.02.02). 13.02.03 Q-02 already asks whether they are one mechanism — this file is the third vote for yes." | "One mechanism" is a live open question, not a lock. 13.02.03 Q-02 ("Are component sets per-category (like flaw checklists and attribute schemas), and should all three share one mechanism?", owner Agent, deferred to `/create-prd-architecture`) and 13.01.01's Cross-Cut Note ("Worth Step 6 confirming they are one mechanism, not two") both remain open. Choosing E answers Q-02 affirmatively ahead of the workflow it is deferred to.; It commissions a small extension to a locked enum. 13.01.01 D-02 locks that "Every attribute declares a **consequence class**: `facet` \| `safety` \| `compliance` \| `logistics`", and a grade-ceiling projection is none of the four — so E requires either a fifth class or an explicit carve-out, plus a disposition for D-10's activation rule (safety/compliance draft until >=80% coverage; facet activates immediately).; It leaves a real tension visible rather than resolving it: CX-14 Q1 says moderation owns the schema, while 13.02.02 Q-05 says the per-category content has no persona. DEC-028 is cited here only in the direction it genuinely runs: it forbids a second copy (eliminating F) but does **not** by itself select an owner between D and E — author-where-owned presupposes ownership, which is the very thing this axis decides.)*

So the common case degrades to plain Option G.; Adds a dependency on 13.08.02 Q-02 resolving before the corroboration half is implementable. | no |
| **I — Collapse both rows to the lower ceiling (Good) and delete the Excellent allowance** | Any structural `repaired` flaw caps the grade at Good. 13.02.01's Grade Ceilings loses one row and the Scale table's grade-3 Requires cell loses "at most one professionally-executed, stable, disclosed structural repair". | One rule, no repair-quality input anywhere, nothing for a seller to self-assess.; Removes the row that is currently unimplementable because nothing tells the system which of the two applies. | It is a substantive grade-semantics change, not a vocabulary fix: it alters what Excellent means. Under 13.02.01 D-07 grade definitions are immutable once published and versioned, so this is a `v2` publication with a comp-bucket mapping, not an edit.; Its usual justification — that no source supplies repair quality — is false as stated. 13.08.02 records the repairer, 13.09.03 is a professional bench assessment, and 13.02.02 DT-04 already models a repair as flaw-severity `repaired` plus a simultaneous originality fact. (The commonly cited 13.02.02 quote, "The platform makes **no claim to evaluate photo quality** — asserting a computer-vision capability here would be fabricating a mechanism", rules out CV assessment of *photo quality*, not platform knowledge of repair quality, and does not support the collapse.); It penalises the exact behaviour the sub-domain rewards elsewhere: a seller who had a break professionally repaired and disclosed it lands in the same bucket as one whose repair is failing. | no |

**Recommendation**: H — Keep the split, seller-asserted, with first-party records pre-filling and contradictions surfaced — This axis must not be decided as a side effect of O1. It changes what a grade *means*, which is versioned content under 13.02.01 D-07, whereas O1 and O2 change only how the ceiling is computed. Option I is the tempting default and should be rejected: it deletes a live allowance in the Scale table on the false premise that no source supplies repair quality, when three do for on-platform repairs. H keeps the allowance and reuses the pre-fill-never-auto-assert pattern already specified for precisely this class of fact, degrading gracefully to G where no record exists. If the owner prefers not to take a dependency on 13.08.02 Q-02, G is the safe fallback and preserves the Scale table equally well; only I requires a grade-definition version bump. *(precedent: 13.02.02 D-07 with DT-05 — ratified, and pointing this way: platform-held first-party records for this unit pre-fill and surface contradictions but never auto-assert, because the seller signs and carries the liability. 13.02.03 D-03 is the same constraint one file over, which D-07 explicitly inherits.)*

**Affected files**: `13-gear-marketplace/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure/13.02.01-condition-grading-scale.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure/13.02.02-mandatory-flaw-disclosure.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure/13.02.03-modification-originality-disclosure.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure-cx.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure/13.02-condition-originality-disclosure-index.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/gear-marketplace-cx.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.01-canonical-gear-catalog/13.01.01-model-records-taxonomy-attributes.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.09-tradein-consignment/13.09.03-inspection-intake-grading.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.08-returns-rma-warranty/13.08.02-rma-repair-routing.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.08-returns-rma-warranty/13.08.01-returns-refunds.md`, `13-gear-marketplace/13.04-price-discovery-market-data/13.04.01-price-guide-comps.md`


### DQ-13

> **File**: `16-venues-studios-spaces/16.01-place-records-rooms/16.01.02-room-space-first-class-entity.md` · **Domain**: `16` · **3 open / 1 locked**

**When a second org runs one room inside a place record claimed by someone else (a studio subleasing a venue's upstairs room), decide separately: how that org's authority over the room comes into existence, whether the place claimant can take it away, and — if the org can establish itself independently — what proof it uses.**

**Why it blocks**: Two [DEEP] files dated the same day specify incompatible mechanisms for the same thing and no later file adjudicates them. 16.01.01 D-07 says a room's operating org is "claimed, not granted — via 16.05.02's ladder, anchored to the room operator's own pre-existing facts", and its Role Lens says the Operator "Grants nothing to a sublessee". 16.01.02 D-09 says "The room is the unit of operational delegation — one record, one claim, a room granted to another org", with an edge-case row defining revocation. Until this is resolved an implementer cannot build room-level write permission, cannot know whether 16.05.02's claim unit is the record or the room (D-14 says record; Q-03 is open and routed to 16.01.02), cannot implement the room's Live gate for a second-org room (16.01.02 D-11), and 16.05.07 D-17's merge-confirmer rule rests on a premise that may not survive. 16.01 index Q-01 ("Containment — ... Two records with a link, or one record with two claims?") is still open on disk.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| Axis 0 — Is containment one record or two? | 16.01.01-place-record-type-taxonomy.md · D-06: "**Containment is room-level operating org, not a second record.** A studio subleasing a room inside a venue is one record whose subleased room carries a different operating org \| A record is a building (D-04) and there is one building. Two records would duplicate the address, load-in and **premises licence** — one document about one building — and disagree the moment any of them changes (DT-07)". Corroborated, not contradicted, by the file on the other side of this conflict: 16.01.02 DT-11 rejects two records because it "duplicates one building and re-splits the calendar D-01 exists to keep whole"; and by 16.05.07 N-4, which offers "containment migration (16.01.01 D-06 — fold the contained record's rooms into the premises record, transferring their operating org)". No option below reopens it. |

#### DQ-13.1 — Axis 1 — Origin: how does the second org's authority over the room come into existence?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does a room operating org come into existence by the place claimant granting the room, by that org independently proving control of the room, or by both paths being available?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1 — Own proof only (16.01.01 D-07 stands; 16.01.02 D-09 is rewritten)** | There is no grant surface at all. A room operating org exists only where that org has completed 16.05.02's proof-of-control ladder scoped to the room, anchored to facts the place claimant did not supply. The place claimant is notified within 24h and may dispute to 24; the room stays live and bookable throughout. 16.05.02 Q-03 is answered "yes, a room is a claim unit" and D-14 ("The claim unit is the **place record**") is amended. In 16.01.02, D-09 and DT-11 are rewritten and the "Delegation revoked" edge-case row is deleted, because nothing is revocable. | One later [DEEP] file already spends it as a premise. 16.05.07 (2026-07-17) D-17 reads: "16.01.01 D-07 made a room's operating org claimed-not-granted precisely so a landlord is not the gatekeeper of a tenant's presence. A merge confirmed only by the survivor's claimant hands that lever back (DT-13)". Choosing A1 costs no rewrite there.; It removes the landlord's leverage completely and at every point in the lifecycle — the harm 16.01.01 DT-08 names: "A grant makes the venue Operator the gatekeeper of an independent business's entire platform presence — its calendar, its bookings, its money. In a rent dispute the grant is a lever."; The sublessee's presence never depends on the landlord's cooperation, so a landlord who simply refuses to act cannot keep a real business off the platform. | The claim unit has to change. 16.05.02 D-14 states "The claim unit is the **place record**", and its anchor snapshot (D-07) reads the record's contact fields' most recent `seeded`/`statutory` values — which belong to the venue, not the sublessee. A room-scoped anchor set does not exist and must be defined.; For exactly the population it is meant to serve it is document review permanently, not as a fallback. 16.05.02 DT-10: studios cannot be seeded (16.05.01 D-05), so "a studio record has no seeded values, therefore no anchor set, therefore no strong rung — ever". DT-11 and D-14 remove the postal rung at a shared address. 16.05.02 Q-01 calls that ladder position an unresourced burden and is explicit that "it is an ops cost, not a code cost".; During L7 review the claim is Provisional, and 16.05.02 D-08 grants Provisional no calendar and no rate card — so a sublessee with a cooperative landlord still cannot trade for the 5-business-day review SLA.; It opens the surface 16.01.01 Q-09 names: "a successful hostile room claim inherits a live calendar and deposits belonging to a business that is not even the record's claimant, and the place claimant — the only party watching — has no financial exposure to make them notice." | **yes** |
| **A2 — Grant only (16.01.02 D-09 stands; 16.01.01 D-07 and DT-08 are struck)** | The place claimant hands a named room to another org as a grant on their own claim. The delegate owns that room's spec, calendar, rate and enquiries; identity, address, type set and status stay with the claimant. No room claim unit, no change to 16.05.02 D-14. Whether the claimant can take it back is Axis 2. | No new claim semantics. 16.01.02 DT-11's stated reason holds: "two claims on one record requires a partial-claim concept 16.05.02 does not have and should not grow." Consistent with 16.05.02 D-14 exactly as written today.; Instant and free for the sublessee — no document queue, and it works for the org 16.05.02 DT-10/DT-11 show can never anchor.; It is the only model the newest artifact propagates. The domain CX (2026-07-18) carries D-09 alone at CX-03 Q3 line 112 ("room-level delegation (16.01.02 D-09) is a new grantable power the sublease question forces") and CX-07 Q3 line 227, and its mechanism table at line 296 lists "Room-level delegation on a single claim (D-09)". D-07 appears nowhere in it. Choosing A2 costs no CX rewrite.; It closes 16.01.01 Q-09's hostile-room-claim surface outright: nobody can acquire a room the claimant did not hand over. | A landlord who simply never grants leaves a genuine sublessee with no platform presence at all and no recourse. No source defines any fallback under this model.; It reverses 16.01.01 D-07 and flips DT-08 from REJECTED to CONFIRMED, and it removes the stated basis of a later ratified decision — 16.05.07 D-17's rationale would have to be re-founded on something other than "a landlord is not the gatekeeper of a tenant's presence".; It is not free of new machinery. 16.01.02's own cross-cut note concedes: "**Delegation (D-09) is a new grantable power on the claim that 16.05.02 does not currently model** — it must not require a partial-claim concept." | **yes** |
| **A3 — Both: grant is the cooperative default, own proof is the escape hatch** | The place claimant may grant a room to another org — instant, no ops cost, no claim unit involved, which is how the ordinary cooperative sublease is recorded. Independently, a room operator may establish itself through 16.05.02's ladder scoped to the room when the claimant will not cooperate or has vanished. 16.05.02 Q-03 is answered "yes, a room can be a claim unit" and D-14 is amended; 16.01.02 D-09 survives as one of two origins; 16.01.01 D-07 survives as the other. Both files' decision text is narrowed rather than struck. Requires a specified collision rule for a claim arriving on an already-granted room. | It is the only option under which both files' actual arguments hold. 16.01.02 DT-11's "no partial-claim concept" is true of the default path most subleases will use, and 16.01.01 DT-08's harm — the venue Operator as "gatekeeper of an independent business's entire platform presence" — is removed, because the sublessee always has a path that does not need the landlord.; The two-path shape is already ratified in the file that owns claims. 16.05.02 D-10 and its Behavior section: "**The cooperative path is one action**: org A initiates transfer to org B, org B accepts, done. No re-verification. ... **The challenge path** exists for the uncooperative or absent incumbent (D-10)". Same problem, same answer, one level up.; Neither later artifact is stranded: 16.05.07 D-17's premise (a sublessee is not at the landlord's mercy) survives, and the Step 6 CX propagation of D-09 stays substantially correct because the grant still exists. | It commissions the most machinery of the three — both the grantable power 16.05.02 "does not currently model" (16.01.02 cross-cut note) and the room-scoped claim unit D-14 currently forbids.; Two origins means two authority provenances on one room, and no source defines what happens when a granted room operator later claims, or when a room claim lands on an already-granted room. That collision must be specified before an implementer can build it.; It carries 16.01.01 Q-09's hostile-room-claim exposure (the claim path is the vector) without A2's compensating simplicity. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A3 — Both — The two source files are not arguing about the same thing. 16.01.02 DT-11's whole argument is about origin cost — that a second claim on one record "requires a partial-claim concept 16.05.02 does not have". 16.01.01 DT-08's whole argument is about dependency and leverage — that a grant makes the landlord the gatekeeper and "in a rent dispute the grant is a lever". A3 is the only option that answers both rather than picking a loser. It also matches the real distribution of cases: most subleases are cooperative and should cost nobody anything (A1 charges every one of them a permanent L7 document review, because 16.05.02 DT-10 establishes a studio can never have an anchor set), while the uncooperative landlord is precisely the case a fast path cannot serve (A2 gives that sublessee nothing at all). Drafting consequence, not a directional argument: whichever option is chosen, the room-authority rule should be authored in 16.01.02 and referenced from 16.01.01 and 16.05.02, because both other owners nominate 16.01.02 — 16.01.01 Q-08 ("a room carries an operating org that may differ from the place's claimant, which is a new permission axis on the room" ... "theirs to absorb, not mine to write") and 16.05.02 DT-11 ("whether the place record supports per-room claims by different orgs is 16.01.02's to answer (Q-03)", Q-03 deferred to "16.01.02 · /create-prd-architecture"). *(precedent: 16.05.02 D-10 — the cooperative-transfer-plus-challenge-path shape, applied to the same problem (an authority held by a party who may or may not cooperate) in the file that owns claims: "The cooperative path is one action ... The challenge path exists for the uncooperative or absent incumbent." This precedent points at A3 directly. The P-04 author-where-owned rule is deliberately NOT used here as a directional precedent — see challengeResponses.)*

#### DQ-13.2 — Axis 2 — Extinguishment: can the place claimant end a granted room operator's authority unilaterally?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Where a room operating org's authority came from the place claimant's grant, may the claimant end it unilaterally, only through an adjudicated dispute, or only until the room operator has independently proved control?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **B1 — Unilateral, effective-dated (16.01.02's current text)** | The claimant revokes the grant. Per 16.01.02's edge-case row, "Revocation is **effective-dated after the last confirmed booking**, or requires an explicit Operator-fault cancellation of each. It must never orphan a booking whose counterparty has already paid a deposit." | It is already written and already protects paid deposits — no new booking-safety rule is needed.; Zero adjudication load: 24 is never involved in a sublease ending.; It matches the physical fact in the ordinary case — the tenant leaves, and the room is the landlord's to re-let. | It is the exact harm 16.01.01 DT-08 rejects by name: "revoking it takes a trading studio offline over a landlord/tenant row the platform has no business adjudicating. That is 16.05.02 DT-03's rejected reasoning (\"freeze the record\") arriving through a side door."; The effective-dating only protects bookings already confirmed. Beyond that horizon the room operator loses calendar, rate card and enquiry inbox at the landlord's discretion — the take-a-trading-business-offline outcome 16.05.02 D-03 exists to prevent one level up.; It makes 16.05.07 D-17's stated rationale false while its rule stands (under B1 the landlord IS the gatekeeper), so D-17 must be re-founded on a different argument. | no |
| **B2 — Adjudicated: a revocation is a dispute, not an act** | The claimant cannot extinguish the grant by fiat. Initiating revocation opens a dispute with a counter-window and escalation to 24, and the room stays live and bookable throughout — the shape of 16.05.02 D-03 and D-10 applied to a granted authority. The room operator is never asked to prove anything. | It removes the only harm 16.01.01 DT-08 actually names (the lever) without requiring the room operator to prove anything — so it still works for the anchorless population 16.05.02 DT-10/DT-11 describe.; Its shape is ratified twice in 16.05.02: D-03 ("A contested claim freezes the **claim**, never the record") and D-10's 14-day counter-window with maximal notification, where "Silence is what transfers control, so the notification burden must be maximal."; It preserves the clause 16.01.01 D-07 and 16.05.07 D-17 both end on (16.05.02 D-03 is the principle they inherit it from, per 16.05.07 DT-13; D-03 itself does not contain the clause) — "the **room stays live and bookable** throughout". | No source defines a dispute path for a grant. 16.05.02's D-03/D-10 machinery is defined over claims and anchor snapshots; a revocation dispute over a non-claim authority has no anchor to re-prove against and must be specified from scratch.; It puts the platform inside the landlord/tenant row that 16.01.01 DT-08 itself says "the platform has no business adjudicating" — 24 becomes the arbiter of whether a sublease has genuinely ended.; A landlord whose tenant has genuinely vacated must wait out a dispute window before re-letting their own room on the platform. | **yes** |
| **B3 — Revocable until the room operator independently proves control, then not (two-tier)** | The grant is revocable while it is the only basis of authority. Once the room operator completes a strong rung on its own facts, the authority is anchored and can only be disputed, never revoked. Coherent only if Axis 1 = A3 (a claim path must exist to harden into). | It bounds the lever to the window before the room operator has proved anything — which is also the window before they are holding other people's deposits.; It has a tier precedent in the owning file: 16.05.02 D-08's Provisional/Full split — "Provisional exists so an Operator is not dead for 5 business days. It grants exactly the actions whose worst case is a reversible, attributable wrong value — never the calendar, terms, badge, or money." | That same precedent cuts against it on contents: 16.05.02 D-08's pre-proof tier grants no calendar, no rate card and no badge — which is everything 16.01.02 D-09 says a delegate owns. A room operator in the un-hardened tier would hold a spec editor, not a business.; The population it is meant to protect is the one that can never leave the revocable tier: 16.05.02 DT-10 (studios are never seeded, so no anchor set, ever) plus DT-11/D-14 (postal not anchor-eligible at a shared address). For them B3 is B1 with extra steps.; It needs a room-authority state machine (granted / anchored / disputed) that no file has, plus a rule for a granted room's existing bookings if the grant is revoked before hardening. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: B2 — Adjudicated — 16.01.01 DT-08's rejection of the grant is, read closely, entirely a rejection of unilateral revocation: the words are "the grant is a lever" and "revoking it takes a trading studio offline". B2 removes that and leaves the grant, which is why the two source files stop conflicting once this axis is separated from Axis 1. B2 also costs the room operator nothing, which is what distinguishes it from B3 — 16.05.02 DT-10/DT-11 establish that the sublessee studio is structurally unable to complete any strong rung, so any option that conditions protection on proof gives that population nothing. Be explicit with the implementer that B2 is new machinery: 16.05.02 D-03 and D-10 are defined over claims and anchor snapshots, and a revocation dispute over a granted authority has nothing to re-prove against, so the counter-window's evidence test must be specified rather than inherited. Note also that if Axis 1 = A1, this axis does not arise at all: there is no grant to revoke, and authority ends only the way a claim ends under 16.05.02 D-11 (24 overturns, a challenger wins, or the proof is shown forged). *(precedent: 16.05.02 D-03 — "A contested claim freezes the **claim**, never the record \| Freezing takes a trading business offline over the platform's inability to adjudicate (DT-03)" — together with D-10's 14-day counter-window. Both point the same way: a disagreement about who runs something must not take that something off the market. They are precedent for the shape, not for the evidence test, which does not transfer.)*

#### DQ-13.3 — Axis 3 — Proof: what does a room operator use to independently establish control, given 16.05.02's anchor set is empty for this population by construction?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**If a room operator can establish itself without the place claimant (Axis 1 = A1 or A3), which rung of 16.05.02's ladder does a room claim run on?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **C1 — L7 document review, with the sublease/licence agreement as the document and the place claimant's org as the external cross-check** | The room claim runs 16.05.02's existing L7 rung unchanged. The reviewer performs L7's stated checks — document address against the record's address, and the document's named party against the claimant org name (01) — and here the lease additionally names the landlord, whose org is already on the record. The place claimant is separately notified within 24h and may dispute (16.01.01 D-07). No new anchor class, no new provenance class. | It adds no rung and no provenance class — it uses L7 exactly as 16.05.02 specifies it.; The landlord cross-check is a genuine strengthening of L7 for this specific case. 16.05.02 says of L7 that where the register cross-check is unavailable "L7 is a human reading a forgeable PDF"; a sublease naming a landlord whose org is already the record's claimant supplies an external check that generic L7 lacks.; The claimant notification is a second independent check and is already required by 16.01.01 D-07's own wording ("The place claimant is notified and may dispute"). | It is L7 permanently for this population, not as a fallback. 16.05.02 Q-01 flags exactly this as unresourced: "The weakest, slowest, forgeable, human-operated rung is the *primary* rung for the Producer ... Is that acceptable (and resourced — it is an ops cost, not a code cost)". Room claims add volume to that same queue.; 5-business-day review SLA, and 16.05.02 D-08 gives Provisional no calendar and no rate card — so a room operator on the claim path cannot trade until a human clears the document. | no |
| **C2 — Add place-claimant attestation as a room-scoped anchor rung** | The record's claimant org confirms the room operator, and that confirmation is treated as an anchor for a room claim. Instant, no document queue. | Instant, and no ops cost at all.; The attesting party genuinely does have first-hand knowledge of who occupies the room. | Under Axis 1 = A3 it is redundant — a cooperative claimant already has the grant path, which produces the same outcome with no new anchor class.; It makes the escape hatch depend on the party it exists to escape, reintroducing at origin exactly the dependency 16.01.01 DT-08 rejects.; It invents a provenance class 16.05.03's ranking does not have — the same objection 16.05.02 Q-06 raises against the "corroborated presence" rung ("it invents a 6th provenance class 16.05.03 D-02 does not have"). | **yes** |
| **C3 — Lock only that a room can be a claim unit; defer the rung set** | Answer 16.05.02 Q-03 "yes" and amend D-14 to admit a room claim unit, but leave which rungs a room claim may run to 16.05.02 and /create-prd-security, alongside Q-01 (studio ladder) and Q-05 (jurisdiction). | It keeps the rung question with the file and pipeline stage that already own it: Q-03 is routed to "16.01.02 · /create-prd-architecture", Q-01 to /ideate-validate, Q-05 to /ideate-validate, and 16.01.01 Q-01 (anchor stability window) to /create-prd-security.; It avoids pre-committing a security parameter before the threat model exists — the reason 16.01.01 Q-01 was deferred in the first place. | An implementer cannot build the escape hatch, only declare it. Whether the claim path is usable at all for a sublessee is entirely a function of which rung it runs on, so deferring the rung defers whether Axis 1's answer functions.; It leaves 16.05.02 with an amended D-14 whose consequences are unstated, which is the same half-answered condition that produced this conflict. | no |

**Recommendation**: C1 — L7 with the lease and the landlord cross-check — It commissions nothing. The rung, the reviewer's checks and the notification all exist in 16.05.02 and 16.01.01 today; the only new statement is that a sublease agreement is an acceptable L7 document for a room-scoped claim and that the named landlord is checked against the record's claimant org. C2 should be rejected on its own terms — under A3 a cooperative landlord already has the grant path, so an attestation rung buys nothing and costs the independence the claim path exists to provide. C3 is defensible but incomplete: it would leave the escape hatch declared and unbuildable. The residual parameters — SLA capacity, and whether room claims share 16.05.02 D-15's per-org rate limits — should still route to 16.05.02 Q-01 and Q-05 rather than being invented here. *(precedent: 16.05.02's L7 rung specification itself: "a human checking (a) the document's address against the record's address, (b) the document's named party against the claimant's org name (01), and (c) for a premises licence, the named holder against the public licensing register". C1 adds a fourth check of the same kind rather than a new mechanism.)*
