### DQ-14

> **File**: `16-venues-studios-spaces/venues-studios-spaces-index.md` · **Domain**: `16` · **4 open / 2 locked**

**Domain 16's statutory records are written in UK vocabulary (premises licence, PAT, PLI, PRS/PPL) but the launch market is locked to the US — the owner must decide whether the place record keeps a jurisdiction axis at all, how many regime profiles are authored at launch, what the US profile's statutory slots contain, and how the availability of an external licensing register is modelled.**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot write 16.01.06's field set at all (it still carries a live `[PENDING — /ideate-validate]` marker on exactly this), cannot know whether 16.05.02's L2 rung and 16.05.07 D-08's decisive dedup key exist in the launch market, and cannot pick 16.02.02's power-connector vocabulary (explicitly blocked on domain Q-13). Eight questions are open on disk against this one point: domain Q-13, 16.01 Q-03, 16.01.06 Q-01, 16.02.01 Q-01, 16.02.02 Q-04, 16.01.01 Q-05, 16.05.02 Q-05, 16.05.07 Q-05. Domain 19 consumes the licensed-capacity ceiling and cannot be specified until the ceiling's issuer and expiry semantics are fixed.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| 5. Which PRO set 16.02.06 treats as normative | ideation-index.md D-32 — '**Primary market = UNITED STATES to start** (owner revision from D-30's global)' — combined with 16.02.06-pro-blanket-licence-setlist-reporting.md, Overview line 10: 'Whether the venue holds a performing-rights blanket licence (PRS/PPL in the UK; ASCAP/BMI/SESAC in the US)'. The feature already names both sets; once D-32 fixes the launch market to the US, ASCAP/BMI/SESAC is the normative set by construction. There is no product choice remaining, so folding this into the owner question inflates the decision. 16.02.06's Unknown state is unaffected and remains 'the honest default for most of the registry'. |
| 6. Whether the licensed-capacity ceiling is a declared figure or a platform-derived one | Three ratified decisions foreclose a platform-derived ceiling. (a) 16.05.01-place-data-seeding-ingestion.md field table: 'Licensed capacity, curfew, licensable activities \| — \| — \| — \| **never** \| Statutory class. Only a register may write these (D-05)', reinforced by D-13: 'A `seeded`-class source may never write a statutory-class field — even when it has the data'. A platform-computed occupant load would be a non-register write into a statutory field. (b) 16.01.06 D-01: 'Licences/insurance are declarations with expiry, never platform-verified certificates \| Certification is liability the product cannot carry; the honest label is the feature (DT-03)'. (c) 16.02.01 D-02: 'Licensed capacity is a hard ceiling the Operator cannot raise \| It is imposed, not chosen'. No source anywhere proposes the platform computing a figure from floor area and egress; 16.02.01 Q-01 only observes that the US regime computes occupancy that way, which is a statement about the external authority, not a proposal for the platform. |

#### DQ-14.1 — 1. Parameterization shape — does a jurisdiction/regime axis exist, and how many profiles are authored at launch?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the place record carry a jurisdiction-keyed statutory-regime profile, and if so is more than one profile authored for launch?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — No regime axis: replace the UK vocabulary with a single US-shaped statutory record set** | 16.01.06's field set simply becomes the US regime. The UK vocabulary (premises licence, PAT, PLI, TEN) is deleted from the specs, not parked. 16.02.06's normative PRO set becomes ASCAP/BMI/SESAC. No jurisdiction field selects anything; there is one statutory record shape. | Smallest authoring surface — one vocabulary to write and one set of edge-case tables to keep true.; Closes domain Q-13, 16.01 Q-03, 16.01.06 Q-01, 16.02.01 Q-01 and 16.02.02 Q-04 outright (Q-04 says 'The field's shape is settled (D-14); the vocabulary cannot be until the launch market is', so fixing the market closes it). | Contradicts the second half of D-32 verbatim: '`/create-prd-security` keeps the data model jurisdiction-parameterized so later international expansion is additive, not a rewrite.' Removing the axis makes expansion exactly the rewrite D-32 names as the thing to avoid.; Does not actually avoid the work. 16.05.07 D-08 ('the **premises licence number** is the only decisive key — equality proves, **inequality refutes** and overrides everything') and 16.05.02's L2 ('claimant's org name matches the premises licence holder on the public licensing register') are written against a named UK instrument. Under A they must be rewritten as capability-conditioned rules anyway — which is a regime axis under another name (see Axis 3).; Buys no v1 speed: meta/constraints.md places 'Live/Events (16–19)' in the 'Phase 2+ (stay `Must` per D-20, later releases, ~124 Musts)' block, so this vocabulary is not on the v1 critical path. The urgency argues for deciding, not for narrowing. | no |
| **B — Regime axis with two authored profiles: US normative for launch, UK retained as a defined non-launch profile** | A jurisdiction selects a statutory-regime profile that declares, per regime, the field set, which fields carry statutory provenance, the issuer of each, and whether an external register class exists. Both the US and UK profiles are authored to implementation depth; the UK one is flagged non-launch. A jurisdiction with no authored profile yields explicit `unknown`, never a silent UK default. | Satisfies both halves of D-32 — US-first and 'jurisdiction-parameterized so later international expansion is additive, not a rewrite'.; Keeps the existing UK prose in 16.01.06, 16.02.03 and 16.05.* usable as-is rather than requiring it to be rewritten into a neutral form. | Two full vocabularies to maintain, and every edge-case table in 16.01.06, 16.02.01 and 16.02.03 acquires a per-profile column — against a solo builder, for a market not being launched into.; The retained UK profile is not load-bearing in the way it appears: 16.05.02 already authors the absence branch ('Territory has no public premises-licence register (domain Q-13) \| L2 does not exist there and L7 loses its external cross-check. The ladder degrades to postal + a human reading a PDF. Stated rather than papered over'), 16.05.07 Q-05 states the same for D-08, 16.02.03 D-01 states the curfew fallback, and 16.02.01 already has a `Claimed` state for 'No licence record exists (the majority case at cold start)'. Most of what B says it is protecting is already written as capability-absence, not as UK-specific text. | no |
| **B-minus — Regime axis, one authored profile: US only; the UK vocabulary retires into an unauthored profile that yields explicit unknown** | The jurisdiction-keyed profile mechanism exists (satisfying D-32's second half), but only the US profile is authored. The UK profile becomes an unauthored profile whose statutory fields resolve to explicit `unknown`. Rules that named a UK instrument are restated against a profile capability rather than an instrument: 16.05.07 D-08 becomes 'where the profile declares a decisive statutory building key, equality proves and inequality refutes'; 16.05.02's L2 becomes 'available where the profile declares a matchable register'. The UK-specific instrument names leave the specs; the capability they expressed does not. | Satisfies both halves of D-32 at the cost of one authored vocabulary rather than two — answers B's only real cost objection without deleting the axis the way A does.; Follows the precedent D-46 set for the identical class of problem: author the determinate jurisdictions and make every other territory an explicit `unknown` rather than a guess.; Forces the honest form of the rules that currently hardcode a UK instrument. 16.05.02 and 16.05.07 already describe the degraded branch in capability terms, so the rewrite is mostly promotion of text that exists. | Requires editing more existing prose than B: every place that names a premises licence, PAT, PLI or TEN as the instrument must be restated against a capability, in 16.01.06, 16.02.01, 16.02.03, 16.05.01, 16.05.02, 16.05.07 and 16.06.03.; Loses the worked UK example. If the second market is the UK, the vocabulary has to be re-derived rather than un-flagged.; The capability vocabulary itself (what a profile may declare) is new structure that must be kept small, or it grows into a rules engine. | no |
| **C — One regime-neutral abstraction; jurisdiction supplies labels only** | 16.01.06 carries generic slots — occupancy ceiling, liability cover, electrical-safety record, performing-rights licence, hirer requirements — and the jurisdiction supplies only the display label and the issuer's name. A US record and a UK record are the same record with different words on screen; there is no per-regime capability difference. | One data model, one set of edge cases, cheapest to extend to a third market.; Genuinely correct for the largest part of the surface: 16.02.01's three-layer model, D-05's required counting basis and 16.01.06's expiry state machine are regime-neutral already, and 16.02.01 Q-01 predicts exactly that ('though not, we believe, the three-layer model or the basis requirement'). | Cannot express 'this rung is unavailable here'. 16.05.02's own edge case and Q-05 ('Territories without one lose the strongest rung and the weakest rung's only defence simultaneously') and 16.05.07 Q-05 ('the only *decisive* signal vanishes and detection is inference-only') describe capability differences, not label differences. A single labelled slot silently promises a cross-check that may not exist.; Collides with 16.05.01 D-05/D-13. Statutory class has exactly one permitted writer ('Licensed capacity, curfew, licensable activities \| … \| **never** \| Statutory class. Only a register may write these'). A neutral slot with no statement of whether a register exists cannot say who, if anyone, may write it.; In practice it collapses into B-minus once L2, D-08 and the statutory writer rule are made honest — at the cost of having deleted the vocabulary first. | no |

**Recommendation**: B-minus — D-32 does not merely say US-first; it ends '`/create-prd-security` keeps the data model jurisdiction-parameterized so later international expansion is additive, not a rewrite', which rules out A and C. Between B and B-minus the question is whether a fully authored UK profile earns its maintenance, and the sources say most of what it would protect is already written as capability-absence rather than as UK text — 16.05.02's no-register edge case, 16.05.07 Q-05, 16.02.03 D-01 and 16.02.01's `Claimed` state all already author the degraded branch. So the axis is what must be kept; the second vocabulary is not. Live/Events is Phase 2+ per meta/constraints.md, so there is no launch urgency arguing for the cheaper-but-lossy A either. *(precedent: D-46 (CQ-09): 'CQ-09 v1 term and moral-right status is bounded to `US`, `FR`, `DE`, and `GB`. … missing/insufficient evidence and every other territory are explicit `unknown` / not determined.' That is the same shape — parameterize by jurisdiction, author only the determinate ones, make the rest explicitly unknown — and it points the same direction as B-minus. (The author-where-owned rule D-50/D-51 is NOT cited: it governs which spec file authors a requirement across domains, and a regime profile is not a spec-owning file.))*

#### DQ-14.2 — 2. Contents of the US launch profile's statutory slots  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does this decision name the US instruments that fill 16.01.06's statutory slots, or lock the slots and hand the instrument names to `/create-prd-security`?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Name the US instruments now** | The owner asserts the mapping in this decision: occupancy ceiling from the room's posted occupancy figure, a commercial general-liability certificate of insurance in place of PLI, an electrical/fire-inspection date in place of PAT, a state or municipal entertainment/alcohol permit where held, and a hirer certificate-of-insurance requirement in place of the PLI requirement. | Closes 16.01.06's live `[PENDING — /ideate-validate]` marker completely, in one move, with nothing deferred.; Unblocks 16.05.01's ingest mapping, which needs to know which source class writes which field. | None of these instrument names appears anywhere in the ideation tree — a full-tree grep for 'occupant load', 'certificate of occupancy' and 'general liability' returns only 16.02.01 Q-01's parenthetical about how US occupancy is computed. Naming them here records an owner assertion as if it were a spec-derived fact.; It is empirical, per-state work, which is precisely the class of thing D-32 hands to `/create-prd-security`. | no |
| **Lock the slots, defer the instrument names** | The profile is required to declare five slots — occupancy ceiling, liability cover, electrical/fire safety record, performing-rights licence status, hirer requirements — each with an issuer and, where the regime grants one, an expiry; and each remains a declaration, never a verified certificate. The US instrument names that fill those slots are researched and written at `/create-prd-security`. 16.01.06's `[PENDING]` marker changes owner from `/ideate-validate` to `/create-prd-security` rather than closing. | Locks everything that is a product decision and defers only what is empirical.; Preserves the slot set the Role Lens already depends on — the Musician lens reads the hirer PLI requirement and the Producer lens reads PAT status because 'they are the person plugging in a client's amp' — so no role loses a stated need.; Every slot inherits 16.01.06 D-01 unchanged ('Licences/insurance are declarations with expiry, never platform-verified certificates'). | Leaves a `[PENDING]` marker live in 16.01.06 through the rest of ideation, so a reader must follow it to `/create-prd-security` to see the field names.; 16.05.01's ingest mapping row for the register class stays provisional until the instrument names land. | no |
| **Minimal set — only the booking-blocking slots** | The US profile carries occupancy ceiling, liability cover, PRO status and hirer requirements, and drops the electrical/fire-safety slot at launch. | Fewest fields to fill at onboarding, which matters on a registry where 'No licence record exists' is described as 'the majority case at cold start'. | Removes a stated role need: 16.01.06's Role Lens gives the Producer read access to PAT status specifically because 'they are the person plugging in a client's amp — and liability lands on them if it goes wrong'. Dropping the slot deletes that surface without a decision to delete it.; Saves nothing structurally — the slot is one declaration with an expiry, sharing the same expiry engine as the other four. | no |

**Recommendation**: Lock the slots, defer the instrument names — The slot set, the issuer requirement, the expiry semantics and the declared-not-verified rule are product decisions and are all decidable now. The instrument names are per-state legal facts that no ideation source contains, and asserting them here would be an unsourced claim wearing a ratified decision's clothes. *(precedent: D-32 itself performs this split: it locks the market and then hands the parameterization work forward — '`/create-prd-security` keeps the data model jurisdiction-parameterized so later international expansion is additive, not a rewrite.' Same handoff, same recipient stage.)*

#### DQ-14.3 — 3. Where external-register availability is modelled (this is the axis that actually carries the downstream weight)  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is the availability of an external statutory register modelled as a per-regime-profile capability, as a per-authority fact resolved per record, or not modelled at all?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Per-profile capability flag** | Each regime profile declares whether a matchable public register exists. 16.05.02's L2 rung and 16.05.07 D-08's decisive key are offered or withheld on that flag. | One boolean; trivially implementable; makes the capability difference visible instead of implicit. | Contradicted by 16.05.01's own source row, which describes licensing/premises registers as 'Public records; patchy machine access; per-authority formats; terms vary by territory'. Availability already varies below the jurisdiction level inside the launch-adjacent market, so a single per-jurisdiction boolean would be wrong in the UK's own terms and is unlikely to be right in the US's. | no |
| **Per-authority capability, resolved per record** | The profile names the register class that applies; actual availability resolves per licensing authority for the record's address. L2 and D-08 gate on the resolved value, and an unresolved value renders as explicit unknown, never as available. Whether any given US jurisdiction has such a register is an empirical finding for `/create-prd-security`, not something this decision asserts. | Matches how the sources already describe registers — 'per-authority formats' (16.05.01) and D-15's re-ingest cadence 'monthly per source per region', so per-region source coverage is NOT already modelled — 16.05.01 D-15 sets an ingest cadence scoped by source and region, which is not a per-authority coverage record; this option therefore introduces new structure.; Keeps 16.05.02's authored degradation honest at the granularity it actually occurs: its edge case already says L2 'does not exist there' where no register exists, 'Stated rather than papered over'.; Avoids asserting an unverified empirical fact about US register availability in either direction. | A per-authority coverage record must be maintained, and at cold start most authorities will be unresolved — meaning L2 is unavailable in practice for most records at launch even where a register exists.; 16.05.07's Tier 1 review load is unpredictable until coverage is known, so the operational cost of D-08's absence cannot be sized during ideation. | no |
| **Not modelled — declare L2 and D-08 unavailable at launch** | The decision ratifies that the launch market has no usable public premises-licence register, so 16.05.02's L2 rung is removed and 16.05.07 D-08's refutation rule is switched off for v1. | Simplest possible answer; removes two conditional paths from the claim ladder and the dedup detector. | Unsourced. No cited file states that the US lacks per-premises public records. 16.02.01 Q-01 says only that US occupancy 'is derived from floor area and egress, not granted as a figure' — a statement about how the ceiling is computed, not about register availability. Ratifying the wider claim would lock an empirical assertion the ideation tree does not support.; Irreversible in the wrong direction: switching D-08 off deletes the only rule the specs call decisive ('inequality refutes and overrides everything'), and reinstating it later means reopening the merge model. | no |

**Recommendation**: Per-authority capability, resolved per record — It is the only option that neither asserts an unverified fact about US registers nor pretends registers are uniform within a jurisdiction, and it is the granularity 16.05.01 already uses for the same sources. It also keeps 16.05.01 D-05/D-13's writer rule coherent: statutory class has exactly one permitted writer ('Only a register may write these'; 'a `seeded`-class source may never write a statutory-class field'), so where no register resolves, the statutory class has NO writer and those fields can exist only as owner declarations carrying the declared-not-verified label. That consequence must be written into the profile definition — it is the real structural effect of a US-first launch, and it is what 16.02.01's `Claimed` state already describes ('no licence record to check against … No contradiction detection is possible in this state'). *(precedent: 16.02.06's Unknown state — 'Status unknown (the default for every seeded record) \| "Unknown" must be visible as unknown, not absent' — and 16.05.02's stated-rather-than-papered-over treatment of the no-register territory. Unresolved availability renders as unknown, never as available.)*

#### DQ-14.4 — 4. The temporary-permission analogue in a profile with no per-venue statutory licence (16.02.03 D-02)  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Where a profile has no per-venue statutory licence condition, does the statutory temporary-permission exception exist at all? (Note: the provenance fallback is NOT open — 16.02.03 D-01 already decides it.)**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **No statutory temporary permission where there is no statutory condition** | The US profile states that no per-venue statutory curfew condition exists, so there is nothing statutory to extend. Curfew and dB limits are Operator claims labelled as claims (16.02.03 D-01), and any date-range variation is an ordinary typed exception under 16.02.03 D-05 carrying Operator provenance. | Costs no new machinery: D-05's closed vocabulary already provides 'date range' as one of exactly three condition types, and D-02's rationale is that a temporary permission 'costs no new machinery — a TEN **is** a date-range exception with statutory provenance'. Removing the statutory provenance leaves the exception type intact.; Keeps the field machine-resolvable, which 16.07 depends on.; Resolves 16.02.03 Q-03 ('Does the platform track an Operator's remaining temporary-permission allowance?') for the US profile without a separate decision — there is no statutory allowance to track. | 16.06.03 D-22 ('The premises licence is a hard ceiling on overrun the Operator cannot lift in the app') has no input in this profile, so overrun becomes governed by an Operator-declared time. D-22 is not contradicted — it simply does not bind where no licence-derived ceiling exists — but the specs must say so rather than leave a rule pointing at a field that is never populated.; A real US venue may still be bound by a municipal ordinance the platform now cannot represent as statutory. | no |
| **Map a US analogue into the statutory slot** | The US profile identifies an equivalent instrument (for example a special-event permit or noise variance) and treats it as a statutory-provenance date-range exception, mirroring the TEN. | Preserves D-22's hard ceiling on overrun with a real statutory source and keeps 16.02.03 D-02 fully alive in the launch market. | No such US analogue is named anywhere in the ideation tree. Choosing this commissions research plus a per-municipality permission model that no source defines.; Reopens 16.02.03 Q-03 (tracking remaining allowance) as a live question in the launch market, which the source already calls out as 'modelling a statutory allowance the platform cannot verify, in a regime that differs per jurisdiction'. | **yes** |
| **Leave the exception type undefined in the US profile** | The profile is silent on temporary permissions; implementers decide later. | Defers a small question with no immediate consumer. | Breaks 16.02.03 D-05's closed vocabulary of 'exactly three condition types' — the source explicitly refuses 'no general rule engine, no free-text caveat' precisely because 16.07 needs the field 'machine-resolvable'. An undefined type reintroduces the free-text hole D-05 exists to close.; Leaves 16.06.03 D-22 pointing at an unspecified field. | no |

**Recommendation**: No statutory temporary permission where there is no statutory condition — 16.02.03 D-01 already ratifies the provenance outcome, so the only genuinely open part is whether the statutory exception type survives — and it cannot, because a statutory exception with no statutory source is a contradiction. The date-range exception itself survives unchanged under D-05, so nothing is lost mechanically. The one thing that must be written down explicitly is that 16.06.03 D-22 has no input in a licence-less profile. *(precedent: 16.02.03 D-01 — 'Curfew and dB limits are **licence-derived with statutory provenance** where a licence exists; where none exists they are Operator claims **explicitly labelled as claims**, never silently promoted.' The direction is already set for the fallback; this option only extends the same logic to the exception type.)*

That line must be corrected under every option.

| **A3-b — Infer provision: presence from the register, price from 16.06.07's extras, BYO from an empty register** | No provision field in 16. An empty register renders `unstated`, not `none` (16.02.02 States, 'Read-through unavailable'), so `bring-your-own-only` is unassertable and collapses into a gap — the conflation 16.02.02 D-04 forbids.; 'Included' as the absence of a rate line is indistinguishable from an Operator who has not built a rate card. 16.06.07's Behavior lists 'inclusions (what the rate covers)' but structures only the extras taxonomy as `(item, price, unit)` rows; there is no per-gear-item `included` value defined there.; 16.06.07's extras taxonomy carries only the hire leg ('Gear / backline hire \| Rhodes, valve amp, extra mic pair \| per-item per-session'). BYO has no representation anywhere in the sources.; Makes the rehearsal room invisible on its own headline filter: a room that genuinely has no kit and says so cannot be distinguished from one that has not filled the sheet. | no |
| **A3-c — Extend 16.06.07 to own a per-item inclusion enum including a BYO value** | 16.06.07's inclusion element is structured per gear item with values `included \| hire-extra \| by-request \| bring-your-own`, per 16.03.02 D-05's assignment of commercial inclusion to that feature. 16.04 types nothing; the room page renders 15's items joined to 16.06.07's inclusion facet. | Single owner for the commercial facet across all three room types, which is what 16.03.02 D-05 states.; Keeps 16.04 to the five fields 16.04 D-01 argues for. | 16.06.07 as written does not define a per-gear-item inclusion enum — its structured content is the extras taxonomy `(item, price, unit)` plus a `mandatory \| optional \| compound-implied` flag. ### DQ-16

> **File**: `19-ticketing-box-office/19.04-door-scanning-access-control/19.04.01-ticket-scan-validation.md` · **Domain**: `19` · **4 open / 3 locked**

**Two [DEEP] files give the door scanner opposite rules for an old-but-present manifest replica — you are deciding whether an old replica can stop a scanner from opening at all, and, separately, what the warning number and its override scope are.**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer building the door scanner's readiness state machine cannot tell what a device holding a three-hour-old replica does. 19.04.01 D-06 says it opens and shows an amber warning ('Staleness never blocks the door; only a **missing** replica blocks'). 19.04.02 D-06 plus its States row 'Stale (block)' say it cannot enter ready at all ('Replica older than the hard block threshold (120 min) \| Blocking — ... must re-sync to open'). Both files are [DEEP], both are cited from the sub-domain seam, and the two files also disagree on the amber number (60 vs 30 min) and on the override scope (per-show vs per-venue). Until this is settled nobody can build the readiness state table, the pre-doors provisioning screen, the threshold configuration surface, or the scan-side amber verdict — and 19.04.02 Q-03, which defers ratification of the numbers to /create-prd-compile, cannot be answered because it does not know which pair of numbers it is ratifying.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| 1. Does staleness ever turn a scan into a refusal (per-scan verdict axis)? | 19.04.01-ticket-scan-validation.md D-02 + D-06 + States row 'Warning'. D-02: 'Default posture on replica/server disagreement is admit-and-reconcile'. States row: '\| Warning \| Accepted but notable (comp no-show pattern, re-entry, stale replica) \| Amber verdict with the reason \|'. 19.04.02 never contradicts this — its 'Stale (block)' row blocks *readiness*, and no text in 19.04.02 converts a ready device's scan into a refusal. Domain ticketing-box-office-cx.md CX-14 synthesis Q5 agrees: 'resolved by never auto-refusing a legitimate holder on a stale replica (avoid false counterfeit accusations, DT-04)'. |
| 2. Does a missing or incomplete replica block the scanner? | 19.04.02-offline-manifest-sync-reconciliation.md D-10: 'A device with no valid replica is blocked from scanning — there is no permissive "admit everyone" fallback.' Agreed verbatim by 19.04.01 States row 'Not ready': 'Replica not downloaded (no replica at all) \| Blocking state before doors ... Only a **missing** replica blocks; staleness never does (D-06)'. 19.04.02's 'Incomplete replica (blocking)' row extends the same rule to a truncated download. |
| 7. Does the 19.05.01 offline-alarm freshness threshold inherit whatever number is chosen here? | 19.05.01-live-count-manifest-state.md Not-product line: 'real-time count propagation (websocket / polling / edge caching), snapshot-isolation mechanism, and the exact staleness threshold value are architecture → /create-prd-architecture.' |

#### DQ-16.1 — 3. Can a present-but-stale replica block a device from becoming ready to scan? (the actual contradiction)  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does an old-but-complete replica ever stop the scanner opening — never (19.04.01), past a hard threshold (19.04.02), or past a hard threshold that the Operator can override on the record?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — Staleness never blocks. Only a missing or incomplete replica blocks.** | One structural rule for the sub-domain: a present, complete replica of any age enters ready and produces normal accept / refuse verdicts, with an amber 'stale' state raised before doors and immediately whenever the device reached the server and learned inventory changed after its replica's timestamp. 19.04.02's States row 'Stale (block)' and its 120-minute hard threshold are deleted; the 'Stale (warn)' row survives, with its trigger number set by axis 4. 19.04.02 Q-03 narrows from ratifying two numbers to ratifying one. | It is the only position on the record formed with the opposing numbers in hand. 19.04.01 DT-04's Source column reads 'Instinct that a validation system must not decide on out-of-date data; the staleness threshold in 19.04.02', and its Outcome is '❌ REJECTED'. Choosing B reverses a considered Deep Think outcome rather than filling a gap.; DT-04's asymmetry argument is on the record and nothing in either file rebuts it: 'Because admit-and-reconcile (DT-02) already absorbs the refund case, a stale replica still produces correct verdicts for the overwhelming majority; its *only* real error is falsely refusing a ticket **issued after** the sync — rare next to the near-certainty that a basement door cannot re-sync on demand.'; Consistent with the sub-domain's founding constraint, index D-02: 'Offline-capable is a precondition, not a feature ... An online-only door does not work in the target market at all.'; The domain-level cross-cut already leans this way on the number: CX-14 synthesis Q1 states 'The replica's freshness gate (amber at 60 min, D-06) bounds the exposure' — i.e. the domain reconciliation records a *warning* gate, not a blocking one. (It does not adjudicate this axis; see axis 1.); The same instinct was independently rejected one sub-domain over — 19.05.01 DT-03: 'Refusing is the loud-offline anti-pattern wearing a safety costume.' That rejection is about the offline count view, not the door, so it is corroborating character rather than governing precedent. | Removes the only forcing function that makes an Operator re-sync. 19.04.02's cross-cut note to 19.06.01: refunds after sync 'are the main source of replica staleness with money attached; they set the staleness threshold's stakes (D-06)'. Under A the threshold sets the stakes for a colour change.; Loses the bound 19.04.02 D-06 explicitly claims: '30/120 keeps the manifest at most ~2h behind money movements while not blocking a normally patchy door.' Nothing replaces it.; No file defines any escalation from an ignored amber, so the Operator's only remaining incentive to re-sync before doors is the warning itself. | no |
| **B — Staleness blocks readiness past a hard threshold (19.04.02 D-06 as written).** | A device whose replica is older than the hard threshold cannot enter ready and cannot scan until it re-provisions. 19.04.01 D-06 and DT-04's outcome are rewritten to say staleness blocks readiness before doors but never converts a ready device's scan into a refusal (axis 1 stands either way). 19.04.01's 'Not ready' States row gains the stale trigger. | Preserves a hard bound on how far the manifest may lag money movements — the stake 19.04.02 assigns to refunds in its 19.06.01 cross-cut note and that domain CX-14 echoes ('they set the stakes for the D-06 staleness threshold').; 19.04.02 owns the readiness gate — 'Readiness is gated on replica freshness, not on connectivity (19.04.01 D-04)' — so under author-where-owned this keeps the rule in the file with jurisdiction (see axis 6).; The blocking state is not exit-less. 19.04.02 names two fixes for a blocking readiness screen — 'get signal and sync, or receive the manifest from a ready device nearby' — and DT-06 commits to 'make the *provisioning* robust — peer transfer of the manifest (DT-03)'.; 19.04.02 DT-06 already accepts a pre-doors blocking state on the reasoning 'A blocked scanner before doors is a fixable inconvenience; an uncounted room mid-show is an unfixable liability.' | Reinstates precisely what 19.04.01 DT-04 rejected after reading these exact numbers, with no counter-argument anywhere in the tree.; D-10's justification does not extend to it. D-10/DT-06 rest on 'a device with no manifest cannot tell a valid ticket from a forged one'. A 121-minute replica still distinguishes valid from forged for every ticket issued before its sync, so the reasoning that licenses the missing-replica block does not license this one.; Both defined exits are weaker than they look here. 'Get signal and sync' presumes signal, which the feature's own premise denies ('connectivity at music venues is bad, and not incidentally bad — structurally bad'). Peer transfer only helps if some peer is fresher, and the spec's own provisioning model syncs every device together before doors ('Before doors, Operator opens the scanner on each device while still in signal') — so peers age together. Peer transfer is also not itself a D-NN decision: 19.04.02's Not-product note routes 'peer discovery, local transport' to /create-prd-architecture and Q-01 asks whether peer gossip is worth its complexity at all.; No source defines an Operator override of readiness. 19.04.01 D-05 — 'Operator override of a refusal is permitted and recorded' — overrides a refusal, not a readiness state. So in the case where neither exit is available, no file says how the door opens. | no |
| **C — Staleness blocks the pre-doors ready transition only, and that block is Operator-overridable with a recorded reason.** | One rule with two moments. Before doors, a replica past the hard threshold refuses the ready transition — the fixable window 19.04.02 is protecting. An Operator may still open the scanner by recording an override (who, why, replica age). Once scanning has started, staleness only ever warns. Both State tables gain a blocking-but-overridable state. | Honours both stakes as literally written: 19.04.02's 'A device with a **stale** replica warns before doors, while the problem is still fixable' keeps a forcing function, and DT-04's 'a basement door cannot re-sync on demand' keeps a way out that does not depend on signal or on a fresher peer.; Extends an existing pattern rather than a foreign one — recorded override is 19.04.01 D-05, whose stated context is the 'same attribution-not-prevention posture as 19.03.03 D-01', which 19.04.02 DT-01 independently calls 'the domain's actual character rather than a local choice'.; Leaves an audit record of every stale opening, which is exactly what 19.04.02 D-01 ('Reconciliation produces evidence, not repair') and the close (D-04) are built to consume. | An always-overridable block does not deliver the ~2h bound it exists for; it only makes exceeding it deliberate.; It does not settle axis 4 or axis 5 by itself — it needs both a warn number and a hard number.; Adds a readiness state neither State table contains, so 19.04.01's 'Not ready' row and 19.04.02's 'Stale (block)' row are both rewritten rather than kept or deleted. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: A — Staleness never blocks. — A is the only position formed with the opposing numbers in view: 19.04.01 DT-04 names 'the staleness threshold in 19.04.02' as its Source and rejects it, so B reverses a Deep Think outcome using an argument the tree does not contain. Its core reasoning survives re-reading — because 19.04.01 D-02 admit-and-reconcile already absorbs the refunded-after-sync case, a stale replica's only residual error is falsely refusing a ticket issued after sync, and a hard block converts that rare false-refusal into a total one at exactly the venues index D-02 says the sub-domain exists for. B's weakness is narrower than the earlier draft of this entry claimed, and the correction matters: 19.04.02 does define two exits from a blocking readiness screen (sync in signal, or peer manifest transfer), so the block is not literally exit-less. But both exits are conditional — one on signal the premise denies, one on a fresher peer that a fleet provisioned together before doors will usually not have — and what genuinely has no definition anywhere is an Operator override of readiness. C is the only forcing function with an unconditional exit and is the right fallback if you want the manifest lag bounded, but it commissions a readiness-override authority no source grants and still does not deliver the bound.

#### DQ-16.2 — 4. What is the amber 'stale' warning default — 30 or 60 minutes?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Which single number is the shippable amber default: 30 minutes (19.04.02 D-06) or 60 minutes (19.04.01 D-06, echoed by domain CX-14)?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **60 minutes (19.04.01 D-06).** | 'Amber "stale" warns at a default 60 min since last successful sync' becomes the sub-domain default; 19.04.02's 'Stale (warn)' row trigger changes from 'older than 30 min' to 60. 19.04.02 Q-03 stays open at /create-prd-compile as ratification of that one number. | Domain ticketing-box-office-cx.md CX-14 already records it as the domain-level reconciliation: 'The replica's freshness gate (amber at 60 min, D-06) bounds the exposure.'; 19.04.01's cross-cut note to 19.04.02 also carries it — 'the 60-minute staleness warning and no-block-on-stale posture (D-06) live at this seam' — so 60 is the number written at the seam itself. | Under axis 3 = A it is the only signal the Operator gets, and a longer fuse means a later first warning.; 19.04.02 chose 30 as half of a 30/120 pair; taking 60 alone discards the ratio that pair was reasoned as. | no |
| **30 minutes (19.04.02 D-06).** | The amber default stays at 30 minutes; 19.04.01 D-06's '60 min' is corrected to 30. Compatible with any answer on axis 3. | Warns earlier, which is the whole point of a warning the Operator can only act on before doors ('warns before doors, while the problem is still fixable').; Keeps the number in the file that owns replica lifecycle, so axis 6's authoring move requires no renumbering. | Contradicts the domain-level CX-14 detail, which already states 60 — so this choice creates one more edit and one more place the reconciliation must be re-recorded.; A tighter amber on a fleet that is normally offline risks the exact desensitisation 19.04.01 DT-03 warns about ('An alarming offline indicator trains the Operator to distrust a system that is working perfectly'). | no |
| **Strike both defaults and let /create-prd-compile set the number.** | Neither file states a default; 19.04.02 Q-03 is rewritten from 'ratify these numbers' to 'set this number'. | Avoids pre-empting a question 19.04.02 already routed out: Q-03 → '/create-prd-compile (performance/ops budget)'. | Contradicts Q-03's own framing, which presupposes a default exists: 'Default is specified and shippable; this is user ratification of the numbers, not a blocker.' Striking the default makes it a blocker.; Leaves the amber state with no trigger, so the States rows in both files become unimplementable. | no |

**Recommendation**: 60 minutes. — It is the only number that already appears at both the seam (19.04.01's cross-cut note) and at domain level (CX-14 Q1), so choosing it reconciles three files by editing one row, while choosing 30 requires correcting the domain CX as well. Either way, 19.04.02 Q-03 survives as numeric ratification at /create-prd-compile — this axis only fixes which default it ratifies. If axis 3 = B or C, the hard number (120 min) is also in scope and no source offers an alternative to it.

#### DQ-16.3 — 5. Is the threshold overridable per show, per venue, or both?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**At what scope is the staleness threshold configurable — per show (19.04.01: 'configurable per show'), per venue (19.04.02: 'per-venue overridable defaults'), or a venue default that a show may override?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Per show only (19.04.01).** | One override scope: the threshold is set on the show. | Matches what the warning actually prices once axis 3 = A. DT-04 relocates the risk to newly-issued tickets and closes with 'the sync discipline is "sync after the last comp/transfer/sales batch," not "sync as late as physically possible"' — comp, transfer and sales batches are show-level events.; One scope, one config surface, no precedence rule to define. | Ignores the property 19.04.02 was pricing. Its threshold trades against 'basement reality' — connectivity, which is a venue attribute, not a show attribute.; A venue that runs 200 nights a year must set the same override 200 times. | no |
| **Per venue only (19.04.02).** | One override scope: the threshold is set on the venue. | Matches the property a hard block prices — connectivity is structural to the room ('Basements, thick walls, load-in doors round the back'), so if axis 3 = B or C this is the right scope for the hard number.; Set once per room; survives every show in it. | Mismatched to the warning's stake under axis 3 = A, where the risk DT-04 identifies is post-sync issuance — a show property.; A one-off show in a normally fine room with an unusually late comp batch cannot be tightened. | no |
| **Venue default, show override.** | The venue carries the default; an individual show may override it. Requires stating a precedence rule (show beats venue). | The two scopes price different things and are not mutually exclusive — connectivity is a venue property, post-sync issuance is a show property — so layering them loses neither stake.; Set-once ergonomics for a resident Operator, with an escape for the unusual night. | No source defines a precedence rule between the two scopes; choosing this writes one.; Two config surfaces to build and one more state for the Operator to reason about, against 19.04.02 D-05 ('The Operator thinks about sync once, before doors, and never again'). | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: Per show — conditional on axis 3 = A. — This axis's answer depends on axis 3 and should be taken after it, not with it. If staleness only ever warns (A), the thing being scoped is a warning whose stake DT-04 explicitly relocates to newly-issued tickets — comp, transfer and sales batches, all show-level — so per-show is the matching scope. If staleness blocks (B or C), the blocking threshold prices connectivity and belongs on the venue, and per-venue should be taken instead. 'Venue default, show override' is the only option that serves both stakes, but it is the one option here that writes a rule no source contains.
