### DQ-17

> **File**: `19-ticketing-box-office/19.05-box-office-counts-drops/19.05.01-live-count-manifest-state.md` · **Domain**: `19` · **3 open / 1 locked**

**A walk-up buyer is admitted at the window without ever being scanned, and no ratified decision says which canonical counter that admission moves — the owner must decide where it is stored, what the resulting all-bodies-admitted number is called, and what a walk-up refund does to it.**

**Why it blocks**: 19.05.04 (Happy Path step 4, D-11, DT-03, the skim edge case) writes to a counter called `admitted` that does not exist anywhere else in the tree — it is absent from 19.05.01's canonical counter table, absent from 19.05.01's provenance sentence (which enumerates writers exhaustively and says 'scanned from device reconciliation (19.04.03)'), absent from the 19.05 sub-domain index's vocabulary ('sold, comped, scanned, held, remaining'), absent from 19.05.05's certified statement line list, and absent from .memory/wiki/decisions.md (verified by grep — zero hits for walk-up/admitted). An implementer cannot build 19.05.01's count schema (how many counters, what each is named, which are stored and which derived), cannot write the D-03 alarm predicate, cannot write the occupancy figure that domain 16's capacity/curfew and 17.09.02's INV-02/INV-08 read, and cannot implement 19.05.04's at-window refund, because two ratified files describe the same physical event with two different counters and neither defers to the other.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| Axis 0 — Does the canonical count carry a single all-bodies-admitted total at all (vs. leaving walk-up admissions out of every room-level counter)? | ticketing-box-office-cx.md CX-04 (domain cross-cut, [DEEP], last updated 2026-07-18 — later than both 19.05.01 and 19.05.04 at 2026-07-17), verified verbatim: "The reconciled, deduplicated scanned count is simultaneously the fire officer's capacity number and settlement's admitted number." Reinforced by 17.09.02-box-office-count-reconciliation.md D-08 ("Three counts, not two: `sold` -> money; `scanned_paid` -> draw; `scanned_total` -> merch per-head and occupancy") whose six-number table defines row 5 as "**Scanned — paid** \| Paying customers who came through the door" and row 6 as "**Scanned — total** \| Every body admitted, paid or comped", and states "Walk-ups are not a seventh number — they are **sold units acquired at the window on the night** (19.05.04)". A walk-up buyer is by definition a paying customer who came through the door and a body admitted, so the canonical count must contain a number they are inside. Also constrained by 19.05.01 D-01: "The count is one canonical state with distinct counters, not a per-consumer computation." CONSEQUENCE: the original entry's Option C (walk-up admission is a ticket-record property only; no aggregate room counter moves) is foreclosed — not by arithmetic, but because it makes 17.09.02 D-08 row 6 and CX-04 false by construction. NOTE — the original entry's stated reason for foreclosing C was wrong and is withdrawn: it claimed INV-02 (`scanned_total <= sold + comps`) and INV-08 (`occupancy_count >= scanned_total`) would 'fail by construction on every walk-up show'. Both are inequalities that an under-count satisfies more easily, not less; they would pass vacuously while measuring nothing. The correct objection is definitional, not arithmetic. |

#### DQ-17.1 — Axis 1 — Storage shape: is the window admission written into the existing `scanned` counter, or into its own stored counter with the canonical admissions total derived from both?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does a walk-up admit-at-birth increment the existing `scanned` counter (one stored counter, two provenance sources), or its own stored window-admission counter with the canonical all-bodies total expressed as a derived counter in 19.05.01's table?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A1 — Merge: the window admission increments `scanned`, whose provenance widens from one source to two** | There is no separate admission counter. 19.05.01's `scanned` is the canonical admissions counter — already glossed there as "Physically walked in, deduplicated across gates" and in 19.03.02 D-02 as "walked through the door", neither of which says a barcode was validated. Its provenance sentence widens from "scanned from device reconciliation (19.04.03)" to two writers: gate scans (19.04.03) and window issue-and-admit (19.05.04), carried as a per-source breakdown under 19.05.01 D-02 ("Every counter carries a source and a freshness; blended counts show a per-source breakdown, never a silent average"). 19.05.04 Happy Path step 4 becomes `paid += 1`, `remaining -= 1`, `scanned += 1` in one atomic transaction. D-11's un-admission becomes an additive linked reversal against `scanned` using 19.04.01 D-07 verbatim ("the original scan is never deleted; the counted total is net (accepts - reversals)"). 19.05.04 D-01's "never scanned" must be restated as "never validated" — which is what DT-01 actually argues ("it is never *validated* — it is admitted at birth"). | One stored number for the fire officer, 16's capacity/curfew and 17.09.02's occupancy comparison — nothing to sum, nothing to keep in step.; Preserves ticketing-box-office-cx.md CX-04 verbatim: the sentence names `scanned` as settlement's admitted number, and under this option that stays literally true.; Both mechanisms it needs are already ratified and were built for these jobs: the per-source breakdown (19.05.01 D-02) and the additive netting reversal (19.04.01 D-07).; 19.07.04's door floor `paid >= scanned` and 19.07.03's no-show-band reasoning ("scanned will always be below paid because of no-shows") stay true, because a walk-up moves paid and scanned together in one transaction. | Contradicts 19.05.04 D-01 as written ("walk-up tickets are admitted at birth, never scanned") and requires restating it, plus rewriting D-11, DT-03, the at-window refund row and the skim edge case, all of which name `admitted`.; Collapses two ratified rules that point opposite ways onto one counter: 19.05.01's refund edge case ("If the ticket was already scanned, the refund does not reduce scanned — the person was in the room") and 19.05.04 D-11's decrement. They must be re-scoped as two conditional rules on one number — the exact 'the one place someone forgets' hazard 19.03.02 DT-02 names.; 19.05.04's skim signal is defined as "an unrung admission diverges `admitted` from `paid`"; on a merged counter that comparison must be restated as a source-filtered read (window-sourced admissions vs walk-up paid), not a whole-counter comparison.; Any 'a scanner device saw this' read — per-gate/device throughput, device reconciliation completeness (19.04.02 D-04) — must filter the counter by source. (Scoped correction: this does NOT weaken the double-scan fraud signal, which rests on the ticket being strictly use-once per 19.04.03 D-01 and operates over per-ticket scan records; a walk-up ticket has no barcode issued for presentation and is admitted exactly once by its creating device, so it can never produce a duplicate-scan record.) | no |
| **A2 — Split with a derived canonical total: `scanned` stays gate-only, window admits are their own stored counter, and the all-bodies total is a derived counter in the same table** | 19.05.01's counter table gains a stored counter for window admissions (19.05.04's `admitted`, provenance = window issue-and-admit, freshness = the selling device's sync state) and a derived counter for the canonical total (= gate scans net of reversals + window admits net of un-admissions), modelled exactly as `Remaining` already is: 19.05.01's table carries "\| **Remaining** \| Sellable right now (= capacity - killed - held - paid - comp) \| The storefront (as a boolean to Fans) \|" and D-08 makes it "decompose on demand into free vs recoverable-from-holds". The derived total is the number the fire officer, 16's curfew check, 19.05.05's statement and 17's occupancy/draw all read; no consumer computes it. `scanned` keeps its gate-device meaning for device and gate reporting. 19.05.04 D-01, D-11, DT-01, DT-03 and the skim edge case survive verbatim. | Contradicts no ratified decision text in 19.05.04 — D-01 ("never scanned"), D-11 ("decrementing `admitted`"), DT-03 and the skim edge case ("an unrung admission diverges `admitted` from `paid`") all stand exactly as written, with their cross-cut notes to 16 and 24 intact.; The two conflicting refund rules never collide: 19.05.01's "the refund does not reduce scanned" applies to the gate counter, 19.05.04 D-11's decrement applies to the window counter, and the derived total composes both correctly with no conditional rule anyone can forget.; Satisfies 19.05.01 D-01: the total is canonical state, not a per-consumer computation. D-01 forbids consumers computing their own count; it does not forbid derived counters, and the table already contains one.; Applies 19.03.02 DT-02's own test rather than overriding it — DT-02 rejects one counter with a type flag because the parts have "different *arrival times* and different *consumers*"; a gate scan (validated at the lane, on the night) and a window admit-at-birth (unvalidated, at the moment of sale) differ on both.; Leaves 19.05.01 D-03's alarm (`scanned > paid + comp`) operating on a gate-pure counter, which is what its rationale is about ("a double-scan (19.04.03) or a counterfeit"). | Makes ticketing-box-office-cx.md CX-04 false as written — `scanned` would no longer be settlement's admitted number — so the domain cross-cut (the later-dated file, 2026-07-18) has to be edited to name the derived total.; 19.05.01's counter table grows from six rows to eight, and the Musician Role Lens ("On the night, sees scanned vs paid") must be re-pointed at the derived total or the artist reads a room-fullness number that omits 20-50% of the gate (19.05.04 Overview: walk-up is "routinely 20-50% of the gate").; The derived total needs a stated freshness rule (it is only as fresh as its stalest input) — a small definition D-02 does not spell out for derived counters, though `Remaining` faces the same question today.; Every downstream reference to `scanned` as the occupancy/draw number (17.09.02 D-08 rows 5 and 6, INV-02, INV-03, INV-08; 17.11.01; 19.05.05's statement line) must be re-pointed at the derived total, which is the same rebinding work Axis 2 prices. | no |

**Recommendation**: A2 — split with a derived canonical total — Both options land the walk-up inside the admissions family, which Axis 0 already requires, so the choice is purely about which body of ratified text gets rewritten. A1 preserves one sentence (CX-04) and rewrites 19.05.04's D-01, D-11, DT-03, the at-window-refund row, the skim edge case and two cross-cut notes; A2 preserves all of those verbatim and rewrites one cross-cut sentence plus a set of downstream name bindings that Axis 2 has to touch under either option anyway. A2 also avoids the only genuinely dangerous residual in A1: two opposite refund rules living as scoped conditionals on a single counter, which is precisely the failure mode 19.03.02 DT-02 rejects a type-flagged counter for ("a single counter with a flag makes every consumer filter correctly every time — and the one place someone forgets is the place the artist is underpaid"). Under A2 the composition is arithmetic, not a rule anyone applies. *(precedent: 19.05.01's own counter table already contains a derived canonical counter — "\| **Remaining** \| Sellable right now (= capacity - killed - held - paid - comp) \|" — with a named consumer, and D-08 ("Remaining decomposes on demand into free vs recoverable-from-holds for the Operator") establishes that a derived counter in this table is expected to decompose into its parts on demand. That is the exact shape A2 needs, in the exact file. This precedent points toward A2 and away from the original entry's claim that a derived counter is machinery 'no source defines'.)*

#### DQ-17.2 — Axis 2 — Vocabulary: what is the canonical all-bodies-admitted number called, and does domain 17's `scanned_paid`/`scanned_total` vocabulary rebind to it?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the canonical admissions total keep the name `scanned` (with 17.09.02's `scanned_paid`/`scanned_total` and 19.07.03/19.07.04's comparisons unchanged), or is it renamed to `admissions_total`/`admissions_paid` with `scanned` demoted to the gate-observed source or sub-counter?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Keep `scanned` as the name of the canonical admissions total** | The word `scanned` continues to name the number that includes bodies no scanner ever saw. 17.09.02 D-08's six-number table, INV-02, INV-03, INV-04, INV-08, 17.11.01's draw record, 19.07.03's discrepancy reasoning, 19.07.04's `paid >= scanned` door floor, 19.05.05's statement line and 19.05.01's Musician Role Lens all stand textually unchanged. | Zero rename churn across domains 17 and 19; every existing invariant and comparison reads correctly without edit.; Preserves ticketing-box-office-cx.md CX-04 word-for-word.; 17.09.02 D-08's row 6 already glosses the name against its literal meaning — "**Scanned — total** \| Every body admitted, paid or comped" — so the tree has already accepted `scanned` as a label for an admissions concept. | The name is literally false for 20-50% of the gate at the beachhead venues (19.05.04 Overview), which is precisely the kind of name-vs-meaning gap that produces the wrong filter downstream.; Under Axis 1 = A2 the name is actively ambiguous: `scanned` would name both the gate-only stored counter and the total, so one of the two must be renamed regardless.; 19.07.04's door floor is written as "`paid >= scanned` when WeJammin ran the door" — a BE implementer reading only that line will bind it to whichever counter carries the name, and under the merge (A1) that is now a different quantity than the one 19.04 produces. | no |
| **Rename the canonical total to `admissions_total` / `admissions_paid`, reserving `scanned` for gate-observed admissions** | 19.05.01's table names the canonical all-bodies number `admissions_total` (and, where the paid subset is needed, `admissions_paid`). `scanned` means what its name says: an admission a gate device validated. 17.09.02 D-08's rows 5 and 6 and INV-02/INV-03/INV-04/INV-08 rebind to `admissions_paid`/`admissions_total`; 17.11.01's draw record, 19.07.03's reconciliation basis, 19.07.04's door floor and Paid<scanned row, 19.05.05's statement line, the 19.05 index vocabulary line and CX-04 are restated with the new names. Source breakdown (gate vs window) is carried on the total per 19.05.01 D-02. | Every downstream comparison states which quantity it means, removing the filter-someone-forgets exposure at the point where it is cheapest to remove — the name.; Makes 19.07.03's and 19.07.04's door checks unambiguous: they compare an ingested paid number against WeJammin's independently observed *gate* count (19.07.03 DT-01: "the only count WeJammin independently observes is scanned"), which is `scanned`, not the total that includes the platform's own window sales.; Under Axis 1 = A2 the rename is not optional churn — two distinct quantities need two distinct names, and this is the smaller half of that work. | Touches ratified text in a second domain (17.09.02's table and four invariants, 17.11.01), which is cross-domain edit surface the owner is paying for a naming gain.; 17.09.02 D-08 is itself flagged as not fully settled — "Refines D-02 and contradicts 17.11.01 DT-01 — escalated, not unilaterally applied (Q-03)" — so renaming its vocabulary lands on top of an open question rather than a closed one.; Requires editing CX-04, the domain's most-cited count sentence. | no |

**Recommendation**: Rename the canonical total to `admissions_total` / `admissions_paid` — Under the recommended Axis 1 = A2 this is forced, not optional: two distinct quantities exist (gate-observed admissions and all admissions) and one name cannot carry both. It also fixes the one place the current text is genuinely load-bearing on the ambiguity — 19.07.03 DT-01 says WeJammin's *independently observed* count is the door scan, which after any merge is no longer the same quantity as a counter that includes WeJammin's own window sales; keeping one name for both would let a reconciliation compare a number against a component of itself. If the owner instead picks Axis 1 = A1, this axis flips to a genuine judgement call with no forcing argument, and keeping `scanned` is the cheaper answer.

#### DQ-17.3 — Axis 3 — Refund semantics: does a walk-up refund reduce the canonical admissions total, given that an already-scanned advance ticket's refund does not?  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is the canonical admissions total 'bodies that entered the room, ever' (so no refund reduces it and 19.05.04 D-11's un-admission is dropped), or 'bodies the venue is accountable for as present' (so an at-window un-admission reduces it while a post-scan online refund does not)?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Exit-scoped — an at-window un-admission reduces the total; a post-scan online refund does not** | Both existing rules survive, each scoped to what physically happened. 19.05.04 D-11 and DT-03 apply because the walk-up buyer refunded at the window minutes later either never entered or has left, so counting them over-states occupancy ("leaving `admitted` stale over-states occupancy for capacity, the statement, and the split"). 19.05.01's rule applies because a scanned advance ticket refunded online belongs to someone who was in the room: "the refund does not reduce scanned — the person was in the room; the discrepancy is a real fact the close must carry." | Keeps both ratified texts true; neither has to be withdrawn.; Keeps the safety number honest in both directions — the fire-officer/16-curfew figure neither over-states (walk-up refund) nor under-states (post-scan refund).; Under Axis 1 = A2 this needs no conditional rule at all: it falls out of the two stored counters composing. | Under Axis 1 = A1 it becomes a conditional on one counter — 'refunds reduce it only when the refund is a window un-admission' — which is the forgettable filter 19.03.02 DT-02 warns about.; The distinction must be written down explicitly somewhere; today it is implicit in two files that never reference each other on this point. | no |
| **Ever-entered — no refund of any kind reduces the total; 19.05.04 D-11 is withdrawn** | The total means 'bodies that were admitted at any point tonight'. A walk-up refund reverses `paid` and `remaining` (19.05.04 D-05) but leaves the admissions total alone; the discrepancy is carried as a close exception line under 19.05.05 D-05 ("Every line is distinct — no aggregation"). | One rule, no conditional, nothing to filter.; Consistent with 19.05.01's stated posture that a post-admission refund is "a real fact the close must carry" rather than an erasure. | Directly withdraws 19.05.04 D-11 and contradicts DT-03's ratified reasoning that leaving the admission stale "over-states occupancy for capacity, the statement, and the split (DT-03)".; Over-states a safety number: a buyer refunded at 20:05 who walked back out is still counted against capacity/curfew (16) all night.; Distorts 19.05.04's skim signal, which depends on the admission count tracking actual bodies rather than historical events. | no |

**Recommendation**: Exit-scoped — It is the only option that leaves both ratified statements standing, and 19.05.04 DT-03's reasoning is explicit that a stale admission count corrupts occupancy, the statement and the split. Its one weakness — becoming a forgettable conditional — exists only under Axis 1 = A1 and disappears entirely under the recommended A2, where the two stored counters compose the correct answer without anyone applying a rule.

**Affected files**: `19-ticketing-box-office/19.05-box-office-counts-drops/19.05.01-live-count-manifest-state.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.05-box-office-counts-drops/19.05.04-walk-up-day-of-show-sales.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.05-box-office-counts-drops/19.05.05-box-office-close-certified-statement.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.05-box-office-counts-drops/19.05-box-office-counts-drops-index.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.05-box-office-counts-drops/19.05-box-office-counts-drops-cx.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/ticketing-box-office-cx.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.04-door-scanning-access-control/19.04.01-ticket-scan-validation.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.04-door-scanning-access-control/19.04.03-multi-scanner-coordination-re-entry.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.07-external-ticketing-integration/19.07.03-count-discrepancy-reconciliation.md`, `19-ticketing-box-office/19.07-external-ticketing-integration/19.07.04-manual-count-entry-attestation.md`, `17-live-booking-settlement/17.09-settlement-reconciliation/17.09.02-box-office-count-reconciliation.md`, `17-live-booking-settlement/17.11-draw-history-market-intelligence/17.11.01-verified-draw-record.md`


### DQ-19

> **File**: `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04.03-digital-sales-name-your-price-bundles.md` · **Domain**: `20` · **3 open / 2 locked**

**Under name-your-price, when a fan pays £20 for a £5 record, the drummer's 10% is 10% of *something* — you are deciding where that 'something' rule lives (one platform-wide constant, one value per split agreement, or one value per shop listing), when it stops being changeable, and what the value is.**

**Why it blocks**: Two [DEEP] files give contradictory answers in their ratified Decisions tables, so an implementer cannot place the field. 20.04.03 D-07 says 'The NYP basis is captured and locked at listing' (body: 'captured on the product at listing, is immutable for the life of that listing'); 20.04.04 D-07 says 'Default split basis is paid-net, fixed at capture, immutable after first sale' (body: 'a field on the split agreement in domain 09, chosen at split-capture time'). The sub-domain CX file does not resolve it — CX-03 synthesis Q5 says outright 'The conflict is normative, and it is genuinely unresolved'. Until it is settled nobody can write the split-capture schema (does a split agreement carry a basis column?), the catalogue product schema (does a listing carry one?), the payout arithmetic (what number do percentages multiply), the payee statement line, or 20.04.03 D-10's fail-closed listing gate ('A listing cannot go live with an unresolved basis' — unresolved *where*). Domain 09 currently defines no payout-basis field anywhere, so the schema cannot be written from the specs as they stand.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| Ownership of the split percentages and the payout-plan source | 20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04.04-d2f-revenue-split-payout.md D-01: 'Splits are referenced from domain 09 read-only, never copied or store-authored \| A copied split drifts. A drifted split is the reconstruction failure the platform exists to prevent — committing it in our own storefront would be self-refuting. Store-side override rejected as not store-authored.' Reinforced by 20.04.01-storefront-product-catalog.md D-02: 'The split is referenced from domain 09, never copied; the plan **version at sale time** governs each sale.' |
| Disclosure timing — when the payee must be told which basis will be applied | Locked by the *agreement* of the two otherwise-conflicting decisions, so it survives whichever one is overturned. 20.04.03 D-07: '...and shown to every payee before they agree the split \| ... A basis discovered on a statement is a dispute (24); a basis shown before agreement is a contract.' 20.04.04 D-07 context: 'Deterministic and pre-agreed; never discovered on a statement.' 20.04.04 Q-01: 'Must be settled and shown **before** any collaborator agrees a split.' 20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront-cx.md CX-03 role scoping, Producer: 'Whichever way this resolves, they must be told *before* they agree to the split, not after the first payout.' Enforced at listing by 20.04.03 D-10: 'A listing cannot go live without: resolved basis, encoded formats, and (for multi-owner works) sale authority.' |

#### DQ-19.1 — AXIS 1 (open) — Granularity: how many distinct basis values can exist on the platform, and which record holds them  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is the ask/paid/paid-net basis a single platform-wide constant, one value per split agreement in domain 09, or one value per storefront listing?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **D — Platform-wide constant (one value for all of WeJammin)** | There is no basis field on any record. The platform states one rule — e.g. 'splits are applied to the amount actually paid, net of platform cut and processor fees' — as a term of the service. It is printed on the split-capture screen next to the percentages, on every product page, on the fan receipt and on every payee statement. Nobody chooses it per record, so there is nothing to lock per record; the only mutability question is the platform's own change policy (Axis 2, option L4). 20.04.03's contribution shrinks to display plus its existing D-10 gate, which under D can never fail for basis reasons. | Satisfies the locked disclosure axis by construction rather than by machinery: a constant printed on the capture screen is necessarily 'shown to every payee before they agree the split' (20.04.03 D-07) and necessarily 'applied to every payee identically' (20.04.03 body), with no consent step, no propagation and no ordering hazard.; Needs no new field in domain 09, so it does not collide with 09.02.01's hard design budget: '**it must be faster than not doing it**' and 'a 4-contributor sheet must be completable by the Producer in **≤ 8 interactions and ≤ 90 s** ... Any proposed addition to this flow is measured against the 8/90 budget; if it does not fit, it is not capture, it is paperwork.' A displayed constant costs zero interactions; a negotiated field costs at least one on the platform's single most budget-constrained screen.; Dissolves the no-rights-record case for free. 20.04.01 D-06 and 20.04.04 DT-02 specify listing with a recorded 'no split on file — you're taking 100%' acknowledgement; there is no split agreement there to hold a per-record field, so options A and C must specify that path separately. D does not.; Matches how both Q-01s are actually written — as one owner ruling on one default, not a per-record choice: 20.04.04 Q-01 'NYP basis — ask, paid, or paid-net-of-fees? Default specified as paid-net (D-07); confirm or reset. Must be settled and shown **before** any collaborator agrees a split', and 20.04.03's body 'whichever is chosen, the storefront's contract is identical'.; Sits in the same arithmetic, and the same disclosure surface, as the platform cut — which is already a platform-wide constant the owner sets once (20.04 index Q-01: 'Does the platform take a cut of D2F sales, and how much?') and which 20.04.03 D-02 already requires be shown to the fan ('Show the line from fan to artist, including our cut'). | No per-record variation is possible, ever — a charity listing on 'ask' or a one-off arrangement cannot be expressed without reopening this decision. (Stated honestly as a real loss, but note: no file in the tree requires per-record variation; grep for charity/donation across domain 20 returns only tax-treatment questions on tips and pledges, and 'per-listing'/'different basis' return nothing. The loss is of optionality, not of a specified requirement.); It contradicts how both authors explicitly modelled the thing: 20.04.03 D-07 'captured and locked at listing' and 20.04.04's 'a field on the split agreement in domain 09, chosen at split-capture time' both describe a per-record field. D overturns the *location* clause of both.; Creates one new obligation the specs do not currently cover: what happens if the platform ever changes the constant, when thousands of splits were agreed under the old disclosure. That needs a stated rule (Axis 2, option L4) — so D is the cheapest option, not a free one. The challenge's 'strictly cheaper than A, B and C' overstates it.; Concentrates a money rule in the platform's terms rather than in an agreement each payee signed, which is a weaker consent artefact if a payee later disputes it in domain 24. | no |
| **A/C — Per split agreement (a field in domain 09, set at split-capture time)** | The basis becomes a real column on the domain 09 split agreement, chosen at capture alongside the percentages and consented to by every ledger party in the same pass. Every listing of that recording references it read-only and displays it; the store never holds a copy. One recording carries exactly one basis, so a standalone download, a bundle and a re-listing after delisting all pay identically. (Whether it then locks at first sale or simply rides the plan version is Axis 2 — the A/C split.) | Can satisfy the locked disclosure axis honestly and with no chronology problem: the payee sees the basis on the same screen, at the same moment, as the percentages they are consenting to.; It is the lean the sub-domain CX file already records — 20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront-cx.md CX-03 synthesis Q3: 'Arguably the collaborator must consent to the basis rule, which makes it part of the split agreement rather than a store setting. That is the cleanest reading and the most work.' (An explicit lean, not a ratification; Q5 of the same section says the conflict 'is genuinely unresolved'.); Preserves 20.04.04 D-07's location clause verbatim, so the file that thought hardest about payout arithmetic does not have to be reopened.; Gives each payee a per-agreement consent artefact naming the basis, which is the strongest possible position in a domain 24 dispute.; Allows genuine per-record variation later without a further architectural decision. | This is the option's headline cost, not bookkeeping: **domain 09 defines no payout-basis field anywhere.** Verified — grep across 09-rights-ownership for ask/paid/paid-net/NYP returns zero, and the only 'basis' hits are 09.02.04's *dilution* basis ('The proposer chooses the basis') and 09.01.02's row key '(party, pool, role, contribution-basis, share, consent-state, entered-by)'. Neither is a payout basis. A therefore requires real authoring edits to 09.02.01, 09.02.04 and the 09.02 index — a cross-domain scope expansion, not a domain 20 change.; As currently written it is a P-04 violation. 20.04.04's cross-cut note says 'the **basis** field (ask/paid/paid-net) is authored here as a domain 09 property' — a domain 20 file originating a spec another domain owns, which is exactly what P-04 forbids ('author where owned, reference where not; never a second copy of another domain's requirement'). Choosing A/C means moving that authorship into domain 09 properly, not leaving the assertion where it is.; It spends interactions on the platform's most budget-constrained screen. 09.02.01's design budget is absolute — '≤ 8 interactions and ≤ 90 s' for a 4-contributor sheet, and 'if it does not fit, it is not capture, it is paperwork, and paperwork is what this feature exists to defeat.' Adding a negotiated money term to the split sheet is measured against that budget and is not obviously affordable.; The no-rights-record path (20.04.01 D-06 / 20.04.04 DT-02: list with a recorded 'taking 100%' acknowledgement) has no split agreement to hold the field, so the basis for that path must be specified separately.; Overturns 20.04.03 D-07's location clause ('captured and locked at listing', 'immutable for the life of that listing') — those lines are deleted. | no |
| **B — Per storefront listing (a field on the catalogue product, set by the lister)** | The basis is a store field on the product, chosen when the product is created, applied to every payee on every sale of that listing. Two products selling the same recording may carry different bases. Payees learn of it at listing time via the existing notification path rather than consenting at capture. | Keeps the basis next to the price rule it modifies, on the screen where the lister is actually working — NYP floor, suggested price and basis set together (20.04.01's price-rule table already owns `name-your-price` with `floor` and `suggested`).; Requires no change to domain 09; the whole seam stays inside domain 20.; Preserves 20.04.03 D-07 verbatim, including 'immutable for the life of that listing'.; Permits per-listing variation (a charity listing on 'ask', a normal listing on paid-net) without touching a split agreement. | It fails the locked disclosure axis on chronology, and this is decisive. 20.04.04's happy path captures the split in 2023 (step 1) and lists the product in 2024 (step 2). A value created at listing time cannot have been 'shown to every payee before they agree the split' a year earlier. This is the one argument against B that does not depend on how you classify the field.; The only thing that could rescue it — a per-listing payee consent flow — is defined in no file. What exists is *notification*, not consent: 20.04-cx.md CX-02 synthesis Q4 reads in full 'Collaborators should be told when a work they are on goes on sale. They should not learn from the fan.' Choosing B therefore commissions machinery no source defines.; One recording with two bases means the same 10% pays different amounts depending on which product the fan happened to buy, and the payee agreed to neither — the 'basis discovered on a statement is a dispute (24)' failure both D-07s exist to prevent.; Falling back to notification-only disclosure weakens 20.04.04 D-05 ('Payees see the split, the basis, and the session that produced their money') from consent to announcement.; 'Immutable for the life of that listing' is a second, incompatible lock model bolted onto an object whose other payout fields already bind at settlement (20.04.01 D-02 'the plan **version at sale time** governs each sale'; 20.04.03 edge case 'The payout plan **version** is bound at **payment settlement**'). | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: D — Platform-wide constant — B is disqualified rather than merely weaker: the locked disclosure axis requires the basis be shown before the split is agreed, and 20.04.04's own happy path agrees the split a year before the listing exists, so a listing-time field cannot satisfy it — and the per-listing consent flow that would rescue it exists in no file (CX-02 synthesis Q4 provides notification, not consent). That leaves D versus A/C, and the deciding fact is that domain 09 has no payout-basis field and its capture screen has an absolute budget against acquiring one: 09.02.01 states 'it must be faster than not doing it', fixes '≤ 8 interactions and ≤ 90 s' for a 4-contributor sheet, and rules that anything that does not fit 'is not capture, it is paperwork.' A negotiated ask/paid/net term is a poor candidate for that budget, and adding it means real authoring edits to 09.02.01, 09.02.04 and the 09.02 index — cross-domain scope the owner should agree to deliberately, not inherit from a domain 20 cross-cut note. D avoids all of it, satisfies the disclosure lock by construction, and dissolves the no-rights-record case (20.04.01 D-06 / 20.04.04 DT-02) that A/C must specify separately. It also matches how both Q-01s are phrased — one owner ruling on one default, 'confirm or reset', settled 'before any collaborator agrees a split'. Choose A/C instead if you want per-record variation or a per-payee consent artefact naming the basis; that is a real benefit, and it is what you are buying with the domain 09 edits. Be clear about the cost either way: D overturns the location clause of BOTH D-07s, A/C overturns 20.04.03 D-07's, B overturns 20.04.04 D-07's. No option leaves both intact — they contradict each other, so one of them is going down whatever you choose. *(precedent: The platform cut (20.04 index Q-01, 'Does the platform take a cut of D2F sales, and how much?') is already a single platform-wide money constant the owner sets once and the platform discloses rather than negotiates — 20.04.03 D-02, 'Show the line from fan to artist, including our cut'. The basis sits inside that same arithmetic (paid-net = paid − platform cut − processor fees) and on the same disclosure surfaces, so treating it the same way is consistent rather than novel. P-04 (author where owned, reference where not) is also load-bearing here, but it points AGAINST the entry's earlier use of it: 20.04.04 saying 'the **basis** field (ask/paid/paid-net) is authored here as a domain 09 property' is a domain 20 file originating another domain's spec, which is the violation P-04 names. P-04 does not favour placing the field in domain 09 — it says that if you place it there, domain 09 must author it and domain 20 must only reference it.)*

#### DQ-19.2 — AXIS 2 (open) — Lock trigger: when the basis stops being changeable  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Once a basis applies to a work, what event freezes it — the first settled sale, nothing special (it simply rides the plan version bound at settlement, like every other split field), the life of the listing, or a platform terms-change policy?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **L1 — Locked at first sale (pairs with A)** | The basis is mutable only through the domain 09 amendment + re-consent path (09.02.04) until the first sale settles against that agreement, after which it is frozen. This is 20.04.04 D-07's ratified wording preserved exactly: 'fixed at capture, immutable after first sale'. | Preserves a ratified decision unchanged.; Blocks a lister lobbying collaborators to re-consent a more favourable basis once the over-payment data is visible — a guard that plain version-binding does not provide.; Gives the fan-facing product page a stable line for any product with sales history. | It is the only split field with a private immutability trigger; every other field binds at settlement per 20.04.01 D-02, so the object carries two lock models.; The window between listing publish and first sale stays mutable, which makes 20.04.03's 'immutable for the life of that listing' simply wrong and requires the product page to re-render if a pre-first-sale amendment lands. | no |
| **L2 — No bespoke lock; the basis rides the plan version bound at settlement (pairs with C)** | The basis is one more field of the split, changeable only via 09.02.04 amendment + re-consent, with each sale applying the version live at payment authorization/settlement — exactly as 20.04.01 D-02 and 20.04.03's settlement-binding edge case already specify for every other split field. 20.04.04 D-07's 'immutable after first sale' is retired. | One lock model for the whole plan instead of two.; Reuses only machinery that already exists and is ratified: 09.02.04 amendment/re-consent, plus plan-version-at-settlement binding with the version named on every statement (20.04.04: 'Statement names the plan version applied to each sale').; No payee can be surprised — any change needs their re-consent, and their statement names the governing version. | Overturns a ratified decision (20.04.04 D-07's 'immutable after first sale') purely for uniformity.; Two fans paying £20 on different days for the same live product can have different bases applied, so the fan-facing basis line becomes time-varying — friction against 20.04.03 D-02's 'show the line from fan to artist'.; Removes the guard against renegotiating the basis once the over-payment data is in. | no |
| **L3 — Immutable for the life of the listing (only coherent with B)** | 20.04.03 D-07's wording taken literally: the basis is frozen for that listing from publish to delisting, and a re-listing may carry a different one. | Preserves 20.04.03 D-07 verbatim.; Trivially stable for the fan: the line on the product page never changes while the product is live. | Inherits every disqualifying problem of Axis 1 option B — it presupposes a listing-owned field.; Puts a listing-life lock on one payout field while 20.04.01 D-02 binds all the others at sale time: two lock models on one object.; A delist-and-relist becomes a legitimate way to change what a payee is paid without their consent. | no |
| **L4 — Platform terms-change policy (pairs with D)** | There is no per-record lock because there is no per-record field. The constant is fixed at launch; if the platform ever changes it, the change is versioned as a platform term, applies only to splits agreed after the change date, and existing agreements continue to be paid on the basis that was displayed when their parties consented. Reuses the existing statement obligation (20.04.04 D-05, 'Payees see the split, the basis, and the session') by naming which platform-terms version governed each sale. | Preserves the locked disclosure axis across a change: no payee is ever paid on a rule they were not shown.; Keeps a single lock model — the same 'the version live when it was agreed governs' shape that 20.04.01 D-02 already uses for the plan.; Costs one versioned platform-terms record, not a per-record field, a consent flow or a bespoke trigger. | It is new specification work — small, but real. This is why option D is the cheapest option and not a free one.; Requires the payout engine to resolve a terms version per agreement date, which is a second version dimension alongside the plan version.; Both D-07s' explicit lock clauses ('locked at listing', 'immutable after first sale') are replaced rather than preserved. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: L4 — Platform terms-change policy — Conditional on Axis 1: L4 is the coherent partner of D, L1 of A, L2 of C, L3 of B — so answer Axis 1 first and this axis mostly follows. Recommending L4 because it is the only option that keeps the locked disclosure axis intact under change while adding just one versioned record, and because it avoids the structural complaint that sinks L1: a lock trigger private to one field, on an object where 20.04.01 D-02 already binds every other field at sale time. If you pick A on Axis 1, take L1 rather than L2 — L2's gain is uniformity, and it pays for that by retiring a ratified decision and by making the fan-facing basis line time-varying under a live listing, which 20.04.03 D-02's 'show the line' principle argues against. *(precedent: 20.04.01 D-02 — 'the plan **version at sale time** governs each sale' — and 20.04.03's edge case 'The payout plan **version** is bound at **payment settlement**, not at add-to-cart, and the entitlement records that version.' L4 applies the same versioned-governance shape to a platform term instead of a plan. No precedent supports a lock trigger private to a single field, which is what L1 and L3 each are.)*

#### DQ-19.3 — AXIS 3 (open) — The value itself: ask, paid, or paid-net-of-fees  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Which amount do the split percentages multiply — the asking price, the amount the fan actually paid, or the amount paid net of the platform cut and processor fees?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **paid-net — the amount paid, net of platform cut and processor fees** | £15 paid, £1 fees, £1.40 platform cut → basis £12.60; a 10% drummer receives £1.26. This is 20.04.04's specified default and its happy-path arithmetic. | The drummer shares in the fan's generosity, which is what makes the fan-facing promise true: 20.04.04 DT-04's line 'Your £20 was shared with the 4 people who made this record'.; The artist does not absorb the platform cut and processor fees alone on behalf of everyone else.; Already the specified default (20.04.04 D-07) and already worked through in that file's happy path and statement line, so choosing it needs no new arithmetic anywhere. | The basis moves with fee structures, so a payee's effective take changes if the platform cut or processor rates change — which couples this to 20.04 index Q-01 (the platform cut) and to the payments-provider choice that `meta/constraints.md` still leaves pending. | no |
| **paid — the gross amount the fan entered** | £15 paid → basis £15; the 10% drummer receives £1.50 and the artist absorbs the platform cut and fees from their own share. | Simplest possible statement line and the easiest to explain to a payee.; Insulates payees from fee and platform-cut changes entirely. | The lister carries 100% of the platform cut and processor fees on behalf of every other payee, which grows worse as the number of payees grows.; On a low-margin sale the percentages can exceed what the platform actually collected. | no |
| **ask — the asking price / NYP floor, with the over-payment treated as a tip to the lister** | £5 floor, £15 paid → basis £5; the 10% drummer receives 50p and the £10 over-payment goes entirely to the lister. | It is the reading 20.04-cx.md CX-03 gives most sympathetically: 'The 25p reading says the £12 over-payment was a tip to the person the fan loves', and 'Nobody agreed a percentage of a tip'.; Structurally consistent with 20.05.03's lean on tips (D-03, 'Tips attach to the person; splits do not apply' — explicitly an unratified reading needing an owner ruling). | Directly contradicts the fan-facing promise the storefront intends to make: the fan who paid £20 believing it reached 'the people who made this record' would have sent £10 of it to one person (20.04.04 DT-04).; It makes the NYP over-payment — which 20.04.03 calls 'the point' and the mechanic behind Bandcamp's payouts — invisible to every payee except the lister.; It gives the lister an incentive to set a £0 or minimal floor, which collides with the co-owner-consent gate 20.04.03 already had to build for exactly that hazard. | no |

**Recommendation**: paid-net — It is already the specified default (20.04.04 D-07) with its arithmetic and statement line worked through, and it is the only value consistent with the fan-facing claim the storefront makes. Flagged here to keep it from being silently overwritten: this axis is NOT decided by Axes 1 or 2, and it is already carried as Q-01 in BOTH 20.04.03 and 20.04.04, owner-assigned, deferred to `/ideate-validate` — so if it has already been ruled on in that queue, leave it alone and answer only Axes 1 and 2. Note the interaction: under Axis 1 = D the answer is a launch-blocking platform setting; under A/C it is the default offered at capture.

**Affected files**: `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04.03-digital-sales-name-your-price-bundles.md`, `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04.04-d2f-revenue-split-payout.md`, `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront-cx.md`, `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront-index.md`, `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.04-direct-to-fan-storefront/20.04.01-storefront-product-catalog.md`, `20-fanbase-direct-to-fan/20.05-memberships-patronage-campaigns/20.05.03-tipping-micro-patronage.md`, `09-rights-ownership/09.02-split-capture-agreements/09.02.01-moment-of-creation-split-capture.md`, `09-rights-ownership/09.02-split-capture-agreements/09.02.04-split-amendment-reconsent.md`, `09-rights-ownership/09.02-split-capture-agreements/09.02-split-capture-agreements-index.md`


### DQ-20

> **File**: `21-promotion-marketing/promotion-marketing-cx.md` · **Domain**: `21` · **2 open / 1 locked**

**Decide what earns a coverage item its "verified" badge — the artefact itself (a fixed, live, retrievable article) or the fact that WeJammin sent the pitch — and, separately, whether the pitched-vs-organic fact is shown to the EPK reader at all.**

**Why it blocks**: An implementer cannot define the coverage record's verification-strength field, the rule that computes it, or the EPK badge render, because the two owning documents assign the badge from different inputs. 21.07's States table triggers Verified on "Live URL, retrievable, fixed source confirmed (article)" with no pitch condition; promotion-marketing-cx.md CX-05 defines verified as "WeJammin sent the pitch and holds the link" and CX-08 synthesis Q3 says "organic coverage lacks the pitch and is verifiable only to `claimed`/`verified-at-timestamp`". Under one reading an unsolicited broadsheet review renders verified; under the other it can never render verified. The same pair of documents also gives `verified-at-timestamp` two incompatible definitions. Until both are settled, 21.07 Q-03's `[PENDING — /write-be-spec]` evidence taxonomy cannot be written, 21.09 cannot specify its badge surface, and the pinned-snapshot-vs-live-render rule in CX-05 synthesis Q5 has no defined value to render.

#### Axes already locked (do not re-decide)

| Axis | Locked by |
|---|---|
| Axis 2 — What `verified-at-timestamp` means: the mutable-source artefact class, or the post-rot decay state | `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/21-promotion-marketing/21.07-coverage-clipping-log.md` — D-05: "Coverage carries **three** verification strengths, not two — verified (fixed article), verified-at-timestamp (mutable playlist add), claimed (typed / self-attested)"; DT-03: "**verified-at-timestamp** (playlist add, live-but-mutable, badge names the confirmation date and evidence)"; and the States table, which carries BOTH "Verified-at-timestamp \| Live but mutable source confirmed (playlist add)" AND a separate "Decayed \| Source link rotted \| Verification lapses; the artist is told before the EPK quietly weakens". Corroborated by the contradicting file's own ownership concession, promotion-marketing-cx.md CX-05 synthesis Q1: "the quote's verification strength is owned by 21.07 ... not an independent copy of the verdict." |

#### DQ-20.1 — Axis 1 — What input determines the verification-strength badge: artefact class, or pitch provenance  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Is a coverage item's verification strength determined by what the source artefact is and whether it can be retrieved now, or by whether WeJammin sent the pitch that produced it?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A — Artefact class determines strength; pitch provenance never affects it** | Strength is read from the item's `type` field (already in 21.07's Behavior list: review / feature / interview / premiere / playlist add / radio add) plus a retrievability check. A fixed, live, retrievable article renders `verified` whether or not a pitch produced it; a live-but-mutable playlist add renders `verified-at-timestamp`; anything with no retrievable source (typed quote, self-attested radio add) renders `claimed`; a previously-verified source that rots moves to the separate `Decayed` state. Pitch parentage stays where 21.07 already records it — the Attributed / Organic states, which drive the CRM flip (CX-04) and the directory denominator — and never touches the badge. Choosing A requires two edits that are genuine additions, not restatements: (i) CX-05's Relationship paragraph and CX-08 synthesis Q3 are repaired to match 21.07, and (ii) a NEW decision in 21.07 (D-06) declaring that verification strength and pitch provenance are two orthogonal axes, plus a split of the current single undifferentiated States table into a strength column and a provenance column. 21.07 nowhere states that orthogonality today — it lists Verified / Verified-at-timestamp / Claimed / Decayed / Attributed / Organic in one flat list — which is why this is an open decision and not merely a typo repair. | It is the only reading the owning feature's own trigger text supports. 21.07 States: "Verified \| Live URL, retrievable, fixed source confirmed (article) \| Marked verified; usable as a verified EPK quote" — the trigger names an artefact property and contains no pitch condition. D-05 and DT-03 both enumerate the three strengths by artefact class only: "verified (fixed article), verified-at-timestamp (mutable playlist add), claimed (typed / self-attested)".; The contradicting document concedes ownership to 21.07 three paragraphs below the contradicting sentence. CX-05 synthesis Q1: "the quote's verification strength is owned by 21.07; the EPK holds a *reference* plus a pinned snapshot, not an independent copy of the verdict." CX-05 synthesis Q3 then frames the constraint as a verified-vs-typed contrast — "only verified (not typed) quotes can render with a verified badge ... a permission the log enforces, not the EPK" — with no pitched-vs-organic element. The provenance gloss is isolated to one parenthetical in the same section that disclaims authority over it.; It keeps 21.07 D-03 and the Organic state intact: "Coverage with no pitch parent is fully valid" / "Organic \| No pitch parent \| Fully valid — often the best coverage there is". Under A the domain's own stated best case can carry the strongest badge.; It matches D-04, "We verify the link, not the reading" — the verification act 21.07 commits to is a link/retrievability act, which is performable on any URL regardless of how the artist found it.; It adds no check the spec does not already require: 21.07 already carries a `Decayed \| Source link rotted` state and the link-rot edge case, both of which presuppose that the platform re-fetches coverage URLs; and the artefact type is already a captured field. | The document it overrules is the later and deeper one. promotion-marketing-cx.md is `[DEEP]`, last updated 2026-07-18; 21.07 is `[PARTIAL]`, last updated 2026-07-17 (and still listed `[SURFACE]` in the domain index children table). Choosing A means an affirmative, more recently deepened statement loses to an earlier, shallower one on ownership grounds. That is a defensible cut, but the owner should make it knowingly.; It weakens what the badge proves about WeJammin specifically. Index D-02 rests the domain's existence on "WeJammin can verify the quotes (it sent the pitch, it holds the link)"; under A the badge means only that a URL resolves to a fixed page, which any party could check. The pitch-holding advantage survives as a capture/CRM advantage, not as badge semantics.; A self-logged organic item is chosen entirely by the artist — they pick the URL and pull the quote — so the platform observed less of the chain than for a pitched item, and under A that difference is invisible on the badge (it remains visible only as the Organic state, and only if Axis 3 renders it).; It edits ratified `[DEEP]` CX prose in two places — a correction of existing text, not an additive change. | no |
| **B — Pitch provenance determines strength; organic coverage is capped below verified** | `verified` is reserved for coverage WeJammin can trace end to end: it sent the pitch, holds the link, and the published piece resolves against it. Organic coverage is accepted into the log and fully valid as a record, but its badge is capped at `verified-at-timestamp` or `claimed`. This requires amending 21.07 D-05, DT-03 and the Verified row of the States table to add a pitch-provenance condition, and redefining `verified-at-timestamp` to carry two meanings (mutable source, and unattributed-but-live source). | It is the only reading under which the badge asserts something no competitor can reproduce, which is the literal content of index D-02 and of 21.07 DT-01: "The platform is present when coverage happens (it sent the pitch, it holds the link), so it can capture the fact at source rather than accept a claim later."; It is honest about a real epistemic gap: for an organic item the platform never observed the pitch-to-publication chain and is trusting the artist's choice of URL and pull-quote.; It needs no new data: the optional pitch parent is already a field on the coverage item (21.07 Behavior: "its link to a pitch (21.03.03)"), so the badge reads something the model already holds. | It contradicts 21.07 D-03 and the Organic state outright — "Coverage with no pitch parent is fully valid" / "often the best coverage there is" — by making the domain's own stated best case structurally incapable of its strongest badge.; It collapses evidence classes that DT-03 exists to keep apart. Because B redefines `verified-at-timestamp` to cover unattributable-but-live sources, a pitched mutable playlist add and an unsolicited fixed live article land on the SAME badge. DT-03 confirms "coverage is **not one class**" precisely to stop "the weakest-evidenced artefact borrow[ing] the credibility the article earns"; B reintroduces that by flattening. (Note: it does not invert the order — an earlier draft of this entry and the audit's reading of CX-05's literal text claimed inversion. Inversion is true of CX-05 as written, whose `verified` clause carries no artefact qualifier at all, but it is not true of Option B as drafted here.); It sits crosswise to D-04, "We verify the link, not the reading". If the verification act is a link act, provenance is not part of what is verified, so the badge would assert something the stated act does not test.; It forces the largest spec churn of the two: reopening 21.07 Q-03 (currently "✅ RESOLVED (D-05, DT-03)"), rewriting D-05, DT-03 and two States rows, and deciding whether the distinct `Decayed` state survives or collapses into the widened `verified-at-timestamp`.; It systematically disadvantages the artists with the strongest real press, since unsolicited coverage is by definition unpitched. This is a consequence of the rule, not a measured claim about the user base. | no |

**Recommendation**: A — Artefact class determines strength; pitch provenance never affects it — 21.07 is the owner of the verification taxonomy, and the contradicting file says so itself: CX-05 synthesis Q1 states the strength "is owned by 21.07 ... not an independent copy of the verdict", which makes the provenance gloss in the same section's Relationship paragraph a non-owning duplicate rather than a competing ruling. On the text, 21.07's Verified trigger contains no pitch condition, D-03 declares parentless coverage fully valid, and D-04 commits verification to a link act. Option B would require the feature's own stated best case to be incapable of its best badge, would flatten a fixed article and a mutable playlist add onto one badge against DT-03, and would reopen a decision already recorded. Two cautions the owner should weigh: the CX is the later, deeper document, so this is an ownership cut and not a recency cut; and A is not a pure repair — it requires a new 21.07 D-06 asserting that strength and provenance are orthogonal, because 21.07 currently lists both sets of states in one undifferentiated table and nowhere says they are independent. That D-06 is load-bearing: without it a downstream reader can re-derive the provenance reading from the same flat table. *(precedent: P-04, author-where-owned / reference-where-not (ratified 2026-07-21, from 07.08.01 D-06, generalising 07.07.03 D-04: "two copies of third-party requirements would drift, and one would be wrong"). It points at 21.07 here because 21.07 is the feature that produces the verification verdict and CX-05 synthesis Q1 explicitly concedes that ownership; the parent CX should reference the taxonomy, not carry a second differing copy. Deliberately NOT cited: the stale-parent-CX pattern from Domain 07. That pattern covers a parent holding an unanswered `[PENDING]` marker against a `[DEEP]` child; here the parent is itself `[DEEP]`, is the later document, and makes an affirmative contradicting claim against a `[PARTIAL]` child. The shape does not match and it cuts the other way on recency and depth.)*

#### DQ-20.2 — Axis 3 — Whether the pitched-vs-organic fact is rendered to the EPK reader alongside the strength badge  `[ ] UNRATIFIED`

**Does the Attributed / Organic provenance fact stay an internal log state, or does it also render publicly on the EPK as a second qualifier next to the strength badge?**

| Option | Means | Pros | Cons | New machinery? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **3-A — Provenance stays a log state; only the strength badge renders publicly** | Attributed / Organic remains what 21.07 already has: a state on the coverage record, visible to the Musician, driving the CRM flip (CX-04) and the directory denominator. The EPK renders one badge per item — verified / verified-at-timestamp / claimed — and nothing about how the coverage was sourced. | It changes nothing in 21.09, whose badge surface is `[PARTIAL]` and whose pin/retention mechanics are `[PENDING — /write-be-spec]` — the least-specified surface in the pair.; It keeps the EPK item to a single label, which matches 21.07's stated reason for having badges at all: "Nobody checks, because checking is tedious and the person receiving the EPK has thirty of them."; The provenance fact is not lost — it is already recorded as the Attributed / Organic state and remains available to the artist and to the CRM. | The journalist reading the EPK cannot tell a platform-observed capture from an artist-self-logged one, which is exactly the distinction index D-02 says the platform is uniquely able to make.; If the owner later wants to render provenance, it is a new spec change on 21.09 rather than something designed in now. | no |
| **3-B — Render a second provenance qualifier on the EPK item** | Each EPK coverage item shows two independent labels: the 21.07 strength badge, plus a provenance qualifier — e.g. `verified · platform-captured` for an item WeJammin pitched and holds the link for, `verified · self-logged` for an organic item the artist added. Neither label demotes the other. This is the substance of what the previous version of this entry called Option C; splitting the axes is what lets it be chosen alongside Axis 1 Option A instead of instead of it. | No information is discarded: both the artefact fact 21.07 owns and the provenance fact index D-02 leans on are on the artefact where the reader is.; It preserves D-02's defensibility claim without paying Option B's cost, because `platform-captured` is a label only WeJammin can apply.; It fits D-07's honest-attribution posture, "Honest attribution over impressive attribution" — surface the difference rather than collapse it. | It commissions machinery no source defines. Nothing in 21.07, 21.09 or the CX file specifies a provenance qualifier, its vocabulary, or how the EPK lays out a compound label; choosing 3-B commissions a new 21.09 render spec and a new 21.07 decision binding the qualifier to the existing Attributed / Organic states.; It lands that new surface on 21.09, which is `[PARTIAL]` with `[PENDING — /write-be-spec]` pin/retention mechanics and an unresolved leak-control question (Q-02).; A two-part label works against the reason 21.07 gives for badges: a reader with thirty EPKs who must decode a compound label is a reader who stops decoding.; It is a presentation escalation of a fact already recorded, not new knowledge. | **yes** |

**Recommendation**: 3-A — Provenance stays a log state; only the strength badge renders publicly — 3-B is the only option in this entry that requires spec no source has written, and it requires it on the least-specified surface in the pair. The fact it would render is already captured (Attributed / Organic), so nothing is lost by deferring; if the owner wants it later it is additive to 3-A, whereas unwinding a compound badge is not. Keeping this as its own axis matters regardless of which option wins: it means the owner can choose Axis 1 Option A without that answer silently deciding what the EPK shows.

**Affected files**: `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/21-promotion-marketing/promotion-marketing-cx.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/21-promotion-marketing/21.07-coverage-clipping-log.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/21-promotion-marketing/21.09-campaign-press-kit-epk.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/ideation/21-promotion-marketing/promotion-marketing-index.md`, `.memory/wiki/specs/audits/ideation-ambiguity-report.md`
